## THE ARRIVAL OF THE SEVEN 

_A dense fog cloaks the sea, as if the world has not yet come into being. The emptiness extends indefinitely towards the east from whence the humans once sailed to Ravenland, guided by the Raven God with a snake in its talons. Twelve hundred years later no one remembers what is out there, but now a rhythm forms in the haze. The silhouettes of seven tall fig­ ures in black emerge from the void, rowing a sloop through the fog, their oars rising and falling as one at a steady pace. They show no sign of urgency, nor fatigue, despite the length of their voyage. Each stroke is performed with purpose._ 

he oarsman on the second bench on the port side has an injured elbow that clicks Tevery time he pulls the oar to his chest. A human would find the noise intolerable after only a few hours, but for the oarsmen it sets the pace, like the drops of a water metronome. They row in silence. No one has spoken for weeks, as there is no need – they all know where they are going and what they must do. 

The rocky coast of Ravenland looms in the night. The oarsmen pay it no heed and maintain their pace until their sloop crashes into the rocks. Everyone drops their oars in unison, not even bothering to pull them onto the deck, before sliding into the waist- high waves and 

wading ashore – still without a word. They do not tie up the boat that carried them, but let it drift at the mercy of the sea, as they walk inland towards a stone circle erected on a coastline hill by an extinct noble house in memory of something long forgotten. Their bodies are shrouded in black robes. Their faces are hidden, some by bone plates tied together with leather straps, another behind a wooden mask, and a third by woven osiers. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

Sigmyn Talleman blows into his hands to keep them warm while he waits by the stone circle, 

4 

**chapter 1** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

scowling into the wind, taking regular swigs of liquor from a hip flask and swearing just loud enough for his employer to hear him. There is nothing to do but wait. The halfling seeks sympathy from Gerdimer Black, perched on one of the stones with some dead grass under his buttocks. The goblin chatters his teeth and studies the stars in search of bad omens, perhaps praying to the moon goddess. The two of them are lovers, and for their mutual affection they have been banished from their villages, yet they have never looked back with sadness, regret, or even anger. The third man present – their employer – claims to be a halfling as well, but covers his face for religious reasons which makes his origins difficult to place. He speaks in a peculiar manner, but it matters little to Sigmyn, since the man paid the two merchants handsomely just to meet some foreigners on the shore at night. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

The oarsmen march silently up the hill, wet robes flapping around their legs. They stop before the little ones, towering over them, at which point the employer walks up to the newcomers, bows, and quietly steps aside. Sigmyn Talleman does not scare easily, but the mute strangers make him uncomfortable. He does not have the good- natured disposition typical of his kin. Life has left him hardened, inside and out, with no patience for nonsense and foolery. Gerdimer Black grows more watchful as well and jumps down from the stone, ready for whatever may come. 

“I’m told that you have come to Ravenland in search of a missing loved one,” begins 

Sigmyn, who since the early hours of the night has had enough of silence. “My friend and I have long traveled this land, its villages and strongholds, in daytime and at night. We can help, but we will need to know more than your associate has disclosed. Are you from Alderland?” 

The tallest of the strangers puts his hands to his throat under the wooden mask, as if to make sure that he can still speak after the voyage. When he does, the voice is deep and strangely gurgling. Mussels may have nested in his throat while at sea. 

“We are the Seven from beyond the seas. Where is our stolen eighth mother?” 

“The seven what?” sneers Sigmyn, annoyed that there are in fact eight of them, not seven, if one includes the halfman who hired them. “The seven sons of the seventh son, perhaps, as the tale goes?” 

“Yes,” says the man with the wooden mask. “The seven of the seventh mother.” 

While Sigmyn Talleman tries to come up with a sufficiently acid- tongued insult, one of the strangers opens a casket. The box appears to be all they brought with them on their voyage. The gold inside gleams brightly at the halfling and clogs up his mind with thoughts of riches. Meanwhile, one of the oarsmen points at the goblin standing a bit farther away. The motion makes his elbow click 

“It is not one, but two.” 

The strangers all turn to Gerdimer Black. “He is my partner and tracker, and it is me you should be talking to,” says Sigmyn irritably. “So someone has taken your mother? Slavers I presume? Those dregs are multiplying like lice in straw beds ever since the Blood Mist lifted. No problem though, since you carry so much 

5 

**the arrival of the seven** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

gold for her ransom. Leave it to us – though we need to know more in order to buy her freedom. What does she look like, little mommy? Like her sons perhaps?” he grins. 

The oarsman with the wooden mask shifts his eyes back to the halfling. 

“Where is the eighth mother, she who was taken?” 

Talleman glares back and spits at the monolith beside him. Has the stranger blinked even once behind that mask? Who are these lunatics? Human sectarians, if his merchant instincts still serve him. You never know with those people. Had it not been for all that gold, the halfling would already have gestured for Gerdimer to vanish into the night. 

“The parts smell like one,” says one of the strangers. Then the short- statured employer steps forward and bows before he speaks. 

“None of the parts know anything about the mother,” the man explains to the masked newcomers. 

“It has masked its own thoughts,” replies the man in the wooden mask. “The moonstone in its chest will know. Let us question the stone.” 

“Moonstones? Do you also believe the fairytales about Eor casting us into the world in jealousy of the elves and getting us split into two? In that case you would be better off seeing a village priest who shares your views. Those babbling loons are getting together to seek the mythical moonstones in the Ashen Vale. Only death awaits them there, nothing more.” 

“Reach into the moonstone in its chest!” says the man in the wooden mask and the others move closer. 

Gold or no gold – enough is enough. But with his attention fixed on the tall folk, 

Talleman does not notice the smaller stranger until he is startled by a stab in his side. 

“Run Gerdimer! Into the dark!” 

Like frightened rabbits the two merchants run off into the night without any masked strangers in pursuit. 

Gerdimer Black leads the way with his night vision and plows through the autumn cobwebs along the ground, opting for speed over stealth. Only when they reach the forest will they go silent and disappear. So angry and upset is the goblin that at first he does not even notice that Sigmyn has fallen behind, which makes him curse and head back. Carefully retracing his steps, Gerdimer finds several of the strangers crouched over his fallen partner, incapacitated by the poisonous stab that almost instantly paralyzed his muscles. One of the oarsmen is kneeling by the halfling’s side. He has put his gloves and bone mask aside, but the hood droops over his face. The stranger sticks two fingers up Talleman’s nose, and as he pulls back his hand, the hairs on Gerdimer’s back bristle – the fingers have come loose from the body. The goblin cannot help whimpering when he sees them slide of their own accord into his friend’s nasal cavity. As the stranger turns towards him, the goblin screams at the top of his lungs and flees across the moor as fast as his legs can carry him, for the stranger’s face belongs to neither human nor halfling, nor any other creature in the creation, but consists of a crawling mass of muscles slithering amongst one another. 

The figures in black watch as blood starts pouring out of one, then two, and then all the halfling’s orifices during the probe of his insides. Afterwards the fingers do not leave through an existing exit, but simply burst through the 

6 **chapter 1** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

skin to be reunited with the body from whence they came. The remains of Sigmyn Talleman are still shaking, but it is unclear whether he is conscious or even alive. The strangers do not care either way. 

“There is no moonstone.” 

They all look around for the goblin who disappeared into the night. Maybe they can still sense him. 

“Did we eat any thoughts about the eighth mother?” 

“There was no such thought, but it spoke true about clerics seeking moonstones in the Ashen Vale. It is my belief that the thieves dissolved into two to push away any memory of their theft.” 

“Let us track them down and remind them of their treachery!” 

Without another word the oarsmen start to make their way inland, leaving the halfling’s corpse twitching in the stone circle.

**the arrival of the seven** 

## INTRODUCTION 

_The prophet Fangler’s third eye opened in his forehead. “Hear the god’s call! Wyrm shall be reborn in the Bloodmarch, but the pendulum of time has been stolen!” shouted Fangler in a shrill voice. “Retrieve the pendulum for the sake of our god!” Ginmyn put down his beer and glanced at the Ad Vipera officer sitting beside him. “Should we take him now?” “No.”_ 

_Ginmyn made the sign of Lemniscate across his chest. “Surely you don’t believe that blasphemy about the god’s rebirth in the Bloodmarch?”_ 

_“Lilies often grow out of dung. The prophet’s message is of interest to the shedder Poansa, Do you question her?” The officer bared a sharpened corner tooth._ 

_Ginmyn put the tankard to his mouth to drown any further questions._ 

he Bloodmarch is Ravenland’s neighbor to the west, known for its barren plains Tand proud riders who worship the holy volcano known as Horn. In Ravenland the country is often referred to as the Ashen Vale, which is really just the eastern region. The only known passageway between the countries is through Shadowgate Pass in the Thynde Range. 

During the wars between King Algarod of Alderland and his renegade sorcerer Zygofer three hundred years ago, a horde of demons were summoned that slaughtered the Alderlander armies in Ravenland. After losing control of the demons, the sorcerer sent them to ravage the neighboring country of the Bloodmarch while he himself desperately tried to block the 

8 **chapter 2** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

portals he had opened between the worlds. In the legends this event is often referred to as the _Demon Flood_ . 

Zygofer managed to close the gates and many demons perished as the link to their own worlds was severed. Others remained – some in Ravenland, others in the Bloodmarch. Afraid that the demons he had lured to the Bloodmarch would one day return, the sorcerer made a deal with a demon prince to close Shadowgate Pass by perverting the very laws of nature in the area. The pass has been blocked ever since, and few therefore know what has been going on in the neighboring country for the last three hundred years. 

However, during the battles of Vond a few years ago (see _Raven’s Purge_ ) Shadowgate Pass opened once more. No one knows why – perhaps the demons guarding it died or found a way home to their own world. In any event, adventurers and settlers are now gathering on the border of the Bloodmarch, enthralled with curiosity after three centuries of separate existence. 

How much of the horse people’s country survived the ravages of the demon horde? Is there nothing left but desolate wasteland, or have its villages been rebuilt? Who lives there? 

Perhaps there is good soil to farm for those who can claim and defend a piece of land? Perhaps the country’s treasures lie scattered among the bones of the dead, there for the taking for whoever gets there first? 

The village of Taregyll was founded almost immediately on the slopes just beyond Shadowgate Pass in the east part of the Ashen Vale. Shacks were built and occupied. Adventurers, mercenaries, treasure hunters, missionaries, merchants, and refugees all come here from Ravenland. Taregyll is really nothing more than a lawless backwater with a few simple shacks built around a couple of inns. Aslenes from the east have also come to Taregyll. Some weep with joy that the pass has opened to the world, while others warn strangers not to come here, especially as a demonic plague haunts the entire region, where the woods are blood red and alive and awaiting their prey. Some Aslenes wish to barter with the newcomers or pay warriors to cleanse certain areas and take back the land they once lost. There are rumors that the horse clans are uniting again under a strong leader farther inland to drive the demons from the Bloodmarch once and for all. 

## **RAVEN’S PURGE**

This book is chronologically set after the campaign _Raven’s Purge_ which took place in Ravenland. Some of the events described here began there, but it is not necessary to have played _Raven’s Purge_ before the campaign _Legacy of Horn_ which is included in this book. If the gaming group intends to play _Raven’s Purge_ at some point, it would be best to do so before embarking on _The Bloodmarch_ .

## OVERVIEW 

_The Bloodmarch_ is a campaign module for the Forbidden Lands roleplaying game. Here you will find descriptions of the Bloodmarch region and its peoples, creatures, and terrain. Key players are introduced along with their disagreements and respective interests. 

adventurers go searching for a set of holy weapons. The campaign can be played in a variety of ways, each with a different focus. The adventurers can ally with or support either of the key players, oppose them, or simply ignore them. The campaign brings the adventurers to various parts of the Bloodmarch and can be interspersed with side quests if so desired. 

Some of this is presented in the accompanying campaign _Legacy of Horn_ where the

## THE JOURNEY TO THE BLOODMARCH 

_“Go west, young man, and grow with the land!”_ 

to decide the reason for their journey to the Bloodmarch. Here are some suggestions that can easily be combined: 

– From Ferrale Mutra’s speech to the banished Alderlanders, before they were lowered from the bulwark of the Iron Lock. 

The player characters will likely begin their adventure as Ravenlanders or Alderlanders who have come to Taregyll in the Ashen Vale through Shadowgate Pass. You may choose another starting point, but you and the players must then explain who their characters are and how they ended up there. You are also free 

**PLUNDERERS:** The player characters have heard that the country west of Ravenland has just been opened to outsiders. There will no doubt be treasures to collect if they get there early. If they do not wish to keep all the plunder themselves, they can always find less scrupulous merchants as well as a fence’s guild in Taregyll. These people will buy anything from equipment to jewelry and magical artifacts, and can even place orders on behalf of interested parties. 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

**SETTLERS:** After the tumultuous events of _Raven’s Purge_ , many people just want a quiet place to settle down, build a village, and farm the land. Perhaps they go looking for it in the Bloodmarch, where vast areas are said to be deserted and the soil is fertilized with the volcanic ashes of Horn. 

**RETURNING CLANSFOLK:** Quards and Galdanes, two horse clans originally from the Bloodmarch, fled to Ravenland before the Blood Mist made all travel impossible three centuries ago. Many of them dream of returning to their homeland now that it has been opened. The player characters could lead a scouting party or an entire community to the west. 

**ELVEN AGENTS:** The elves of Ravenland worry about their Aslene kinsfolk, the red elves, whom the elders of the Stillmist say are in the grip of a collective madness that makes them do the demons’ bidding and dwell in the scarlet, misgrown forests of the Bloodmarch. 

## **FROM THE BITTER REACH?**

The tunnels are supposedly connected to Belderand and offer underground passage to the Bloodmarch from the icy Winding Ways of the Bitter Reach as well as from the misty mountains of Ravenland. Whether these stories are true or not is highly unclear as most dwarves will not say a word on the matter.

## THE LAY OF THE LAND 

_“The plains of the Bloodmarch once blossomed so abundantly that hot days would see rivers of nectar flow through the vales. The mountains yielded ore so pure that shards of unforged iron could be used for axes. Of course, this was before the Demon Flood. Ever since the sorcerer Zygofer had his demonic hordes lay waste to the country ten generations before us, the land has been dead and deserted, apart from the twisted nightmares that now roam its plains and forests. Yet there should be much treasure to find for those brave enough to face their fears. Some say the demons no longer howl with hunger as they used to and that the clans are riding once more to drive the abominations out.”_ 

he Bloodmarch is located on the same parallel as Ravenland and its climate is Tsimilar, only hotter and drier since it is farther away from the sea. The central plains have volcanic soil and are rich with dark sand mixed with fertile ash. 

Before the Demon Flood there were many villages along the outskirts of these plains, often located close to one another to better defend against plundering horse clans. There 

are also areas resting on white limestone. These lie outside the horse people’s territory, since they believe that the volcano god Horn is not present there. 

The Bloodmarch is surrounded by mountains with only a few passes. Tall and strange rock formations are a common sight, especially in the east. All the rivers draining the mountains are said to converge in the south, where they are swallowed by the earth. 

13 

**the lay of the land** 

## NATURE IN THE BLOODMARCH 

## CRIMSON FORESTS 

The main feature that distinguishes the Bloodmarch’s natural environment from that of its neighboring countries is that the vegetation in certain areas has been tainted by demonic influences. Such places are commonly known as Crimson Forests. 

From a distance the forest looks as if a mountain of entrails has been poured over the landscape. 

Closer up the trees are blood red and malicious, with flowers that glow in strange colors and appear to be watching strangers by turning their heads towards them. Some say that the air in the Crimson Forests is laden with the flowers’ fumes, as imperceptible as they are poisonous. 

The vegetation is said to have a mind and will of its own. It harbors predatory creatures that are neither plant nor beast. People living near these places say that visitors are changed after spending time in a Crimson Forest – a malady they carry with them, if they manage to leave the area at all. Red elves are the only kin in the Bloodmarch that live in the Crimson Forests. 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## FIRELANDS 

Firelands are another geographical feature of the Bloodmarch. Here the magma flows so close to the surface that it burns bare feet. In some places the magma even sees daylight. Entire landscapes can be ablaze, while boiling water erupts into the sky. There is not much life in the Firelands, but since it is considered sacred by the priests of Horn a couple of temples have been built where the earth’s crust is thick enough. 

## ASHLANDS 

Ashlands are barren black deserts of volcanic ash. Traveling through the Ashlands is dangerous since there is nothing to eat or drink. During droughts the wind carries so much ash that it impairs one’s vision. Furthermore, the particles can be toxic to inhale. Rain turns the ash into black mud. A light rain is ideal for travel since it binds the dust while the ground remains firm. Ash merchants and mineral collectors are often encamped nearby, only scouring the Ashlands for commodities when the weather permits. Humans can sometimes disappear without a trace in the Ashlands, and some say the ashes are crawling with hidden predators. 

## FOOD AND WATER 

Travelers may have difficulty finding food and water in the hotter, more arid parts of the Bloodmarch and should replenish their supplies whenever possible. In the new terrain types, Ashlands and Firelands, food and water are scarce and what little water they contain is undrinkable (page 41). People who value their health and sanity should never drink or eat anything from the Crimson Forests, though alchemists and obscure thinkers see these raw materials as opportunities rather than threats. 

## KIN & GODS 

This section describes the kin inhabiting the Bloodmarch and the gods they worship. 

## THE HORSE CLANS 

Most humans in the Bloodmarch belong to one of the five horse clans that each have claimed one of the country’s central plains. Being nomads, the clans have no problem with friendly outsiders visiting their land and taking what they need to survive, but anything of economic value such as permanent settlements and trading caravans requires the approval of the respective chieftains and a tribute equivalent to one- tenth of the value. 

The five horse clans are named after the five dormant volcanos of the Bloodmarch, which are thought to be the sons of the volcano god Horn. These clans are called Houns, Sabirians, Caberians, Galdanes, and Quards. The clans believe that they are born from the volcano god Horn, who gave them their land and the astra to drive out the indigenous people called _Vasnians_ . A more historical analysis suggests that the horsefolk arrived more than a thousand years ago from the Drawn Lands in the west, expelled the Vasnians, and adopted the god Horn as their principal deity, likely influenced by the dwarves of Eldhem. The clans strongly disapprove of the latter description. 

**HOUNS:** Sella the Liberator who once liberated the Bloodmarch and organized the horsefolk into clans is thought to have belonged to the Houns, which makes the clan in question think of themselves as more special than the others. There are few of them left, as their clan was decimated during the Demon Flood and their territory is largely dominated by Crimson 

16 **chapter 3** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

Forest. They maintain a good relationship with the horned dwarves of Firestead. 

The young Houn chieftain is called Selligar Horne of Havenmark and is trying to unite the clans. 

**SABIRIANS:** The Sabirians were warned in advance of the Demon Flood and had time to prepare, though they believe that they are stronger, more virile, and more capable than the Houns and that they therefore dominate by right. The Sabirians have formed a loose alliance with the Caberians with many marriages between the clans. Sabirians think that they are the best riders and have the best horses, including the legendary winged steeds. 

The Sabirians’ chieftain is Mommodar the Gnawer, an aged and cunning warrior. Mommodar disapproves of Selligar Horne’s claim to leadership over the clans and considers himself a better alternative. 

Kanender the Mad managed to break through the enemy and escape through Shadowgate Pass into Ravenland, where many Galdanes remained. Now that their homeland has opened, many have returned to be reunited with the small number of survivors who are still scraping by back home. 

The leader of the Galdanes in Ravenland is Kormella Mira of Moldena, a descendant of Kanender the Mad. Her lineage gives her prestige among her own people, but the Aslene Galdanes regard Kanender as a traitor. However, Kormella is known as one of the finest horse warriors there is, hard as stone toward friends and enemies alike. She hates demons and followers of Rust, but seeks closer relations with the Quards to counter the alliance between the Sabirians and Caberians. 

Trandesso Haveman is the leader of the Aslene Galdanes. He is an unimaginative traditionalist who cares more about his own and his people’s pride than about preparing them for a changing future. 

**CABERIANS:** The Caberians have traditionally been at war with the Alderlanders, who every so often would attack through Gander’s Pass before it was blocked by the Demon Flood. The Caberians value traditional masculinity. Weapon smithing, combat training, and the skaldic arts are central to their way of life. Typical Caberians are easily offended and quick to challenge someone to a duel. 

The Caberian chieftain is Merdekai, a scarred and imposing horse warrior in his mid- forties. Merdekai supports Mommodar the Gnawer’s claim as leader of all the clans. 

**GALDANES:** The Galdanes were hit first by the Demon Flood as their territory shares a border with Ravenland. Totally unprepared, the clan was slaughtered in droves before the legendary 

**QUARDS:** The Quards have long waged war on the indigenous Vasnians and never really recovered from the devastation of the Demon Flood. Quards are often distrustful and mostly want to be left alone. Groups of Quards have returned to the homeland since Shadowgate Pass was reopened, but their Aslene brothers and sisters think that dishonorable practices like farming have made them soft. Loyal to their own kind, the Quards have nevertheless allowed the returnees to stay in the Crimson Forest by the lake Greyne, but will not help them cleanse the area of demonic plague. 

The Quards’ chieftain is called Jorgundos Ash, a quiet man regarded as a just and attentive leader, though he is notoriously difficult to convince of anything he does not already know or believe. 

17 

**the lay of the land** 

## **HORN AND HAVE** 

At the start of the game, Horn is the only active volcano in the Bloodmarch. The country’s horse clans and dwarves worship the volcano as their god and father. Myth has it that Horn gave the clans their horses at the time of Sella the Liberator and later helped them free the land from Vasnian hordes. Sometimes Horn erupts with fiery rage, but for the most part he sends out clouds of fertile ash which allow grass and herbs to thrive in the Bloodmarch. His sons are the five dormant volcanoes that lent their names to the horse clans. Most of the Bloodmarch rests on volcanic ground, save for a few smaller areas around Oxengelder which the power of Horn cannot reach. As GM you are free to place the adventure site Oxengelder anywhere on the map. The hexagon with Oxengelder and all land within two hexagons of it have a limestone base. 

To the horse clans, Horn is the god of power, construction, and creation, while Have has become a goddess of fire and fertility. The center for worshiping both deities is the temple of Ashenstead on Mount Faltenvil, an adventure site you may place anywhere on the plains. 

Despite their differences, the horse clans get on well with the dwarven followers of Horn. As a matter of fact, it was the dwarves who first encouraged them to worship Horn and forged Horn’s Astra 

for Sella the Liberator. Clan legends state that Horn gave them his astra for the war with the Vasnians, but the dwarves simply thought it was safest to have others test their new and powerful weapons, which were expected to have serious side effects. Most of the dwarven technology that forged Horn’s Astra was lost during the demon invasion. 

The priests believe that Horn in recent centuries has brought demons and other ills upon the Bloodmarch because the horse clans did not get along after the liberation. Now he puts their faith and strength to the test one last time to see if they can unite and drive out their common enemies. Selligar Horne of Havemark has accepted the challenge with the support of Likolapis, high priest of Horn at Ashenstead on Mount Faltenvil. 

Have, “the Blue Goddess,”, is the horse clans’ fire goddess and wife of Horn. She ignites the inner fire in people, whether it be rage, love, or a burning passion for one’s work. To “have” is to be filled with Have’s passion, and to “be having” is to carry a child, a dream, or an ability. Have is therefore the goddess of war, love, and fertility, but also the source of learning and creativity. 

Devotees of Have are sworn enemies of all demons, particularly the demonic red vegetation that threatens the land in the name of the god Rubor. _The Pyrolytes_ are a cult of demon hunters within the Church of Have. Their order produces holy fire and blue tar, both of which increase damage to demons. Both can be picked up at the Temple of Have. 

18 **chapter 3** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## VASNIANS 

The Bloodmarch’s indigenous population are called Vasnians, named after the region of Vasnia in the north, which they still inhabit in great numbers. Vasnians could be found all over the Bloodmarch before the horse people arrived from The Drawn Lands in the west. 

Vasnians are said to have a bit of orc blood in their veins, and they live in close contact with the region’s orcs and wolfkin. The horse clans assert that the Bloodmarch was given to them by Horn and that Vasnians were the weed that had to be removed. Vasnians, on the other hand, maintain that the clans stole the Bloodmarch from them. 

Vasnians are physically strong and sturdy. They have no written language and cherish the oral tradition, which has led the clans to look down on them as a lesser people. But Vasnians see themselves as part of nature, interconnected with all living things, rather than individuals. Like the orcs they worship the goddess _Volitia_ . Many Vasnians dream of reclaiming their land, including the prominent leader Konana. 

## RAVENLANDERS AND ALDERLANDERS 

Besides the horse clans and Vasnians there are humans of more mixed descent in the Bloodmarch. Most of them make their living as farmers in fortified villages at the edge of forests, on plains and hills. 

**SETTLERS:** In recent years, settlers have been pouring in from Ravenland, trying to survive as best they can under threat from various demons, plundering riders, or “adventurers” scourging the land. Some settlers are religious refugees who have fled their homes to escape persecution 

in an unspoiled environment. Most of the refugees worship the gods Raven or Wyrm. 

**ALDERLANDERS:** Rumor has it that Rust Churches in both Ravenland and Alderland are keeping an eye on the newly opened Bloodmarch, and that they have sent scouts on reconnaissance missions for a future invasion. The Congregation of the Serpent in Alderland is also said to have sent troops to the Bloodmarch – some say in search of a savior. 

## ELVES 

**RED ELVES:** The elves of the Bloodmarch are known as red elves, due to their more or less red skin, hair, and clothes. Everyone else looks at them with suspicion. The prevailing belief, even among elves from Ravenland, is that they were driven mad by the demonic plague. The red elves apparently want nothing to do with anyone else, and keep to themselves in the slimy gloom of the Crimson Forests. 

They seem to be utterly loyal to their leader, Vaerefor van Reiben, who dreams of a completely demonified world in the name of the god Rubor. Red elves are said to believe that red is the only natural color, while all other colors were created by the evil god Kolor in mockery of creation. Red elves often ride on the backs of strange creatures from the Crimson Forest, mainly sarcoptes and slimeshells. There are humans who have joined the red elves’ cause and are serving as their agents beyond the Crimson Forests. 

**RAVENLANDER ELVES:** Apart from red elves, one may also encounter regular elves from Ravenland in the Bloodmarch – visitors who are often 

19 

**the lay of the land** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

sent to locate, investigate, and perhaps remedy what has happened in the Bloodmarch, retrieve some missing object, or root out some unnatural being. 

**HALF ELVES:** Half elves are rare in the Bloodmarch and the ones encountered there usually come from Ravenland. The region’s red elves appear not to breed with humans, but there may be exceptions. 

dwarves view him as the malleable aspect of the stone god Huge. 

Aslene priests of Horn have developed a spe- cial form of Stone Singing called Magma Sing ing, which allows them to mold buildings. The Aslene dwarves abolished their clan system as clan wars made it possible for demons to invade Firestead as well. 

**MOON ELVES:** Moon elves are so rare that some even doubt their existence, but many swear that there really are moon elves hiding in certain parts of the Bloodmarch. Moon elves are said to be humanoid creatures with pale, bluish skin and white hair. Whether they really are related to elves is a matter of dispute. According to myth, they are cursed by the god Wyrm and carry a moonstone in their chest rather than the ruby of other elves. Moon elves are skilled in illusory magic and vanish without a trace as soon as anyone catches a glimpse of them. 

## DWARVES 

The dwarves of the Bloodmarch are based in Firestead, where they through isolation have managed to defend their only city, Helya, from the demonic plague. Aslene dwarves have a horn jutting from their forehead and call themselves Caprids. Kulli is their derogatory word for hornless dwarves, whom they consider weak degenerates. Other hornless creatures such as humans and elves are lumped together under the name baas kulli – lesser kulli. In return, hornless dwarves often imply that the Caprids have horns because their ancestors were so ugly they had to mate with goats. 

Caprids worship the volcano Horn. The god Horn is also called Warm Huge since the 

## ORCS 

_Volitia, Volitia, mother of all Heed your loyal servant’s call For the might you our kind gave I will make your foe my slave_ 

## – Orcish lullaby 

Unlike their brethren in Ravenland, the orcs of the Bloodmarch were never enslaved by elves or dwarves. They believe themselves to be the oldest and most primordial kin in the world, molded from the first soil by the creator god Clay. The orcs worship their goddess Volitia and male orcs are expected to complete her creation and purpose, called Étosh. 

The Aslene orcs move around in small family units and make their living hunting, gathering, and plundering. They hold regular assemblies where representatives of each group make pacts and mete out justice. The female orcs’ position is as strong as it is in Ravenland. Orcs tend to live in peace with both Vasnians and the region’s wolfkin. 

On the one hand, the Aslene orcs despise their relatives in Ravenland for allowing themselves to be subjugated by dwarves and elves, and even worse, trying to emulate them. Aslene orcs 

20 

**chapter 3** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

believe that an orc simply _is_ , and does not need to _become_ anything. Anything new will only dilute the old ideals and the message of Volitia. On the other hand, the leaders understand the power of unity within their kin, not least because the orcs in Ravenland far outnumber the ones in the Bloodmarch. 

They have common enemies in the plaindwelling humans, as well as dwarves and elves. The only real negotiations between the countries’ orcs are handled by females, since the males have a tendency to mock and start killing each other. The Sisterhood of Viraga in Ravenland has representatives in Vasnia. 

## **VOLITIA AND ÉTOSH** 

**==> picture [29 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [23 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

creatures and celebrate victories; males fight for rank and hunts are arranged where ecstatic females hunt down prey which are torn apart while still alive and finally devoured. There are no religious buildings or scriptures since the orcs of the Bloodmarch despise all writing. The Sisterhood of Viraga has no presence in the Bloodmarch, but the female orcs wield as much power as they do in Ravenland. 

The all-mother Volitia is a pantheistic forest goddess who ordered the creator Klei (the orcs’ name for Clay) to create the world simply because she could and wanted to. The purpose of the living is to, like Klei, fulfill their roles according to Volitia’s intentions which is evident in nature. 

**==> picture [29 x 279] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [23 x 279] intentionally omitted <==**

The orcs think they are closer to the all-mother than anyone else and that their primary duty is to reflect her in the world – through desire and capacity. The _desire_ is for pride, authenticity, procreation, power, and independence, while _capacity_ is the ability to overcome challenges and dominate weaker kin. Desire is mainly considered a female virtue and capacity a male one, but they are intertwined into a whole. 

Étosh is not a god, but a term for Volitia’s will, intention, and creation. Males are supposed to embody Étosh through manly deeds, such as war, song, competition, procreation, and domination. They see themselves as the all-mother’s tools and their accomplishments are what matters. A good male is capable, commanding, strong-willed, and produces strong offspring. Worthiness before Volitia is often displayed through religious symbols of varying complexity. It is possible to ritually challenge others for ownership of such symbols. 

The religion’s practice is simple, with orally preserved rituals, trials, and sacrifices, where old trees, glades, and rock formations are often used as alters. At the cult sites they sacrifice

## WOLFKIN 

There have been wolfkin in the Bloodmarch for a very long time. They are more confident than their fellow kinsfolk in Ravenland, and nobody sees them as the spawn of demons. It is instead said that the wolfkin of the Bloodmarch were created long ago by the Nightwalker Guard for a specific purpose now forgotten, though it is supposedly hidden in their noses. Wolfkin in the Bloodmarch often mingle with orcs and will sometimes produce offspring with one another, known as grargs. 

**==> picture [156 x 11] intentionally omitted <==**

**GRARGS:** Grargs are bulkier, more aggressive, and more animalistic than ordinary wolfkin. They also have tusks which make them look like wild boar on two legs. Grargs often move around the dwellings without living in them, because they are temperamental, sterile, and unable to form packs, but they can participate in hunts and battles alongside their relatives on both sides. The wolfkin keep them on a tight leash and do not hesitate to kill grargs who pose a threat to their young. In major battles the orcs will sometimes arm grargs with heavy knuckledusters and other crude weapons, drug them into a frenzy, and use them as vanguards on the battlefield where they advance like flesh- tearing windmills.

Worshipers of the Nightwalker often accept the moon goddess Eor as the god’s helper, his eyes, and sometimes his wife. The shadowy mist creatures known as umbras which can sometimes be glimpsed at dusk and dawn are believed to be sent by Guard to report on what they see and hear. The god also has his own cult in the Bloodmarch – the Order of Egression, who in the name of Guard bring peace to the dead so that they can move on from this life. 

Wolfkin in the Bloodmarch refer to the Nightwalker as the [Ranghöge][Ranghöge] and see him as the protector of nighttime creatures. Rather than praying to

## HALFLINGS AND GOBLINS 

## REGIONS 

There are few native halflings in the Bloodmarch, though small groups of them are rumored to dwell in secluded valleys. They are very shy, but also much more sympathetic towards each other than in Ravenland. Perhaps it is true that moon elves and wolfkin before the Demon Flood would hunt and kill these two kin, who never again trusted anyone but each other. 

## ASHENVALE 

The eastern plain of the Bloodmarch is called Ashenvale and was traditionally home to the Galdane horse clan. This part of the Bloodmarch was hit hardest and without warning by the Demon Flood in the year 875 AS, whereupon the Galdanes who did not make it to Ravenland were virtually wiped out, at least in their human form. The clan has recovered somewhat in recent years, but is still weak and few in number. 

The halflings and goblins encountered in the Bloodmarch come almost exclusively from Ravenland. They are pilgrims in search of the moonstones that some say were torn from their chests when their kin was split into halflings and goblins. 

23 

**the lay of the land** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## **EOR** 

**==> picture [30 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

Halflings and goblins worship the moon goddess Eor and think of her as their mother. Other kin generally dislike the moon, which they perceive as false and scheming. In Ravenland mythology, Eor was jealous when the Red Wanderer of the sky cast rubies into the world and created the elves. She therefore sent down her own moonstones from which the nocturnal moon elves were born. 

**==> picture [30 x 356] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 356] intentionally omitted <==**

But at the time of the Shift, when the Raven God divided the land, the moon elves tried to trick him. As punishment he tore the moonstones form their chests and split their kin into halflings and goblins. The moonstones were allegedly hidden in the Bloodmarch. 

In the Bloodmarch, Eor is only worshiped by halflings and goblins from the outside world. They revere salt as holy dust fallen from the moon. It can penetrate anything and be ritually purified into a powder so holy that it preserves food from rot. 

**==> picture [186 x 27] intentionally omitted <==**

Contrary to what some people believe, the region is not named the Ashenvale after the devastation, but because its soil consists of grayish black volcanic ashes from the volcano Horn. This ash is highly fertile as long as it is irrigated. Many Ravenlanders mistakenly believe that the entire Bloodmarch is called the Ashenvale, since they do not know about its more remote areas. 

## THE BLACK 

The Black north of the Ashen Vale is even blacker with Horn’s volcanic ashes. It also has cliffs of obsidian – black volcanic glass – a traditional material for temples, sculptures, weapons, jewelry, and talismans throughout the Bloodmarch. The rocky black landscape is streaked with porous white cinder, which creates a strange contrast throughout the entire region. 

The Black and Vasnia in the north first belonged to the country’s indigenous people, Vasnians, and later to the Quard horse clan. The Quards were at constant war with the Galdane and Houn clans from Havemark. Six centuries ago, a large group of Quards emigrated to Ravenland. The descendants of those who stayed behind, who were also decimated by demons, still portray them as weaklings and traitors in their stories. 

## VASNIA 

Vasnia is a more mountainous and less accessible region dominated by the Bloodmarch’s indigenous people, the Vasnians. There are plenty of orcs and wolfkin as well. The horse clans will usually not enter Vasnia unless they are many and well- armed, since they are not viewed favorably there. There are rumors about a few heavily 

24 **chapter 3** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

fortified villages built around ore deposits, but otherwise Vasnia consists almost entirely of wilderness. The Quards maintain a nominal claim on Vasnia, but in practice they have no control over the region. 

a foothold in Firestead as well. But the country is still relatively unscathed by the demons’ ravages, partly because there is not much to destroy. The dwarves are relentlessly hunting down all hateful miscreations and digging lava ditches through the use of magma singing. 

## HAVENMARK 

Havenmark is named after the volcano god’s wife, Have. The region was once lush and beautiful with verdant hills and woods where elves and humans lived in peace with each other. Now the land is badly tainted with demon plague and the very center of the unnatural vegetation’s spread. Most who are not red elves stay away from there. 

The Houn clan that once ruled Havenmark is all but extinct and its few surviving members tend to avoid both humans and other kin, but have nevertheless been energized by their strong young leader Selligar Horne. The consensus in the Bloodmarch is that Alderland somehow contributed to the Houn’s downfall through betrayal. But then again, malicious rumors about the Alderlanders spread like weed in the Bloodmarch. 

## FIRESTEAD 

A river of lava known as Burn flows westward from the volcano Horn until it crashes down into the wide gorge called Scorne Gorge. The dwarven realm north of Burn is called Firestead and coped fairly well with the Demon Flood, since the dwarves during the worst attacks managed to hold [Grinagap], the only natural bridge over the lava river. However, as civil war broke out between two dwarven clans, the Tarkins joined forces with demons who thereby gained 

## THE WAILING 

The wind- swept plain of The Wailing is named after the goddess Wail. Here, like in the even stormier Kreysel, the Sabirian horse clan once rode on their winged steeds. The eastern part of The Wailing is often called the Forest of Stone because of its numerous ravines and pillar- like cliffs. It is believed that the horses who roamed this area before the humans arrived developed wings so they would be able to move through the immense landscape and escape predators. The Sabirians are now as divided as the other horse clans in the Bloodmarch. 

## KREYSEL 

The plain narrows toward the west into the Screaming Land of Kreysel. Here the west wind howls so strongly and fiercely that few creatures can survive for long. The enduring plants are thick and grow along the ground, while animals in the region have developed turtle- like legs so as not to get swept away. The wind grew stronger during the Demon Flood, likely enhanced by magical means as the sorcerers of The Drawn Lands beyond Kreysel closed the passage to protect themselves. Anyone who travels west will eventually be stopped by the raging hurricane near the boundaries of The Drawn Lands. No one knows what lies beyond. 

25 

**the lay of the land** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## STRILLING 

Strilling mainly consists of arid shrublands where carnivorous dread raptors prey on wild horses and whatever else they can find. The regions of Strilling and Druma traditionally belonged to the Caberian horse clan, a militant warrior clan where young children were taught to fight on horseback before they could walk. In fact, it is said that Caberian warriors cannot walk even as adults because they rarely leave their saddles, which has left them so bowlegged that they can only waddle along like ducks, to the amusement of others. 

## DRUMA 

Druma could have been a pleasant, undulating land with lush forests, if not for the constant battles between the Bloodmarch and Alderland that were fought there. At the time of the wars there were a number of fortifications in the area – not only walls, palisades, and forts, but specially prepared - plains, cleared and hardened to facilitate cav alry charges. Ever since the route to Alderland was closed during the Demon Flood, the region has fallen into relative obscurity and a certain calm has settled there in recent centuries. Unfortunately, this calm is not only suitable to farmers, but also to demons trying to establish themselves in the world of humans. 

## VARINA 

The southwest corner of the Bloodmarch is hillier than the plains up north. The landscape gradually rises into a highland towards the [randbergen] in the southeast. It is a beautiful place, but nevertheless avoided by the horse clans who think it lies beyond the domains of the volcano god Horn. Furthermore, the region is ill- suited for riding as stones will often come loose under the horses’ hooves and cause them to stumble. 

## HADRUMA 

The highland of Hadruma rises towards Gander’s Pass in the southeast. It has been a devastated area for as long as anyone can remember, populated exclusively by vultures and wild dogs. On its barren slopes lie the bones and armor parts of countless generations, withered and trampled by subsequent armies. 

The Gander, a special guild of mages honored by both sides, watched over the pass for as long as it was open, but never favored either side in the wars. The eight wisest of the Gander climbed up the gigantic pillars that lined the pass and stayed there for the rest of their lives, at the end of which they hurled themselves to the ground below. Anyone who wanted could climb the eight pillars, known as [Gåsbenen], to seek advice and prophecies. 

During the Demon Flood, the sides of Gander’s Pass collapsed into several of the pillars and the pass has been blocked ever since. No one knows whether the collapse was caused by an earthquake or if the Gander triggered it themselves.

## MAGIC

ll spells from the _Player’s Handbook_ in the base game are found in the BloodAmarch as well. In addition, there are three new schools of magic: Magma Song as practiced by the country’s priests of Horn, Mentalism as practiced by the moon elves of the Bloodmarch, and Oneiromancy as it is taught by the followers of the Dreamstress (page 96). 

Adventurers who were not born in the Bloodmarch cannot know these magical traditions at 

the start of the game, but must find masters willing to teach them. 

Furthermore, there are local orders and cults that can cast spells from already existing magical traditions. These are the _Order of Egression_ (Blood Magic), the _Pyrolytes_ (Elemental Magic and Healing), and _Ad Vipera_ (Shapeshifting). The _Order of the Ironbrows_ from Alderland also has magnetic abilities similar to magic. 

27 

**magic** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## MAGMA SONG 

Magma Song is a sub- tradition of Stone Song, - dedicated to the volcano god Horn in the Blood march. Magma singers can control lava, fire, and hot water. Dwarven priests of Horn can learn both Stone Song and Magma Song, but must increase their ranks in the sub- traditions separately. Magma singers of other kin usually do not know Stone Song. 

Magma singers believe in the memory and potential of matter. They turn stone into lava by awakening its memory of what it once was, rather than by adding energy. That is why Magma Song does not work on limestone or sandstone, which were never magma. Likewise, water can be turned into steam by making it realize and fulfill its potential. Understanding and empathy are central in the lives of magma singers. Poetry is therefore highly valued by these priests. 

## IGNITE 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Immediate 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** Fire 

By manipulating the element of fire, you can cause an inanimate object to suddenly burst into flames. If the spell is used on a carried object, its bearer must drop the object or suffer one point of damage to Strength. If used on clothes or armor, the wearer suffers an attack with a number of base dice equal to double the Power Level (Weapon Damage 1, non- typical). The victim catches fire and keeps taking one point of damage per round until the fire is put out with a successful MOVE roll. Armor does not protect against this spell.

## FIRE’S FRIEND 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Personal 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A pinch of sulfur 

## MOLD STONE 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level ✥ **INGREDIENT:** Liquid lava or metal 

For the duration of the spell, you become immune to all forms of fire, including dragon fire and lava. For each Power Level another person in your vicinity can become fire’s friend too. However, the spell does not protect equipment or clothes. 

You can make hot, liquid stone or metal shape itself and solidify in any rough- cut form you want. Up to one cubic meter of material can be shaped per Power Level. The molded mass remembers and retains the desired shape when it cools and becomes solid. More precise shapes require a CRAFTING roll and molding weapons requires the SMITH talent. You can use this spell to form ten balls of stone for the spell Magma Cascade. 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## FIRE MEMORY 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Immediate 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A fistful of sand thrown at the stone 

You can instantly melt stone into lava. Up to a cubic meter of stone can be melted per Power Level. Fire Memory only affects stone that was once lava – not marble or other kinds of limestone. 

## WATER MEMORY 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

## STEAM FLIGHT 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Personal 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One round per Power Level and two liters of water 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A container of water carried in one’s arms 

You can make steam shoot out of a carried water container with such force that you are lifted off the ground, either rising vertically one meter per round or flying horizontally one zone per round until the effect wears off or the water runs out. If aimed at a living creature within ARM’S LENGTH range, the steam inflicts one point of damage to Strength per round. 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Immediate 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** Some form of smoke or steam in your vicinity 

## MAGMA CASCADE 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

You can make water instantly go from ice to cold water to warm water to steam or vice versa, one step per Power Level. The spell can affect up to two liters of water per Power Level. A container whose liquid is vaporized explodes and inflicts one point of damage per Power Level to Strength on everyone within ARM’S LENGTH range. Water that is instantly frozen can shatter a lock or container and cause opponents to slip unless they succeed with a MOVE roll. The water inside living creatures cannot be affected. 

Bonus: You can heat or cool the beverage in your cup to a suitable drinking temperature without spending Willpower Points or rolling dice. Furthermore, you are not negatively affected by changes in weather temperature. 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Immediate 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A fist- sized stone 

You can make a fist- sized chunk of volcanic rock explode in a cascade of magma that burns anyone standing too close and sets fire to combustible objects. The stone can explode in the desired location with a successful MARKSMANSHIP roll; otherwise the explosion takes place within NEAR range of the location in question. Alternatively, you can make a suitable stone on the ground explode in a magma cascade just as the target walks by, without making a skill roll. Everyone within ARM’S LENGTH range of the explosion suffers one point of damage to Strength per Power Level.

## FIREWALL 

   - ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A spear stuck into the ground and broken 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** Pulverized coal 

You cause a one- meter- thick horizontal or vertical firewall to burst out of a volcanic rock. The flames stretch one meter from the rock per Power Level and cover up to three meters in width per Power Level. Anyone who tries to pass through the wall suffers one point of damage to Strength per Power Level. 

You can summon a fire wyrm (page 111) from beneath the earth as long as you are standing on volcanic ground. The fire wyrm arrives in one turn and attacks everything in sight, except the person who summoned it. The fire wyrm cannot burst through solid rock, but can hurl up to half its body onto the rock in order to attack.

## SUMMON FIRE WYRM 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3

||**MENTALISM**<br>**SPELL**<br>**RANK**<br>Mental Strength<br>1<br>Wordplay<br>1<br>Traceless<br>1<br>Confusion<br>2<br>Amnesia<br>2<br>Truth Sense<br>2<br>False Shape<br>3<br>Fata Morgana<br>3<br>Geas<br>3|

The moon elves of Anoma beyond the seas were masters of mentalism, a magical tradition that affects the perception, memory, and will of intelligent creatures. They stole the Eight Mother from the worm people and fled, first to Ravenland and then onwards to Aslene. Modern moon elves have lost much of this knowledge, but are still excellent manipulators. 

## MENTAL STRENGTH 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level 

You can make one allied person or creature per Power Level immune to all damage to Wits and Empathy, as well as all fear attacks. When targeted by magical attacks against these attributes, the person can make an INSIGHT roll (not an action) with a positive modification equal to the Power Level to resist the attack. The roll cannot be pushed.

## WORDPLAY 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Permanent 

You can make one person per Power Level reinterpret what is said by using an ambiguous or homonymous word – i.e. a word or phrase that sounds the same but means something else. Example: “So _you_ will give me this animal?” when you are negotiating to buy a ewe, after which the farmer gives you the animal for free. 

suffering a critical injury) and incapable of rational thought. The victim must make an INSIGHT roll (not an action) with a negative modification equal to the Power Level to resist the spell. The roll cannot be pushed. Once the spell wears off the person recovers one point of Wits per round. 

## AMNESIA 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Permanent 

## TRACELESS 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Personal 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One hour per Power Level 

Any trace of you and one other person or creature per Power Level is completely altered for the duration of the spell. You do not become invisible, but your appearance, voice, body language, scent, and other personal traits change so that even a trained bloodhound immediately loses your trail. You cannot control how others perceive you – that is randomized or decided by the GM. 

You can make one intelligent person per Power Level forget what happened during the previous turn. The victim must make an INSIGHT roll (not an action) with a negative modification equal to the Power Level to resist the spell. The roll cannot be pushed. An affected person is not changed in any other way, but must reassess the situation. 

## TRUTH SENSE 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A nut 

## CONFUSION 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One round per Power Level 

The spell lets you tell if someone is lying, see through illusions, and understand what something hidden or distorted really is. For each Power Level the GM reveals something significant about the thing’s true nature. 

You can briefly reduce another person’s Wits to zero, which renders them Broken (without

## FALSE SHAPE 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Personal 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level

You can change your own appearance, voice, and smell into those of another living creature between half and double your original size. You do not acquire the creature’s attributes or language, even if it seems that way to others – it is only an illusion. Everyone who sees you can make an INSIGHT roll to understand that it is an illusion. Each additional Power Level adds –1 to the INSIGHT roll. A person within ARM’S LENGTH range who touches you immediately realizes that it is an illusion. 

the caster must concentrate on it and cannot - perform any other actions. More complex illu sions require a higher Power Level. A motionless illusion of a wall requires Power Level 1, but creating a convincing illusion of a person running around the corner of a house might require Power Level 3. The GM determines the Power Level required based on the description of the illusion. 

## GEAS 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3

## FATA MORGANA 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One round per Power Level 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A mirror or polished piece of metal 

You can create an illusion and control it with your mind. The illusion looks and acts according to your will, but is completely immaterial, has no scent, and leaves no trace. Everyone who sees the illusion can make an INSIGHT roll to understand that it is an illusion. Each additional Power Level adds –1 to the roll. Anyone who touches the illusion immediately sees through it. In order to maintain the illusion, 

Geas forces a creature to perform an action of your choosing. The creature must be able to understand you for the spell to work. The spell can make the creature perform almost any action, but you cannot force it to commit suicide or act in a way that leads to its death. The victim must make an INSIGHT roll (not an action) with a negative modification equal to the Power Level. On failure the creature remains enchanted until the action is carried out, no matter how long it takes. If the action proves impossible to carry out, the spell is canceled after one day per Power Level.

## ONEIROMANCY 

Oneiromancy is also known as dream magic. The tradition is taught by the Aslene prophet called the Dreamstress. The Dreamstress is a key player in the campaign _Legacy of Horn_ (page 96). Oneiromancers usually perform their magic while dreaming or in a trance, but a few spells that affects the sleep of others can be cast while awake. 

An oneiromancer who has put themselves into a magical sleep wakes up without the Willpower Points spent. 

## PREMONITION 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Personal 

- ✥ **DURATION:** The day after a night’s sleep 

Once per Power Level, you can make a skill roll for an action and only once you have seen the outcome of that action decide whether you actually went through with it or chose not to, since you knew beforehand that it would turn out badly. With Power Level 2 and higher, premonitions can also apply to your allies’ skill rolls, but it only works if they have slept next to you. You do not have to reveal the outcome, and the person in question decides whether or not the action is performed. Premonitioned rolls cannot be pushed or re- rolled after backing out. 

You can affect the sleep of one or multiple living creatures by injecting nightmares into their minds. The victims suffer damage to Wits equal to the Power Level. A victim that has its Wits reduced to zero suffers a critical fear injury. For every two additional Power Levels another victim is affected. The nightmares also render the creature SLEEPLESS for the next day. The spell has no effect on monsters. 

## RESTORATIVE SLEEP 

   - ✥ **RANK:** 1 

   - ✥ **RANGE:** Personal 

   - ✥ **DURATION:** The day after a night’s sleep 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

## NIGHTMARES 

After a night of restorative dreams you can increase one attribute or skill level per Power Level by one step for the rest of the day. 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Immediate

## DREAM VISIT 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Unlimited 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level 

In your sleep you can travel to any place that you have previously visited, invisibly watching - and hearing what is going on there. An addi tional Power Level allows you to appear before and communicate with people in that place, but not act physically. 

## MARA 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

## LULLABY 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Takes effect in one turn. Sleep lasts a Quarter Day unless the sleeper is woken up. 

You sing a quiet lullaby that makes humanoid creatures within range fall into a deep slumber unless they succeed with an INSIGHT roll (not an action) with a negative modification equal to the Power Level. The sleepers can be woken up, but will stay asleep if left alone. The lullaby also affects allies unless they somehow plug their ears. 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Personal 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One Quarter Day 

## DREAM TRAVEL 

You can summon a mara and force it to haunt a victim. For attributes and attacks, see Mara in the adventure site Oxengelder (page 194). You decide what form the mara should take, as long as it is not bigger than a human. But the figure is always twisted and terrifying, and a summoned mara always shares some features with the oneiromancer who conjured it. When summoning it, the sorcerer slips into a dream where the victim is visualized for the mara, who immediately goes after them. The sorcerer must have a clear mental image of the victim for the mara to understand who it is supposed to haunt. The mara does everything in its power to attack its victim, and everything it sees the sorcerer can see as well, as long as they remain in the dream. After completing its task, the mara disappears back into the shadows. The sorcerer may choose to wake from the dream at any time, but can never resume contact with the mara. 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Unlimited 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Travel after a Quarter Day’s sleep 

In your sleep, you can travel to any previously visited location. You bring with you any items on your person, but no animals or bulky baggage. Each additional Power Level allows you to bring another sleeping creature with you on your journey, but only to places that you have personally visited. You are transported after a Quarter Day’s sleep and wake up fully rested at your destination.

## SAND SLEEP 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Immediate, sleep lasts a Quarter Day 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** A handful of fine sand 

By throwing a handful of sand around yourself, you render all living, warm- blooded creatures of human size or smaller within NEAR range unconscious. Resisting the spell requires a successful INSIGHT roll (not an action) with a negative modification equal to the Power Level. Only you can wake them up while the spell is in effect. The spell also affects nearby allies. 

You can conjure or repair a non- magical item of moderate value during a night of prophetic dreams. You can also recreate someone’s lost body part, heal a condition, or even bring someone back from the dead, but only if you have not slept since the death or injury occurred.

Magnetism is a form of iron- based magic developed within the Rust Church’s warrior cult the Ironbrows (page 81). It is typically not taught to anyone outside of the order.

- ✥ **RANK:** 3 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Permanent after a Quarter Day’s sleep 

- ✥ **INGREDIENT:** Bone from a dead creature

## PATH OF IRON 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One Quarter Day per Power Level 

You can trace a person carrying iron items up to one day after the person passed by, since the weak magnetism of the metal affects the ground where the individual has walked. Each item leaves a unique trace. The trace cannot be followed in water. 

## IRON WILL 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn 

You can manipulate objects that contain metal; locking or unlocking shackles, unlocking

doors without a key, make someone’s sword temporarily stick in its sheath or shield, and so on – provided that the components contain metal. At Power Level 1 you can open or lock simple locks and manipulate light items. At Power Level 2 you can manipulate normalsized items, and Power Level 3 allows you to manipulate large, heavy items. The GM determines which Power Level is required for heavy items. 

## REPEL 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Arm’s Length 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One turn per Power Level 

You can make a metal armor anti- magnetic so that it repels weapons containing metal, including arrows with metal arrowheads. A negative modification equal to the Power Level is added to all attacks with metal weapons. 

## STANCH BLOOD 

- ✥ **RANK:** 1 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Arm’s Length 

- ✥ **DURATION:** Immediate 

## BLOODSHOCK 

- ✥ **RANK:** 3 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Near 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One Quarter Day 

You can stanch a bleeding wound by manipulating the iron levels in the blood, automatically saving the life of a person who has sustained a fatal critical injury. 

## ATTRACT 

- ✥ **RANK:** 2 

- ✥ **RANGE:** Short 

- ✥ **DURATION:** One round 

You can magnetize an enemy in metal armor so that it becomes easier to hit with metal weapons and suffers increased damage from such attacks. The spell adds a number of bonus dice equal to the Power Level to all attacks with metal weapons made against the enemy. You can also use the Power Level to make the effect last longer – each additional Power Level increases the duration by one round. 

You can manipulate an enemy’s blood so that the victim immediately loses consciousness. Resisting the spell requires a successful Wits roll (not an action) with a negative modification equal to the Power Level. The victim cannot be woken, but wakes up by themselves once the magic wears off. You can also use the Power Level to affect multiple victims at once – every two steps of increased Power Level causes the spell to affect an additional victim. 

For example, Power Level 3 can affect two victims and Power Level 5 three victims.

## DRUGS & POTIONS 

This section describes a number of drugs and potions found in the Bloodmarch. 

## BLUE TAR 

Blue Tar is produced by stripping the bark from the holy blue pines of Have and tapping them for sap. The sap is boiled into a thick, bluish- black liquid that has healing and cleansing properties, but is harmful to demons. Boiling blue tar takes time and requires a large stationary facility. 

At the temple of Ashenstead on Mount Falten vil burns the sacred blue flame of the goddess Have. If a torch or lantern is lit inside the temple, its flame also turns blue and holy and can be brought from there. All priests of Have can move such holy fire to some other combustible material and thus keep it burning, but they cannot spread the holy fire into multiple flames. For example, if five torches are lit with holy fire, only one of the six flames will turn blue and holy – the priest decides which one, and in the absence of a priest it is decided at random. 

## DAMAGE TO DEMONS: 

- ✥ Blue Tar grants +1 damage against all demons 

- ✥ Combining Blue Tar with holy fire grants another +1 damage, for a total of +2. 

## HEALING PROPERTIES: 

- ✥ Halts Crimson Plague which one can contract in Crimson Forests. 

- ✥ Cures Crimson Plague if the Blue Tar is first heated on holy fire. 

## OTHER PROPERTIES: 

- ✥ Makes Horn’s Astra glow briefly if they are smeared with Blue Tar. 

- ✥ Can be used to claim ownership of Horn’s Astra (see the potion Blue Blood). 

- ✥ Can be used to fuel the mechanical horse Witherbeam. 

- ✥ Can locally kill demonic vegetation and neutralize spore tissue on the ground. 

## **BLUE BLOOD**

## DENSE WATER 

## LYCOPODIUM POWDER 

Lycopodium Powder is stored in a pouch ( TINY item). 

**WEAPON:** Lycopodium Powder can be used against demons and blown at a demon within ARM’S LENGTH range. The attack is rolled with MIGHT . It has base damage 1 and cannot be parried or dodged. If the attack inflicts damage, the demon starts burning and suffers one point of damage per round until the fire is put out. Putting out the fire requires a successful MOVE roll (fast action in combat). 

**HEALING:** Burning Lycopodium Powder in a wound caused by demonic plague gives it a D12 artifact die for HEALING . However, burning the wound is so painful that the adventurer must succeed with an ENDURANCE roll to resist passing out. On failure the adventurer is unconscious for a Quarter Day. 

Dense Water is condensed water produced by the dwarven druids of Aslene. It looks like ordinary water, but lasts much longer both for drinking and other uses. The resource die for water is only lowered on result 1. Dense Water is also a powerful cleaning agent said to wash away vermin, mange, tattoos, and even disfiguring scars and unfortunate facial features. The water has therefore become much sought after in Alderland. 

In the campaign _Legacy of Horn_ , Dense Water is used to wash away the ownership of Horn’s Astra. It is also used as brake fluid in the mechanical horse Witherbeam. 

The production process is secret and must be supervised by a master. Among the ingredients are a live kidney and water run during certain astrological alignments. 

**REPLENISHMENT:** Dense Water can be purchased or obtained in several places in the Blood march. 

- ✥ The Kogler in the adventure site Tribolia of the Kogler 

- ✥ The adventure site Ashenstead

Crimson Ooze is a viscous, red fluid found all over the Crimson Forests. Some of it is produced by slime snails, but it also flows through the veins of the Veinpearls. Crimson Forest can 

- ✥ Kresos in the adventure site Bann Guelder 

- ✥ Master Menasse (the random encounter Dense Water, page 134)

## JOURNEYS IN THE BLOODMARCH 

_The dew bound the ash on the ground, just as the guide had said it would. Now dried by the heat of day, the dust puffed up with every step until no one could see their feet. They headed towards the volcano Quard in the north, but soon a wind stirred everything around them into gray. The dust soured in their throats like stale beer. They decided to wait it out in their tents, since no one knew where they were going. As they sat there, the laughter of small voices reached them from the haze, and Bodvar who was once a slave swore he smelled the scent of hunger in the wind. Everyone drew their swords, though it gave them little courage._ 

slene, known to many as the Bloodmarch, is drier than Ravenland, with barren cenAtral plains where sharp grass and shrubs are defiantly holding on in the wind. It has no swamps or marshes, though there are plenty of streams and rivers flowing to the plains from the mountains. The soil is often dark with volcanic ash and fertile as long as there is water. 

## NEW TERRAIN TYPES 

terrain types: Ashlands, Firelands, and Crimson Forest. When the adventurers LEAD THE WAY , FORAGE , or MAKE CAMP in these areas and suffer a mishap, a roll is made on the special tables further on in this chapter. 

**HUNT:** If the player characters HUNT in the Bloodmarch, use the rules in the _Player’s Hand­ book_ but roll for the type of animal on the table on page 51. Use the regular mishap table from the _Player’s Handbook_ . Animals in a Crimson Forest are often twisted versions of the ones in Ravenland, and their appearance and behavior can be harder to predict. 

The Bloodmarch has forests, plains, hills, and high mountains, but also three unique

## ASHLANDS 

The black ashlands are desert- like plains of volcanic ash where nothing grows. In this terrain type there is nothing to hunt, nor any edible plants to forage. The only water available is in the form of poisonous, dead pools (Potency D6+3). It is not advisable to make camp in ashlands. No healing or recovery of attributes can take place in this hazardous environment. 

## FIRELANDS 

In firelands, the magma flows so close to the surface that the ground burns under one’s feet. There are no edible animals to hunt. It is possible to forage for heat- resistant algae and certain edible roots, but it is hard and rarely rewarding work. Water is found in geysers and boiling springs, but it is undrinkable (Potency D6+1). 

Making camp in firelands counts as sleeping on bare ground, albeit in sweltering heat rather than freezing cold. The adventurers must make a SURVIVAL roll to get any sleep at all, and wake up THIRSTY . 

Hiking through firelands requires a successful SURVIVAL roll every Quarter Day in order not to suffer 1 point of damage to Strength from the hot ground. 

## **THE MAP**

## CRIMSON FOREST 

The most peculiar terrain type is the crimson forest – blood- red demon jungles tangled like spilled entrails in a strange landscape where grotesque hybrids of animals and plants roam in search of prey. 

In crimson forests it is not hard to find things that seem edible, but whoever eats or drinks anything off the land must succeed with an ENDURANCE roll not to contract Crimson Sickness.

**CRIMSON SICKNESS:** Adventurers who enter a crimson forest risk contracting Crimson Sickness. The GM rolls D3 on the table below for each infected adventurer to determine the initial symptoms. The symptoms appear after a Quarter Day. Each subsequent morning an infected adventurer must succeed with an ENDURANCE 

roll not to drop one step on the table while retaining all previous symptoms. However, the penalties to Empathy do not stack. 

The Crimson Sickness can be halted by drinking Blue Tar (page 38) every day and cured completely if the Blue Tar has been heated on the holy fire of Have.
