## THE LEGACY OF HORN 

_“In the beginning, Horn awoke, and his lava flowed hot over the land of Have so that she bore him five sons: Gald, Quard, Houner, Sabira, and Cabera. They coveted the Bloodmarch, where the Vasnians roamed. The sons chose Sella, eldest son of Houner, to lead the clans. And so Horn gave unto Sella five astra, legendary weapons that granted him the ability to take the god’s form on the battlefield. Then, from deep within, Horn spewed horses with manes of flame and gave them unto the clans, for the land was vast._ 

_Once the Vasnians had been banished, Sella entrusted each clan with one of Horn’s Astra. He then cast himself into the fire of Horn, for the feebleness of age was upon him.”_ 

elcome to the Game master’s section of this book. Players should read no Wfurther! _Legacy of Horn_ is a campaign set in Aslene, the land west of Ravenland that many know as the Bloodmarch. 

## BACKGROUND 

_Horn’s Astra_ are the five holy weapons that, according to legend, the volcano god Horn 

once bestowed upon Sella, the first leader of the Aslene horse clans. These weapons allowed Sella to conquer Aslene and defeat its indigenous people, known as the _Vasnians_ . 

When the fighting was over, the Astra were divided between the country’s five horse clans to prevent internal strife. This gesture of peace did not work as intended, as the clans have been fighting each other ever since. When the sorcerer Zygofer sent an army of demons into the Bloodmarch three hundred years ago, 

52 **chapter 6** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

everything fell apart and nobody knows what happened to the five Astra. 

The campaign is about who will find Horn’s Astra. For the horsefolk, whoever possesses the weapons would be the obvious leader of all clans. For the Vasnians, they would help reclaim the land they once lost. In the Alderlander capital of Brodelmarg, the Prophet Fangler preaches about the god Wyrm’s imminent rebirth in the Bloodmarch, and so groups of holy seekers are making the journey to Aslene, either to find the god and bring him home – or to kill this being before it grows too powerful. Locating and freeing (or killing) the god would likely require one or more of Horn’s Astra. The demons still lingering in the Bloodmarch have their own reasons for seeking the Astra, as do the mysterious moon elves. 

The adventurers can play their own part in this power struggle, ally themselves with one of the other key players, or go their own way – in which case parts of the campaign will serve as a backdrop for their own adventures in Aslene. 

- ✥ Vinhelm Kaal, sent by the moon elves at the hidden temple Agnostica. 

- ✥ Konana, leader of the Vasnians, Aslene’s indigenous people. _The Goblet Staff of Have_ , one of Horn’s Astra, is under 

   - Konana’s control in Vasnia. 

- ✥ Vaerefor van Reiben, leader of the demontainted red elves. 

- ✥ The Dreamstress, a prophetic demon with her own goals. 

- ✥ Octomadria, the Eighth Mother, at least according to the Seventh. 

More information on these individuals can be found in the Key Players chapter. The items that make up Horn’s Astra are described in the next chapter. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## THE PHASES OF THE CAMPAIGN 

Those competing for power are: 

- ✥ Selligar Horne, self- appointed leader of the horse clans. 

- ✥ The Shedder Poansa, agent of Ad Vipera and representative of the Congregation of the Serpent in Alderland. Possesses one of Horn’s Astra: the dagger _Glasstooth_ . 

- ✥ Colonel Grudensteel, leader of the Ironbrows and representative of the Rust Church in Alderland. 

- ✥ Fiena Fromelei, leader of the Penites, seeks to reunite the halflings and goblins. 

- ✥ The Seventh, a strange worm people from the continent Anoma. 

The campaign _Legacy of Horn_ plays out in seven phases, each beginning with certain events. If the adventurers themselves do not initiate the phases, they will be triggered anyway by the actions of other key players. 

Should the adventurers ignore the campaign and just go around plundering the Bloodmarch, the campaign will progress without them and become a backdrop for their escapades. 

The seven phases are outlined below. Listed under each phase are external events that may occur during the phase. As the GM you may adjust these events as you please, depending on how they fit into the developments of the campaign. 

53 

**the legacy of horn** 

## **CAMPAIGN OVERVIEW** 

1. **ARRIVAL IN THE BLOODMARCH:** The adventurers arrive at the adventure site Taregyll. 2. **THE SEARCH FOR HORN’S ASTRA:** Adventurers and key players search and fight for Horn’s Astra. As the weapons are found and claimed, four of Aslene’s five dormant volcanoes – the Sons of Horn – will awake. 

3. **THE HUNT FOR THE EIGHTH MOTHER:** Adventurers and key players search and fight for Octomadria – the Eighth Mother. The Congregation of the Serpent and the Rust Church both believe that this creature is Wyrm reborn – a blessing to the former, a threat to the latter. The moon elves have hidden the Eighth Mother at their temple Agnostica, the adventure site where the final showdown of the campaign will likely take place. At the start of the game, only the moon elves know about Agnostica. 

4. **TIME IS REBORN:** The temple Agnostica exists apart from the world and only returns once a certain number of Horn’s Astra have been collected and the Metrochrone restarted. The Metrochrone is a large clock found at the adventure site Sisters’ Wake. The Metrochrone can only be restarted once the adventurers have retrieved its stolen parts from the adventure site Tribolia of the Kogler or crafted spare parts with the help of the Dreamstress from the adventure site Oxengelder. 

5. **THE RETURN OF AGNOSTICA:** The temple Agnostica emerges when the Metrochrone has been restarted and four of the five sons of Horn have awakened. Agnostica appears in one of the hexagons surrounding the still dormant volcano. 

6. **THE FINAL SHOWDOWN:** The adventurers and at least one key player explore the temple Agnostica, and the fight between them decides the fate of Octomadria and the Bloodmarch itself. 

7. **AFTERMATH:** When the dust has settled, one or some of the key players will be more powerful than the others. The adventurers can experience – or hear about – the consequences of the final showdown. 

PHASE 1: 

## ARRIVAL IN THE BLOODMARCH 

_This phase begins when the adventurers arrive in Aslene._ 

At this phase the adventurers arrive at the village of Taregyll in eastern Aslene. They are briefed on the kin, history, and political 

conflicts of the Bloodmarch. They also learn that the demons who have plagued the land for three hundred years have lately grown stronger and that the leader of the red elves, Vaerefor van Reiben, seeks their help to conquer Aslene in the name of the red god Rubor. 

The horse clans seek to unite under a common leader to combat the demons, but their efforts are hampered by internal strife. 

54 **chapter 6** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The adventurers hear the legend of Horn’s Astra – the mighty weapons of Sella the Liberator that were lost and must now be found. They find out that Horn’s Astra are kept at Ashenstead, the main temple of Horn and Have in Aslene. They also learn that the goddess Have’s Blue Tar is a known weapon against demons, as is her holy fire. Key players can be introduced, either personally or through their agents – see the section on events in Taregyll. 

You can let the adventurers begin the game in some other location, perhaps as native Aslenes, but must then make the necessary adjustments. 

## PHASE 2: 

## THE SEARCH FOR HORN’S ASTRA 

_This phase begins when the players have visited two adventure sites._ 

The players have been told that they must collect Horn’s Astra in order to save the Bloodmarch. They might visit Ashenstead and learn how the Astra are related to each other and that they can be identified, activated, and owned by coating them with Blue Blood. 

The players see some of the country’s five dormant volcanoes – the Sons of Horn – become active when their corresponding astra are awakened (page 61). They also realize that others are also seeking the Astra, and might find them, which awakens the volcanoes. The phase ends with someone finding and claiming ownership of three related astra. Note that this person does not have to be a player character. 

**==> picture [157 x 11] intentionally omitted <==**

## **THE SONS OF HORN AWAKEN** 

The Sons of Horn is the name of five volcanoes in the Bloodmarch. They are dormant at the start of the campaign. Horn itself is a low- activity volcano. 

The first time someone binds one of Horn’s Astra to themselves (page 61) a Son of Horn awakens. In other words, the eruptions indicate how many of the Astra have been found. When four Astra have been found and bound to someone at least once, the temple Agnostica appears (page 232). 

All Sons of Horn follow the same pattern when they erupt. The eruption starts with rumbles and tremors in the ground for a Quarter Day. Then the volcano explodes, spewing copious amounts of ash, fire, and gas. The roar of the explosion is heard throughout the Bloodmarch. After erupting the volcano enters a high- activity stage. The effects of the explosion are: 

- ✥ A pyroclastic avalanche of thousanddegree ash, gas, stone, and lava flows down the slope and utterly devastates a random adjacent hexagon that is not high mountains. Everyone in that area is wiped out, but the GM can give the adventurers a chance to escape. The hexagon then turns into firelands. 

- ✥ In a Quarter Day a second adjacent hexagon that is not high mountains will be flooded by lava and turn into firelands.

**the legacy of horn**

- ✥ In a day’s time, two hexagons in a random direction will be turned to ashlands by a meter- thick ash rain. 

- ✥ The column of smoke can be seen from ten hexagons away.

The adventurers have probably already heard of the Eighth Mother, perhaps from Grudensteel, Poansa, Fiena Fromelei, or the Seventh, who are all seeking the creature for different reasons. 

During the third phase of the campaign, Fiena Fromelei finds a moonstone and is turned, along with a goblin, into a moon elf. She then regains the memory of the moon elves stealing the Eighth Mother, and understands what is at stake (see The Ancient History of the World). This can happen in the adventurers’ presence or somewhere else. 

Fiena remembers the temple Agnostica and realizes that she must find and collect all missing moonstones in the Bloodmarch and

## **THE ANCIENT HISTORY OF THE WORLD** 

started multiplying again. The druids knew that a new generation had been born and that the mass death and rebirth were the worm people’s way to evolve. 

_What is humankind to the Seventh? A mockery and a grievous disgrace. Such shall the Seventh also be to the god Meat: a mockery and a grievous disgrace._ 

Many weeks’ sailing east of Ravenland lies the mysterious continent Anoma. Today it is all but forgotten, but long ago humans and other kin lived there in prosperity. During an expedition to distant marshlands, the country’s druids found a kind of leech that showed signs of primitive intelligence. Separately these leeches behaved like simple ringed worms, but as a group they were able to use tools, understand commands, and build burrows. 

After a couple of lifetimes the humans lapsed into idleness, as the worms became more adept at performing their heavy labor. After only four generations the worms learned to organize to a worrying extent. The animals could gather in large colonies by filling out the skeletons of the animals they ate, and so they grew in strength and intellect. 

After the worms devoured a farmer and were seen walking around with his skeleton, the druids could see that it had gone too far and decided to exterminate the worms. A war was fought, and the humans lost. Now the worms became their masters rather than the other way around. By the fifth generation the worms understood the concepts of mythology and culture. The Fifth called their unborn god Meat – the Crawling Land. They themselves are the preliminary stages of Meat, whom they believe will be born from the twelfth generation of worm people. The worms 

The druids brought the animals home and started breeding them. They soon noticed that the worms could be taught to perform simple tasks. Strong, durable, and tireless, they were forced to do menial work for the humans. It went well, but when the animals grew into the thousands, all but a handful suddenly died – of disease, the humans thought. However, the surviving worms were stronger and more intelligent than their predecessors and soon 

PHASE 4: 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## TIME IS REBORN 

_This phase begins when the adventurers arrive at the Watch of the Sisters._ 

The dead priestesses at the Watch of the Sisters explain who they are, that Agnostica exists 

beyond time and space, and that it cannot return to the Bloodmarch until the great clock – the Metrochrone – has been restarted. They recount how the dwarf Kogler murdered them and stole important parts of the clock to create his own mechanical world, Tribolia, as he is fed up with living creatures 

call this event the Becoming and that is their ultimate goal. 

Beyond the Becoming there will be only Meat, and all living things will be part of her. She is omniscient, omnipotent, and crawlingly omnipresent. The sixth generation learned to control humans and animals by placing leeches on them. The seventh generation of worms even enslaved the country’s moon elves, a strange kin who mastered the magic art of mentalism. The Seventh gave the moon elves a favored status in return for their help in magically controlling the will and memories of other kin. As a reward they were promised a place on Meat’s surface rather than becoming her entrails like other creatures, in a future where the worm is everything and everything is the worm. 

However, when the eighth generation was about to be born, the moon elves decided that enough was enough. They prayed to the moon goddess Eor who sent a giant raven to their aid. Once most of the Seventh had died off as part of the generational shift, the giant raven stole _Octomadria_ – the Eighth Mother who would birth the next generation. 

The moon elves fled Anoma on their ships and followed the raven across 

the sea. The Seventh sent a fleet of enslaved humans in pursuit, but the moon elves twisted their memories with magic and made them think that they were following their raven god to a promised land. So the humans arrived, twelve centuries ago, in a land they called Ravenland after the raven god. 

The moon elves knew that the worm people would come for them sooner or later. Unable to kill Octomadria, they hid her away at their secret temple Agnostica which they moved beyond the world. Since this could not be done permanently and they were in a hurry, they linked the temple’s absence to a clock that was already in the Bloodmarch: the giant clock at the House of Winds. 

Since moon elves inherit their ancestors’ memories, they decided to split themselves into two ignorant kin – halflings and goblins – while waiting for the worm people to go extinct. In the process they tore the moonstones from their chests and hid them in Agnostica. Now the Seventh have learned from their vision that the thieves are in the Bloodmarch and that the Eighth Mother is alive. They have assembled into seven worm creatures on a mission to find and awaken the mother. 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

PHASE 5: 

## THE RETURN OF AGNOSTICA 

_This phase begins when the Metrochrone has been restarted and four Sons of Horn have awoken._ 

Once someone has claimed ownership of four Astra, four sons of Horn will have awoken. And as soon as the Metrochrone is restarted the temple Agnostica emerges in one of the six hexagons surrounding the last dormant Son of Horn (you can choose or randomize where the temple appears). At the same time the Cauldron of Flow is also activated (page 242). Even if someone owns all five of the Astra, one Son of Horn will remain dormant – the one where Agnostica emerges. The volcano that does not awaken is the one whose Astra was the last to be claimed. 

During this phase a race to the temple Agnostica begins. The temple can be removed from the world by activating its altar of time. 

## PHASE 6: 

## THE FINAL SHOWDOWN 

_This phase begins when the adventurers reach Agnostica at the same time as at least one key player._ 

The final battle for the temple Agnostica and the awakening of the Eighth Mother. 

## PHASE 7: 

## AFTERMATH 

_This phase begins when the final battle has been fought._ 

This epilogue summarizes the situation in the Bloodmarch and what is likely to happen next. 

The players receive hints of what might happen in future campaigns. The road to Alderland – Gander’s Pass – is opened. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## ADVENTURE SITES 

In _Forbidden Lands_ , adventure sites play a major role in the player characters’ exploits. That is as true in the Bloodmarch as it is in Ravenland and the Bitter Reach. 

Later chapters of this book describe eight pre-made adventure sites that all have some connection to the campaign _Legacy of Horn_ . These can be placed at various locations in Aslene as described in Chapter 8 of the _Game master’s Guide_ . But there is one difference – the map of Aslene only has one type of adventure site: SETTLEMENT . 

You decide which SETTLEMENT will represent which adventure site. If you have access to the separate map pack for The Bloodmarch, you can use the included stickers to mark the adventure sites on the map as the adventurers learn their locations. However, note that Tare gyll already has an established place on the map, since it is presumed to be the adventurers’ starting point. 

**OTHER ADVENTURE SITES:** If you need additional adventure sites other than those described in this book, you can create them using the tables in Chapter 8 of the _Game­ master’s Guide_ , and adjust them to the people and nature of Aslene.

**the legacy of horn** 

## ARTIFACTS 

_When Sella the Liberator rose anew out of the magma, he was bearing Horn’s Astra and his companions kneeled before him, for it seemed to them that the chieftain emerged stronger and aged in wisdom from the fires of the underworld. His head was crowned by the Helm of Horn, which spoke with the Stonechest of Horn around his torso and Sella’s Dragonboot on his foot. On his belt hung the dagger Glasstooth and in his quiver slithered the three Arrows of the Fire Wyrm. But in his hand the chieftain held the Goblet Staff of Have with an all­ giving grail at its end. It was said that possessing all the Astra allowed Sella to wield the divine power of Horn._ 

t the heart of the campaign _Legacy of Horn_ is the search for the five artifacts Aknown collectively as Horn’s Astra, a set of powerful magical items said to be hiding in the Bloodmarch. 

Legend says that the god Horn gave his Astra to the hero Sella to help him conquer the Bloodmarch for the horse clans, which he succeeded in doing. After the wars each of the five clans took one of Horn’s Astra into their care. Sella thought this would preserve the unity between them, but the peace crumbled almost immediately. 

What really happened was that the goddesses Flow and Wail had sent Sella and his riders from the Drawn Lands west of Aslene to seek peace 

and commerce with the Bloodmarch’s indigenous population, the Vasnians. But the proud Sella was offended by some incident during the meeting and let the peace efforts collapse into open war. 

The goddesses were so furious that they rejected their legates and blocked the road back to the Drawn Lands with an everlasting hurricane that rages to this day. Now godless, the horse clans turned to the volcano god Horn, encouraged by the dwarves of Firestead. 

The dwarves gave Sella the mechanical horse Witherbeam as well as a set of powerful weapons they did not dare test themselves – Horn’s Astra – and from this situation the myth arose. The dwarves’ plan was to let the horse clans drive the 

60 **chapter 7** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

Vasnians from the mining districts of Nuggler’s Harrow, but instead Sella began a war of extermination, which to the dwarves has become an historical embarrassment they do not want to be associated with. The true story is recorded in the Book of Winds which can be found at the adventure site Watch of the Sisters (page 207). 

For the past three centuries, when demons have ravaged the Bloodmarch, much has been destroyed. Four of the five Astra are missing and only clan Cabera in Strilling are said to still have their Astra, the dagger Glasstooth, in their possession (although it is in fact a forgery). But now new winds are blowing and several key players have for various reasons started looking for Horn’s Astra. 

Each of the five Astra has a Horn rune engraved into it. The Astra are surrounded by an aura of faint blue light while they have a rightful owner, but will also glow for a brief moment if they are coated with Blue Tar, which is a way of identifying them. In parentheses are the clan and volcano traditionally associated with each item. 

- ✥ The Helm of Horns (Houns, Houner) 

- ✥ Sella’s Dragonboot (Sabirians, Sabira) 

## BINDING THE ASTRA 

Each Astra can be bound to a person by smearing the item with Blue Blood – an equal mixture of Blue Tar (page 38) and one’s own blood. Being bound to an Astra enhances the powers it confers, but also accelerates the person’s aging. The person remains bound to the Astra until they die or the item is washed with Dense Water (page 39). Only then can the Astra be bound to a new master. 

**THE VOLCANOES:** The first time an Astra is bound to a person it awakens one of the volcanoes known as the Sons of Horn. Read more on page 55. 

## RELATIONS BETWEEN THE ASTRA 

Each of Horn’s five Astra is related to two others, as shown by the pentagram in Sella’s fresco (page 62). The fresco can be found at two adventure sites: Ashenstead and Tribolia of the Kogler. The horse people of Aslene sometimes carry copies of the fresco as a religious symbol, but the copies are not always accurate. 

- ✥ The Dagger Glasstooth (Caberians, Cabera) 

- ✥ The Goblet Staff of Have (Quards, Quard) 

- ✥ The Arrows of the Fire Wyrm (Galdanes, Gald) 

In addition, there is the Stonechest of Horn, a breastplate that can be summoned once a single person owns four of the Astra. See the section Relations Between the Astra below. 

The information required in order to make full use of Horn’s Astra will become available to the adventurer over the course of the campaign. 

## THE POWERS OF THE ASTRA 

Each of Horn’s Astra has three levels of magical powers. 

- ✥ **LEVEL 1** can be used by anyone, without being bound to the Astra. 

- ✥ **LEVEL 2** can only be used by someone bound to the Astra when at least one related Astra is within NEAR range. 

- ✥ **LEVEL 3** can only be used by someone bound to the Astra as well as two of its related Astra.

## THE STONECHEST OF HORN 

Should all five Astra be bound to the same person, the Stonechest of Horn – a powerful armor – will materialize around the person’s torso. 

If four Astra are bound to the same person, the Stonechest of Horn can be summoned through a ritual known to the Horn priests at Ashenstead or in Firestead. During the ritual the fifth and missing Astra will be summoned and bound to the person. 

If the bearer of the Stonechest dies, the armor dissolves into mist and cannot be summoned again. The Stonechest of Horn can only be summoned once in a generation. 

## ACCELERATED AGING 

A person bound to one or more of Horn’s Astra ages faster than normal. Their aging accelerates to ten times its normal rate per total number of bound Astra multiplied by the maximum level for bound Astra. This means that people bound to a single Astra will age ten months in one month. A person bound to all five Astra ages 150 times faster than normal (10 × 5 × 3), i.e. three years in just one week. 

According to legend, Sella liberated the Bloodmarch in three month and aged 36 years in that time. When the work was finished he threw himself into the volcano Horn because he had grown so old. This was not a big deal for him, since Aslene horse warriors consider dying of old age a shameful way to go. A glorious warrior should fall in battle, preferably in a heroic fashion. 

**EFFECT:** If the accelerated aging makes a player character go from young to adult, or from adult to old (page 31 in the _Player’s Handbook_ ), the player must permanently reduce an attribute of 

their choosing by one step, to a minimum of 1. A player who owns any of Horn’s Astra should receive warnings about the rapid aging, for example in the form of sudden wrinkles or hair loss. 

The rapid aging ceases when the owner loses or relinquishes ownership of one or more of Horn’s Astra. In other words, it is possible to carry Astra inactive and only assume ownership by smearing them with Blue Blood when heroic deeds are called for.

## THE GOBLET STAFF OF HAVE 

_It is said that Horn with his kiss gave Sella the Lib­ erator the gift of speech in order that the clans would follow their chieftain’s commands without question, as if they came from the god himself. Then Horn’s wife, Have, presented the hero with her goblet staff so that he whose lips had touched the divine would never have to drink anything but the noblest of bev­ erages. For anything poured into the goblet turns into exquisite wine and empowering nectar._ 

The Goblet Staff of Have is the Astra traditionally belonging to the Quards, linked to the volcano Quard. When the staff is bound to a person, Quard awakens. 

## SUGGESTED LOCATION 

The key player Vaerefor van Reiben once owned the Goblet Staff of Have, before it fell into the hands of the Vasnians during a war long ago. The staff can be found in a chieftain’s hall in Vasnia, mounted on the wall as a prize of war. Konana 

63 

**artifacts** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

is aware of this and is willing to barter with it. She knows its worth and that Vaerefor desires it. Other Vasnians refuse to part with the staff, so a replica will be required in the event of a trade. 

## EFFECTS 

✥ **LEVEL 1:** In one turn, one dose of liquid poured into the staff’s goblet turns into a liquid or beverage of the bearer’s choice – except for magical liquids or poisons, Blue Tar, or Dense Water. 

✥ **LEVEL 2:** In one turn, one dose of liquid turns into any drug, acid, or poison the bearer desires. The staff can also create Blue Tar or Dense Water. 

✥ **LEVEL 3:** Once per Quarter Day, one dose of liquid can be turned into any other liquid or into a dose of Goblet Tonic that heals all injuries (even critical ones), cures all poisons and diseases, and can even resurrect a recently deceased person. 

## DRAWBACKS 

Every time the staff’s magic is used at level 2 or 3, a D6 is rolled. Rolling a **l** means that the hexagon where the user is currently positioned turns into Crimson Forest. The transformation takes one week and first manifests itself in the form of spider veins spreading from an underground source. 

## THE HELM OF HORN 

_The archers marveled at the hal­ fling’s ability to evade every arrow they fired, but eventually they grew exasperated, for the game had dented their pride. Urse sensed deceit – some said fox blood flowed through his veins. He wondered why the helmet’s moon­ stone glowed a fiery red, but when the halfling took it off to look, Urse fired another arrow that pierced the creature from shoulder to shoulder._ 

The Helm of Horns is the Astra traditionally belonging to the Houns, linked to the volcano Houner. When the helmet is bound to a person, Houner awakens. The helmet is made for a human, but can be adjusted to fit other kin at the risk of looking a bit silly. 

## SUGGESTED LOCATION 

The Helm of Horn is missing and can be placed at any of the campaign’s adventure sites. The helmet has a moonstone embedded in its forehead. This allows Fiena Fromelei (page 82) to sense in which direction it is located, although she does not know that the stone sits in one of Horn’s Astra. Removing the moonstone stops the helmet from working as an Astra, though it retains its magic at level 1. Reinserting a moonstone into the helmet restores its function. 

64 **chapter 7** 

**==> picture [219 x 290] intentionally omitted <==**

## EFFECTS 

- ✥ **LEVEL 1:** The wearer can see in the dark. The helmet has Armor Rating 3. 

- ✥ **LEVEL 2:** The owner’s Wits is increased by 1 while wearing the helmet. 

- ✥ **LEVEL 3:** The helmet provides full protection against all non- magical ranged attacks. It also protects against fear attacks and makes the wearer immune to MANIPULATION . 

## DRAWBACKS 

Ranged attacks diverted from the wearer instead hit another creature within NEAR range. The wearer’s hair turns completely white over the course of a week. 

## SELLA’S DRAGONBOOT 

_We couldn’t believe our eyes when the rascal ran off across the rooftops without slipping or stumbling despite the pouring rain, with the stolen goods tied around his neck. On reaching the fishing huts he leapt like a cat from post to post, but finally we had him cornered against the water with no way of escape. But was he arrested, you think? Oh no, right in front of our lowered lances he scuttled across the water’s surface as if it were solid ground. A bowshot from the shore there was a boat waiting for him, which soon sailed off in the night. On the beach we found a right boot of dragon skin._ 

Sella’s Dragonboot is the Astra traditionally belonging to the Sabirians, linked to the volcano Sabira. When the boot is bound to a person, Sabira awakens. Sella’s Dragonboot is a left boot but magically summons a matching right boot for its wearer.

65 

**artifacts**

## SUGGESTED LOCATION 

The Dragonboot is missing and could be found at any of the adventure sites or with the traveling jester Pottus Tynk who thinks it suits his stage costume. See the random event Last Night of the Jesters (page 142). Pottus does not know that the boot is magical, though it helps him maintain balance on stage. 

## EFFECTS 

✥ **LEVEL 1:** Gives the wearer perfect balance which, for example, allows them to effortlessly walk a tightrope. The wearer does not have to make MOVE rolls to keep their balance and cannot be knocked over. 

- ✥ **LEVEL 2:** Allows the wearer to walk on water and lava without suffering damage or plunging through the surface. 

✥ **LEVEL 3:** Allows the wearer to leap to anything within SHORT range (fast action) and land with perfect balance. The wearer can also summon an earthquake around themselves by stomping the ground. Everyone within NEAR range must then make a MOVE roll not to fall over. 

## DRAWBACKS 

The boot’s power stems from the principle of universal balance, which is why all allies within SHORT range get –1 to all Agility- based skills while level 2 or 3 of the boot’s magic is activated. 

## GLASSTOOTH 

_Glass seems transparent because only half of its being exists where you think it does. The rest of it sweeps through the ether in search of sun dust like swallows hunting mosquitoes in summer. The same is true of the dagger Glasstooth, also known as Souldrinker. I have personally seen the weapon in three difference locations. Transient and ephemeral, the dagger preys on souls, but when one of its shapes has taken a life, the parts come together to carve the name of Sella the Liberator at site of the kill._ 

Glasstooth is the Astra traditionally belonging to the Caberians, linked to the volcano Cabera. When the dagger is bound to a person, Cabera awakens. 

## SUGGESTED LOCATION 

At the start of the game, Poansa Moulter (page 76) or the elf Mahogany of the Inglourious Butchars (random event, see page 141) could be in possession of the dagger. The one who does not have the actual dagger carries a replica instead. The person with the real dagger does not know that it is one of Horn’s Astra and has not claimed ownership of the weapon. Poansa and Mahogany 

both believe that they are carrying the profoundly cursed dagger Serpent’s Bane, which allegedly has the power to “kill sunlight, shadow, and the soul itself”. Meanwhile, the Caberians in Strilling claim that they are in possession of Glasstooth, but their revered dagger is a replica. The Caberian Elders know this, but are keeping it to

themselves because the weapon is crucial for the clan members’ self- esteem.

## EFFECTS 

- ✥ **LEVEL 1:** The arrow gives the user a D8 Artifact Die when fired from a bow, and returns to its owner after three rounds (to the quiver if it exists, otherwise on the ground in front of the archer). 

_Stories tell how the Vasnians gave the name­ less rider and his companions a bushel of gold to slay Sella the Liberator while he was praying to Horn alone in the wilds. But the hero was unfazed by_

**artifacts**

The Stonechest of Horn is an ornate breastplate that can be summoned once someone owns four of Horn’s Astra and emerges automatically if someone owns all five. The Stonechest arranges itself to fit the owner and remains even after the person loses their Astra. The Stonechest dissolves upon the death of its owner.

The Stonechest of Horn has Armor Rating 8 and counts as a light item.

The arrow that was buried in Krasylla (page 136) attracts the fire wyrm Erinya, who thinks its owner is the demon. Roll D6 every Quarter Hour that the bearer spends in firelands – one **l** means that the fire wyrm attacks. ## WITHERBEAM 

_Sella the Liberator rode his steed Witherbeam like a storm wind across the battlefield, where the Vas­ nians fell like wheat before the scythe. It was said that the horse was bred in the Drawn Lands, as two desert winds slipped through the hair gap of Vanna and were squeezed so tightly that their air became red. In the end Witherbeam fell, but the dwarves flayed him and filled out his skin with a mechanical body, powered by Blue Tar and water, and returned the animal for Sella to ride. However, to keep the spirits of the machinery alive a living human must be sacrificed under each full moon._ 

While Witherbeam is active, the horse can be extended to carry up to three riders by inserting a couple of side pieces under the edge of the saddle on both sides while squeezing the horse on. This is no simple mechanism, but the Kogler and or some other mechanically gifted individual can understand it. 

Witherbeam automatically gives its rider the talents Path of the Plains and Path of the Knight, both at rank 1. If the rider already has these talents, the rank increases by 1 on Witherbeam’s back, up to a maximum of 3. 

Witherbeam counts as a Combat Trained Horse but has Strength 8, Agility 4, and Movement 3. 

## SUGGESTED LOCATION 

Witherbeam stands on the cliff by the Guardian Temple, a field temple dedicated to Horn and Have, from which he overlooks the plain. See the random encounter The Guardian Temple (page 147). 

## DRAWBACKS 

If it runs out of Blue Tar, Witherbeam stops and remains motionless until it is refueled with Blue Tar. If it runs out of Dense Water it is no longer possible to brake, and Witherbeam keeps running until it runs out of Blue Tar as well. 

## EFFECT 

Witherbeam is steered like an ordinary horse, with reins, legs, and other aids. The horse is fueled with Blue Tar which is poured through the nose after the bigger horn has been unscrewed. The horse brakes if Dense Water is poured through the nose after unscrewing the smaller horn. 

Refueling is done by sacrificing Resource Dice of the respective liquid. The same number of Resource Dice are then transferred to Wither beam. Every Quarter Day that the horse is used a roll is made for each liquid.

**artifacts** 

## KEY PLAYERS 

_“Only fools break ground alone in the wilderness,” said Colo­ nel Grudenstaal. He sized up the woman under heavy eyelids lined with rust­ colored tattoos. “Bandits wish to steal the cattle while the farmer sows his seed. Wise are the farmers who work together, for their strength is doubled and their eyes many.”_ 

_“So are you looking for a wife, hand, or slave for your new farm?” replied Poansa Moulter with a steady gaze. “Our churches are still bitter adversaries.”_ 

_“Have we not been slaves long enough, you and I, albeit under different masters?” answered the colonel and won the woman’s slowly growing smile._ 

everal key players in _Legacy of Horn_ want to get their hands on one or more Sof Horn’s Astra, an ancient set of magical weapons said to have belonged to Sella the Liberator (known among the Vasnians as Sella the Butcher). The key players all have their own intentions, but will likely form alliances with each other over the course of the campaign if it benefits them. Several of the key players behave discreetly and prefer not to reveal their true identities or agendas to strangers. At the same time, many of them need all the help they can get. 

## SELLIGAR HORNE OF HAVENMARK 

Selligar Horne of Havenmark (the byname Horne he added himself) is a handsome, charismatic, and skilled horseback warrior. He is the leader of the respected but decimated Houns and commander of the Horn Riders – a growing group of more or less fanatical horse warriors from all clans. Selligar rides around to strengthen the alliance between the horse clans and generate interest in his cause – to reunite and retake the Bloodmarch using Horn’s Astra. Selligar claims to be a direct 

70 **chapter 8** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

descendant of Sella the Liberator, the legendary figure who conquered the Bloodmarch for the horse clans back when the world was young. It is an accepted and highly respected lineage. 

But there are some who question his claims, since the Hours are said to have destroyed each other to the last man in a ritual combat during the Demon Flood three hundred years ago, to avoid the disgrace of falling in battle against them – which seemed to be the only alternative. This would make Selligar either a false heir or a descendant of one of the cowards who fled the honorable internal slaughter. However, Selligar himself maintains that his 

ancestor Sumber Greatheel alone took responsibility for the clan and led a small force to hold off the demons and give the rest of the tribe enough time to kill each other. Sumber’s warriors were supposedly slain to the last man, but his pregnant wife Ismaela managed to escape. 

No one knows what really happened three centuries ago, but it remains a sensitive issue. 

71 

**key players** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

Selligar has killed several doubters in single combat. But most of the horse clans still accept him, realizing that no one else will be able to unite them. They are running out of time as more and more foreigners cross their borders. Mommodar and Merdekai, leaders of clans Sabira and Quard, would prefer Selligar to go away so that they could lead the united clans themselves. However, it is unlikely that either of them would willingly submit to the other. If Selligar dies, he can be replaced with these two, or with their children Stiltellos and Gerbera (see the adventure site Ashenstead on page 162). 

## **THE HORN RIDERS** 

**==> picture [30 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

The Horn Riders is a group of Aslene horse warriors who have pledged allegiance to the unification of the clans and the liberation of the Bloodmarch. They are more loyal to their vision and their brethren than to their own clan, which is not looked upon kindly by more traditional clansfolk – even though the dream of a united country predates the clan structure, which the Horn Riders are quick to point out. The leaders of the Sabirians and Caberians even consider the Horn Riders a threat and would not shed a tear if Selligar Horne disappeared and his booming, seductive voice was never heard again.

## LEGEND 

_It is said that the Houns’ chieftain, Selligar Horne of Havenmark, emerged from his mother’s womb a capable rider. He mounted the nearest horse, and with the umbilical cord fluttering in the wind, he immediately rode off to inspect his domains. Only afterwards did he return to her breast. The father was never again allowed near his wife, for the boy was jealous and believed that further offspring could only be inferior to himself. He was strong from birth and slew his first challengers in combat before he had grown his fourth tooth. Now he rides across the plains as a proud youth, like his ancestor Sella the Liberator before him, intent on gathering the clans so that all foreign malice can be driven from the Bloodmarch. The seers say that Selligar is destined to rule Ravenland and Alderland as well. He means to open the Galder Gorge to Alder­ land, and lifted one of the giant pillars with his bare arms just to prove himself worthy of such a task. But the time for doing so has not yet come._ 

## APPEARANCE 

Selligar is a tall and handsome rider in his forties, with thick chestnut hair that flows dramatically in the wind as he rides forth (in battle he braids his hair like any other Aslene rider). Selligar is lavishly dressed in silver- plated chainmail worn under a tunic made of brightly colored silk triangles, adored with bronze and gold. Sewn on to the tunic are lemon yellow strips of cloth, which are supposed to flutter behind him to create an illusion of fire when he rides. 

Selligar Horn is loud, hot- tempered, and enjoys luxury and combat as much as the sound of his own voice. He is popular with women and has a number of children spread all over 

72 

**chapter 8** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

the country. He claims to be looking for his future consort, _“the lovely Totilla”_ , but no one knows if she exists or if anyone would even be good enough. His adjutants are used to breaking up fights that their leader gets himself into, as well as playing down and glossing over his illconsidered statements, challenges, and promises. 

## OVERT GOALS 

Selligar Horne truly believes that he is Horn’s chosen one, Sella’s heir, and the destined liberator of the Bloodmarch. His goal is to collect Horn’s Astra, wear the holy items, and lead the clans to drive all demons and other undesirables from the land. He tolerates foreigners as long as they are respectful and do not come from Alderland. Alderlanders are his sworn enemies, and he intends to wreak vengeance upon his arch enemy once the liberation has been achieved. In Aslene eyes, Alderland was responsible for the demon flood that devastated the country. If Selligar gets his hands on one of Horn’s Astra, he will never part with the artifact, believing that it is his by right. 

Since his efforts to unify the clans are progressing rather slowly, Selligar has been fraternizing with the Dreamstress in Oxengelder as a backup plan. The Dreamstress has promised to dream the liberation of Aslene into existence, provided that she can assemble a large enough group of competent dreamers. For her part, the Dreamstress hopes to obtain Horn’s Astra through Selligar. 

fluent in all five clan dialects but cannot shut up in any of them. 

## MEETING SELLIGAR HORNE 

Selligar is active from the start of the game and resides in Havenmark, but is often visiting the other clans along with a personal guard of twenty riders. He never shows up discreetly but prefers to have his arrival announced by bards and heralds. 

## RELATIONS 

Selligar may exchange favors with all the other key players, but would not willingly hand over Horn’s Astra to them. 

**THE DREAMSTRESS:** Selligar Horne is loosely allied with the Dreamstress who will help him unite the clans and find Horn’s Astra. 

**POANSA AND GRUDENSTAAL:** Selligar has never met Poansa or Grudenstaal, but would immediately consider them enemies if he learned that they are from Alderland. That being said, he could quickly change his mind and work with them if he knew that they wish to rebel against Alderland and lead an attack on the country from Aslene.

Selligar has no secret goals. In fact, he finds it difficult not to immediately talk about whatever pops into his head. They say that he is

## POANSA MOULTER 

The Wyrm priestess Poansa started out as a dancer and spy in the Alderlander capital of Brodelmarg. After uncovering what is known 

as “the Rust- Rot Scandal,” where four local noblemen secretly collaborated with the Rust Church and were later executed by snakebite, Poansa was ordained to priesthood and quickly rose through the ranks of the Congregation of the Serpent. Now, only ten years later, she is one of the most senior officers of Ad Vipera – the sect serving as the Church’s secret police. She was the first to be interested in the Prophet Fangler’s speech about the god Wyrm’s rebirth in the Bloodmarch. She is currently leading a secret task force of sixty agents sent to Aslene to find, protect, and bring the god back to Brodelmarg. 

## LEGEND 

_You might think that Poansa is called “moulter” because of her flaking skin. Not so. It’s because she leaves empty shells of people in her wake. The shells are not her own, but the remains of those she has devoured along the way. One of them was Grene­ mer Treba, count of Strula until that venomous woman seduced him and found his secret Rust Altar. Once imprisoned he demanded, as a noble­ man, to be executed by iron – for religious reasons that would become clear later – and appealed to_ 

_Ad Vipera on the matter. He was surprised to find Poansa in one of the council seats. But the ­ Moulter granted his wish and had him exe cuted by iron as requested: they pounded iron nails through his body until the screams died down and the blood stopped flowing._ 

## APPEARANCE 

Poansa Moulter is roughly 35 years old, thin and agile, pale, with a rather ordinary appearance and short, dark hair. The 

76 

**chapter 8** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

inside of her mouth is completely blackened to indicate that she is part of the Sisterhood of the Black Mamba. When she speaks, her teeth with the sharpened canines look bright white against the darkness within. She wears strict, gray clothes of minimalist style and quality. Her voice is calm and composed, and she is an excellent singer. Poansa’s movements are balanced and smooth. 

## OVERT GOALS 

- Poansa is sent to the Bloodmarch by the psy chopomp Vivírula to find, protect, and bring the reborn god Wyrm back to the Congregation of the Serpent in the Alderlander capital of Brodelmarg. She is accompanied and guided by the Prophet Fangler. They do not know whether Wyrm is reborn in the form of a snake, a human, or something else, so they are not sure what to look for. However, Fangler’s mentalist spell TRUE SIGHT is always active as a prophetic ability. 

## SECRET GOALS 

If Wyrm turns out to have a form or temperament that could harm the Congregation of the Serpent, or falls into the wrong hands, Poansa has orders to kill him discreetly. To this end she carries a powerfully cursed obsidian dagger supposedly able to “kill sunlight, shadows, and the soul itself.” This dagger is either Glastooth (one of Horn’s Astra, page 66) without Poansa realizing it, or a replica. Poansa mistakenly believes that the dagger can only be used once, so she keeps it hidden until the right moment. 

If Poansa gets hold of Wyrm – that is, the Eighth Mother – she might keep her for herself and create a power base in the Bloodmarch with 

## **AD VIPERA** 

**==> picture [29 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

Ad Vipera is the Congregation of the Serpent’s secret police in Alderland. The name is actually an abbreviation of the organization’s maxim “Ad vipera, ad versa,” which translates roughly to “those who oppose the serpent are our opponents.” Due to the inflamed political situation that has left Alderland on the brink of civil war, Ad Vipera has a strong position in the country and is free to do what it wants, particularly in southern Alderland and the capital city of Brodelmarg. Their primary enemy is the Rust Church in northern Alderland. 

**==> picture [29 x 317] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 317] intentionally omitted <==**

Now that things are stirring in the Bloodmarch, the Congregation of the Serpent has become interested in the country. When the Prophet Fangler had a vision of Wyrm waiting to be reborn in Aslene they sent a strong force of Ad Vipera agents across the mountains to locate the god, eliminate any threats, and find allies in the country. 

**==> picture [186 x 27] intentionally omitted <==**

“the true snake god” in opposition to Alderland and with her as the god’s supreme priestess. In that case she is open to alliances which will be needed against the powerful enemies she would immediately make in her homeland.

## MEETING POANSA 

Poansa and her subordinates are already in Aslene. They crossed Gander’s Pass on secret paths from Alderland and continued to Oxengelder, where Poansa met with the Dreamstress as an ambassador of Alderland. She usually has a double representing her in Oxengelder, while she herself coordinates smaller groups secretly searching for Wyrm in the Bloodmarch. 

Poansa is sweeping the country from south to north. The adventurers may run into one of her spies before they even leave Taregyll, but their first encounter with Poansa herself will not take place north of the Wailing. She is often accompanied by the Prophet Fangler who believes that he can locate Wyrm if he gets close enough to the god’s new form. Poansa travels discreetly in a small group posing as a land surveyor in search of suitable locations for settlements in Aslene, equipped with basic surveying instruments and the knowledge required for such a mission. 

Poansa Moulter is mainly looking for information and is therefore open to conversations and collaboration with anyone she meets. If she suspects someone of withholding information or items of interest, she will try to have that person taken away and interrogated in private. 

## RELATIONS 

Poansa can accept help from anyone who offers it, but never loses sight of her own priorities and is only loyal to them. 

**THE DREAMSTRESS:** Poansa Moulter is loosely allied with the Dreamstress, who she hopes will be able to locate Wyrm through 

oneiromancy. Communication with the Dreamstress is mostly handled by her double, who is also called Poansa and like the original belongs to the Sisterhood of the Black Mamba. 

**GRUDENSTAAL:** If she decides to steal Wyrm for herself, Poansa may form an unholy alliance with Colonel Grudenstaal where the two of them become a couple and found a new sect that competes with their previous employers. 

**VINHELM:** If Poansa realizes what is really going on, she may join forces with Vinhelm Kaal and the other moon elves in the final stages of the campaign to hide the temple Agnostica forever. Then everything remains the same and the rumor of Wyrm’s rebirth was false, which might suit the Congregation of the Serpent. 

## **POANSA** 

**STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 5, WITS 4, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 3, Stealth 3, Move 2, Marksmanship 2, Lore 2, Insight 3, Manipulation 3 

**TALENTS:** Adaptive, Path of Poison 3, Sharp Tongue 2 

**GEAR:** Dagger, short sword, short bow, leather armor 

**IMMUNE TO POISON:** Like all members of the Sisterhood of the Black Mamba, Poansa is immune to poison. 

**SHED SKIN:** Poansa can shapeshift into a black mamba with Strength 1, Agility 7, Movement 2. In snake form she can move out of ARM’S LENGTH range without the enemy getting to attack. In close combat she has a bite with a lethal or sleeping poison (Potency 8) – an attack she can also use in human form.

## COLONEL GRUDENSTAAL 

Grudenstaal is colonel of the Ironbrows, an inquisitorial order of warriors within the Rust Church in Alderland, headquartered in Raskalma. Grudenstaal has for many years conducted purges of heretics in northern Alderland and sent many of them over the edge of the Iron Lock to live in exile in Ravenland. Through his agents the colonel has gained a good understanding of recent developments in Ravenland. 

After an indiscreet affair with Moilera of Kantamar, ducal consort and niece of Bretien the Procreator who was anointed king by the Rust Church in Alderland, Grudenstaal was left in the political cold. He has therefore been sent away to Aslene to investigate the rumors about the god Wyrm having been reborn there, as foretold by the Prophet Fangler. Grudenstaal was forced to accept the assignment, but he is bitter and feels that the Rust Church has betrayed him after many years of loyal service. 

Once Grudenstaal and his troop of eighty men had entered the Bloodmarch on secret paths near Gander’s Pass, the colonel made the bold move of openly seeking out the Caberians in Strilling, the most militant horse clan in Aslene, who spearheaded the conflict with Alderland as its mortal enemies. 

The risk of immediate slaughter loomed when the clan realized who their guests were, but Grudenstaal’s voice did not waver as he explained to Chieftain Merdekai that the long- standing peace had softened the warriors on both sides of Gander’s Pass. That was why he had come to the Bloodmarch – to find out whether the 

79 

**key players** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

pass could be cleared so the traditional, characterbuilding wars could be resumed. He turned to the Caberians thinking that they would understand his intentions better than anyone. 

When the initial astonishment had settled, a truce was signed to clear the battlefields of Hadruma for future bloodshed. Under the cover of this military endeavor, Grudenstaal and his men are looking for the reincarnation of Wyrm. 

## LEGEND 

_Oh yes, I’ve met Colonel Grudenstaal, the man they call the fist of the Ironbrows. A ruthless man to be sure, like when Holy Frensia was promised by the king – the old man feared the reprisal of the gods, you see – that she would never be exiled to Ravenland, and Grudenstaal kept the monarch’s word by personally kicking the seer over the edge of the precipice. But the man is more than just a hard heart. He listens to everybody and does not hesitate to change his mind if he likes your advice. He is certainly proud, so much so that he always goes his own way, and nobody knows what he might do. I suppose that’s why the powers that be in Alder­ land had him sent off to the Bloodmarch._ 

The colonel is a man of quick and bold action. He never takes his eyes off his goals, both overt and covert, and can make important decisions in an instant. He despises fear and thinks it is better to live and die gloriously with one’s head held high than to crawl through a life of doubt. 

## OVERT GOALS 

Grudenstaal claims that he has come to the Bloodmarch to open Gander’s Pass and prepare the ground for new character- building wars between Alderland and the horse clans. This is what the Caberians believe and want to achieve. If presented with proof that Grudenstaal has been lying, they could very well attack the Ironbrows. 

## SECRET GOALS 

The Rust Church in Alderland has sent Grudenstaal to the Bloodmarch to find the reborn Wyrm and kill the god before the Congregation of the Serpent gets hold of him. Grudenstaal has heard rumors that Wyrm can be found with the help of Horn’s Astra. He also understands how much these old weapons mean to the horse clans and could use them to buy their loyalty for a war against Alderland. 

## APPEARANCE 

Grudenstaal is in his mid- forties, tall and muscular, completely hairless, with the symbols of Rust and Heme tattooed around his head. He wears iron mail every day without exception – not just for protection, but as a religious uniform whose weight is meant to strengthen the body and mind of its wearer. Over the armor he wears a simple grayish- yellow robe. That way he does not attract as much attention, while at the same time making little effort to conceal himself. 

## MEETING GRUDENSTAAL 

After getting settled, the Ironbrows dispatch groups of seekers operating from Strilling. Grudenstaal is particularly keen to make contact with the dwarves of Firestead whom he thinks could melt the obstacles blocking Gander’s Pass into lava. Grudenstaal travels with groups of his own men, but rarely visits the country’s villages and taverns himself 

80 **chapter 8** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

– people of interest are instead brought to his camp, as guests or prisoners. 

in Ravenland. His only moral principle is to advance his own interests. 

## **COLONEL GRUDENSTAAL** 

## RELATIONS 

Grudenstaal keeps an open mind towards all the other key players, viewing them solely as potential opportunities or threats to his own interests. He judges creatures by their ability and could not care less about their sex or race. As a result there are several unexpected associates that he has recruited in the Bloodmarch. 

**POANSA:** Grudenstaal knows that Poansa of Ad Vipera has also come to the Bloodmarch in search of Wyrm. He used to have a mole inside Ad Vipera, but that agent was executed after being exposed by the Prophet Fangler’s magic. Poansa has in turn learned about Grudenstaal’s secret plans through her spies. 

**VINHELM:** Towards the end of the campaign, if Grudenstaal has realized the true nature of the situation, he might join forces with Vinhelm and the other moon elves to hide the temple Agnostica forever. In that case everything stays the same and the rumor of Wyrm’s rebirth turns out to be false, which might suit the Rust Church. 

**THE RUST CHURCH:** Feeling wronged and betrayed by the Rust Church, Grudenstaal might turn against them if the opportunity arises and try to establish his own power base in the Bloodmarch. He knows that many Alderlanders would join him. To this end he could basically contact and ally himself with any great power in Aslene, including Vaerefor van Reiben, Poansa, Vasnia, the Seventh, or the Rust Church 

## **STRENGTH 5, AGILITY 3, WITS 3, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Might 3, Melee 3, Animal Handling 2 **TALENTS:** Adaptive, Magnetism 2, Executioner 2, Defender 1, Horseback Fighter 3, Sword Fighter 2 

**GEAR:** Broadsword, large shield, pike, chainmail, war horse 

**LANCER:** Grudenstaal can wield his pike with one hand while on horseback. 

## **THE IRONBROWS** 

**==> picture [29 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

The Ironbrows is an order of warriors with the Rust Church in Alderland. Their traditional function has been to hunt down, capture, interrogate, and punish heretics, as well as brutally suppressing uprisings. When they learned about the prophecy of Wyrm’s rebirth in the Bloodmarch, the Rust Church sent a troop of Ironbrows to find and destroy the god before the Congregation of the Serpent can get their hands on him. The Ironbrows are proud and ruthless in nature, but realize that diplomacy is needed in Aslene and are trying to secure allies for the future, so they can be patient and even somewhat friendly with strangers whom they deem it useful.

## FIENA FROMELEI 

According to legend, halflings and goblins were once moon elves, with a moonstone in their chest in the same way that elves have a ruby for a heart. They tried to trick the raven god (or the snake god – the myths are unclear on this point) and as punishment the god tore the moonstone from their chest and split their kin into halflings and goblins. The moonstones were supposedly left somewhere in the Bloodmarch. 

A few months ago, the Ravenlander moon maiden and halfling Fiena Fromelei had a nighttime vision telling her that the time had come to find the lost moonstones and unite the two kin. Since then she can hear the moonstones calling from Aslene on moonlit nights and is therefore leading an expedition for the Penites, a cult centered around the inherited guilt of the halflings. More precisely, she can sense the direction and distance to all moonstones while the moon is up. 

She is escorted by a bodyguard of goblin wolf riders led by Crudehack, as well as three hired wolfkin warriors from Ravenland.

The priestess will seek forgiveness for some wrong committed by her kin at the time of the Shift, and will do so at the secret temple of the moon elves, although no one has ever seen it. Come to think of it, no one has even seen a moon elf, have they? So how she will_ 

## **THE PENITES** 

**==> picture [29 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

The Penites is a cult of penitent halflings from Ravenland. They worship the moon goddess Eor and strive to reunite the goblins and halflings, while at the same time atoning for the historical sin that according to legend divided their kin in the first place. The Penites believe that goblins and halflings were once “wholelings” – a noble kin of tall, nocturnal creatures who carried moonstones in their chests. 

**==> picture [29 x 330] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 330] intentionally omitted <==**

Egged on by the moon, they supposedly tried to steal the sun from the sky god Raven in order to plunge the world into eternal night, but the theft failed. The raven god tore the moonstones from the wholelings’ chests, splitting them into halflings and goblins, and hurled the stones over the mountains into the Bloodmarch. 

The Penites seek forgiveness for their kin’s original sin through penance, which may consist of anything from building temples and caring for the sick and poor to brutal self- flagellation in the sun. 

**==> picture [186 x 27] intentionally omitted <==**

_accomplish this is anyone’s guess. Personally, I’ve barely even seen a halfling. Perhaps it’s all made up? Might as well forget the whole thing!_

a moon elf herself (page 127). Vinhelm will immediately detect the presence and direction of a new moon elf in the Bloodmarch and hurry over there. As a moon elf Fiena can, like Salunia, still sense the direction to other lost moonstones.

However, she cannot sense the moonstones inside Agnostica until she obtains one of the lost stones at which point she transforms into 

Fiena has no secret goals. Once she becomes a moon elf, she will know the history of the moon elves and how to summon the temple Agnostica.

**==> picture [30 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

A fourth sits inside Vinhelm. You can place the stones anywhere you like, randomly or deliberately, but not too close to Shadowgate Pass. The remaining 36 moonstones are found in the temple Agnostica, seven of them inside the chests of living moon elves, excluding Vinhelm.

## MEETING FIENA 

Fiena Fromelei och hennes sällskap rör sig bara nattetid när månen är uppe, mer eller mindre i rät linje från Mistrapasset mot den närmaste månstenen som drar månjungfrun till sig. När den första stenen återfunnits vill Fiena i stället hitta templet Agnostica för att föra Blodslandets månstenar i säkerhet undan De sjunde. 

Om äventyrarna möter Fiena Fromeleis sällskap kommer de förmodligen bara att märka de tre ravländska vargfolkskrigarna och i andra hand de vargridande svartalferna. Sällskapet vill vara ifred, men är samtidigt intresserade av användbar information om landet och vad som ligger i deras väg. Om äventyrarna har en av månstenarna så kommer Fiena att söka upp dem för att försöka byta till sig stenen, alternativt stjäla den. 

**VINHELM:** Vinhelm has heard that a group of halflings and goblins has come to the Bloodmarch to find the moonstones, and is keen to see them fail. But he does not know where the group or the lost moonstone are. As soon as Fiena or Salunia turns into a moon elf, he will sense their presence and rush over there before the Seventh get to them. He then wants her help in finding the rest of the lost moonstones, so that he can take them and the new moon elf into hiding at Agnostica. 

**THE SEVENTH:** The Seventh know that a group of halfling priests are on their way to the Bloodmarch in search of the moonstones, and are pursuing them. The worm people are not interested in the group itself, but only to get hold of a moon elf through whom they can find the temple Agnostica where the Eighth Mother is said to be held. One of Fiena’s wolfkin warriors has been taken over by a worm hiding in the fur on the back of its neck, capable of calling the Seventh. They know where the warrior is at all times. 

**GRUDENSTAAL AND POANSA:** In an emergency, Fiena can seek refuge with Grudenstaal or Poansa if she knows that they too are searching for Agnostica. In that case she may offer them “Wyrm reborn” in exchange for protection as well as the moonstones.

## THE SEVENTH 

The Seventh are worm creatures – the remains of the people who were once masters of humankind on the continent of Anoma beyond the sea, east of Ravenland. Each creature consists of roughly one hundred, foot- long, flesh- colored worms gathered in a colony around the skeleton of some creature they devoured. 

Seven worm creatures have recently arrived in Aslene. They are the last of the seventh generation of worms, hence “the Seventh.” You can read more about their background on page 4. Separately 

the worms are no smarter than animals, but together they become both intelligent and strong. 

## **MEAT** 

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

_“May his flesh awaken in your flesh!”_ 

The worm people worship Meat, a god they believe is resting inside all living things, waiting to be born. Meat will emerge along with the twelfth generation of worms, at which time everything living will be gathered inside the god’s body. According to the Seventh, individuals and kin are only passing phases of development, just as their own figures consist of individual worms who are worthless on their own. For the worm people their search for the Eighth Mother is a search for the meaning of life itself. The next generation on the path to Meat – the Eighth – can only be birthed by her. 

**==> picture [24 x 213] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [186 x 27] intentionally omitted <==**

85 

**key players** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## LEGEND 

_I’ve been told that a handful of stern­ looking priests recently arrived in Ravenland from beyond the seas, having made the same journey as the humans who first landed on our shores. The priests are on a mission from the snake god to make sure that the humans honor the god properly. Apparently no one can endure the sight of their faces, which is why they’re all wearing masks._ 

_The priests are said to be carrying strong shack­ les on their belts, with orders to once more enslave the humans if they have not proven worthy of the freedom they received as a test from the god. They leave the skeletons of the tried and rejected in their wake, but cannot die themselves. When these terri­ ble judges heard of Fangler’s prophecy, that Wyrm_ 

_had been reborn in Aslene, they headed west to worship the god in his new form and pronounce their final judgment on humanity. If you cross their path with an impure mind, beware._ 

## APPEARANCE 

The assembled form of the worm creatures roughly takes the shape of the skeleton they use. In human form they look like a human corroded by fleshsoot, so they wear clothing that fully covers their bodies and face masks in order to blend in. In animal form they do not care about their appearance and look like a skinned version of the animal or monster whose skeleton they inhabit. 

## **THE SEVENTH’S FORMS** 

At the start of the game, the Seventh act in seven collectives of one hundred demonic worms each, gathered around seven human skeletons. They can split into smaller groups or merge around larger skeletons if the opportunity arises and it serves their purposes. See the table below. The Seventh count as monsters and all forms use the same monster attacks. The forms have different attribute values depending on the size of the skeleton and the number of worms it contains. 

**==> picture [342 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

**----- Start of picture text -----**<br>
#WORMS FORM STRENGTH AGILITY WITS ATTACK DAMAGE TYPE<br> 10 Cat 2 5 1 Claw 1 Slash<br> 50 Wolf 4 4 3 Bite 1 Slash<br>100 Human 8 3 5 Weapon Varies Varies<br>350 Horse 16* 2 6 Kick 1 Blunt<br>700 Giant 32* 1 7 Tree trunk 2 Blunt<br>**----- End of picture text -----**<br>


* Attacks with twelve Base Dice. 

**IMMORTAL:** The Seventh exist in multiple worlds at once and are virtually immortal as long as Octomadria (page 99) is alive. Until the Eighth Mother is eliminated, they can only be defeated temporarily. Even if seemingly obliterated, the Seventh regenerate in D6 days. 

86 

**chapter 8** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The colony can switch to a new skeleton if one of their worms finds its way to the back of a sleeping creature’s neck and paralyzes it. Then the colony devours the victim and replaces its soft tissue with themselves. The process takes one day. Discarded skeletons are stripped so clean that they almost look carved. For example, the colony can take over a wolf in order to run fast, or a large bird or fish in order to fly or swim. 

The Seventh have collective thoughts so that they all immediately know what one of them knows. If they manage to take over a creature by placing a worm on the back of its neck, everyone immediately learns what the victim knows, but the worms lack a fundamental understanding of other creatures and can therefore misinterpret information or overlook important details. 

## **THE SEVENTH’S MONSTER ATTACKS** 

## **D6 ATTACK** 

- 1 **GATHER STRENGTH!** The Seventh gather themselves and draw power from their otherworldly origin, thereby recovering D6 Strength. 

- 2 **WEAPON ATTACK!** The Seventh perform a weapon attack in their basic form. For damage and type, see the table on the previous page. 

- 3 **PSYCHIC SHOCKWAVE!** The Seventh send out a psychic shockwave, which is a fear attack with nine Base Dice that targets everyone within NEAR range. 

- 4 **PARALYZING WORM BITE!** The Seventh throw some of their worms at everyone within NEAR range, and unless the victims succeed with a MOVE roll (not an action), a worm attaches itself to their skin. Victims affected by the worm’s mucus lose all their actions in the next combat round, but can still function normally in the current round. Then the worm falls off and the victim can act as usual. 

- 5 **CONTROLLING WORM BITE!** The Seventh throw some of their worms at everyone within NEAR range, and unless the victims succeed with a MOVE roll (not an action), several worms attach themselves to their skin. Victims affected by the worms’ mucus fall under the power of the Seventh, who control their every action until the victims succeed with an INSIGHT roll. Victims can make an INSIGHT roll once per round, on the Seventh’s turn. The roll cannot be pushed. Through this attack the Seventh also learn everything the creature knows about the Bloodmarch, the key players, and Horn’s Astra. If the same victim is hit by this attack a second time, the effect is the same as result 6. 

- 6 **TAKE OVER SKELETON!** A swarm of worms hit a victim, who suffers an attack with eight Base Dice and damage 1. The victim’s armor is reduced by half (rounded down) as the worms wriggle under it. If the victim takes damage from the attack, they automatically sustain a critical injury (stab wound). Each subsequent round the Seventh automatically perform an attack on the victim, on the Seventh’s initiative. The attacks continue until the Seventh are defeated or the victim dies. In the latter case, the Seventh can choose to take over the victim’s skeleton. 

87 

**key players** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The Seventh have no emotions and no concept of good and evil. They are entirely practical creatures and will do anything to further the dominance and prosperity of their kind, including forming alliances. Furthermore, they are highly resistant to physical violence as well as magic and need neither sleep nor rest. 

## OVERT GOALS 

The Seventh have no overt goals per se. They see no reason to talk about their intentions – they simply get on with it. They are not secretive, just quiet about their activities. 

## MEETING THE SEVENTH 

The Seventh arrive in Aslene from Ravenland through Shadowgate Pass around the same time as the adventurers, and then go looking for moon elves and by extension the Eighth Mother. The adventurers could encounter these unpleasant creatures early in the campaign, but any alliances or confrontations should be dealt with at a later stage. 

Among humans, the Seventh pretend to be ill with skin rot and claim to have come to Aslene in search of a demon prince whom they heard would be able to cure them. They are interested in receiving information and assistance, and can help their allies in return. 

## SECRET GOALS 

The Seventh want to find the Eighth Mother and through her spawn an eighth generation of worms. They themselves will die in the process, which does not bother them. If they fail their kind will eventually go extinct anyway. 

They know that moon elves inherit the memories of their kin and want to get hold of one in order to find out where the Eighth Mother is hidden. The worm creatures’ ultimate goal is to reach generation twelve and thereby give shape to their god Meat. This, they believe, is end of history and the meaning of everything. 

If the Seventh get their hands on the Eighth Mother, they will produce the Eighth and later try to conquer Aslene, Alderland, and Ravenland by enslaving humans and other kin. They may form alliances with others, but always consider themselves superior to everyone else. 

## THE SEVENTH AND OTHER KEY PLAYERS 

The worm people have no moral or emotional barriers and can forge and break alliances if it furthers their cause. Once they understand that others are looking for Horn’s Astra, they might try to find them as well and later use them as a bargaining chip. They soon realize that others are seeking the Eighth Mother under the name “Wyrm Reborn,” and they will happily play along to gain the seekers’ trust. 

**VAEREFOR:** Vaerefor van Reiben might convince the Seventh that the sticky vegetation of the crimson forest is a precursor to the god Meat and form an alliance with them. Both sides look after their own interests and will break the alliance when it is beneficial to do so. 

88 **chapter 8** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## VINHELM KAAL 

## LEGEND 

None. Vinhelm is largely unknown and wants to remain that way. 

The moon elf Vinhelm was a brilliant but reckless young mentalist at the hidden moon temple of Agnostica. His talents were encouraged by the elders until he started experimenting with narcotic mushrooms as a way of discovering new spells and insights. 

During a fateful dream session a few months ago, Vinhelm made contact with three unknown consciousnesses and accidentally let them know how Agnostica hides the moonstones and the Eighth Mother, whom some interpret as Wyrm Reborn. 

## APPEARANCE 

If one manages to catch sight of him, Vinhelm looks like a bald and somewhat chubby fourteen- year- old boy with mottled gray skin. The skin reflects light, so he avoids all light sources and wraps himself in covering garments. The reflection does not really go away even when he casts VISAGE . 

Unbeknownst to Vinhelm, these consciousnesses belong to the Prophet Fangler from Alderland, the moon maiden Fiena Fromelei from Ravenland, and worst of all – the Seventh from beyond the seas, the same worm creatures from whom the moon elves stole the Eighth Mother and fled twelve hundred years ago. 

The seven elder moon elves were both furious and devastated when they learned what had happened, as their entire lives since the escape had been focused on staying hidden. They blocked parts of Vinhelm’s memory and cast him into the world to make things right. All knowledge of Agnostica, the moonstones, and the Eighth Mother must be eradicated! 

Vinhelm has been traveling the Bloodmarch ever since, accompanied by the thief Orehanne and the druid Sisella, in an attempt to salvage the situation. He is friends with the Dreamstress and has, as the genius his is, quickly learned the art of oneiromancy. Moon elves normally inherit their ancestors’ memories, but since some of these have been blocked, Vinhelm feels a bit empty and lost. 

89 

**key players** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## OVERT GOALS 

Vinhelm is highly secretive in all things and prefers not to talk about his plans or goals at all. 

## SECRET GOALS 

Vinhelm’s objective is to keep the temple Agnostica hidden at all cost, and preferably erase any clues and all knowledge about the temple, the moonstones, and the Eighth Mother. If this is not possible, he will try to lead all seekers astray. As a last resort he will use violence, most likely by proxy. 

Once Fiena Fromelei becomes a moon elf, Vinhelm will seek her out and with her help try to locate the rest of the lost moonstones. He then intends to bring her, himself, and the moonstones safely to Agnostica, before letting the temple vanish forever. 

## MEETING VINHELM 

At the start of the game, Vinhelm and his companions are traveling the Bloodmarch to identify and eliminate people interested in Agnostica, moonstones, and the Eighth Mother (or Wyrm Reborn). They have built a fake temple as a trap. 

They can be encountered anywhere and are keen to get people to talk. Vinhelm knows every language spoken in Aslene and can appear in virtually any guise he chooses by casting the VISAGE spell. Through Agnostica he has access to endless amounts of gold. He can communicate with the mother temple by raising an altar under the moon and exchange information 

and items on that altar. He can only return to Agnostica if he has at least three moonstones (including his own). 

Vinhelm wields powerful magic in the form of mentalism and oneiromancy. The adventurers will likely notice his presence without seeing him or quite understanding what is happening. Someone might think that they met a stranger, but have no memory of it whatsoever. The adventurers might start to think each other’s thoughts as they relax, or see a glowing moon face that immediately vanishes without a trace. 

## RELATIONS 

The moon elves hoped that the worm people had died out over the past 1200 years, and experience a collective shock when they realize that the Seventh have come to the Bloodmarch to hunt them down. 

The moon elves’ first priority is to keep away from everyone else, but if the Seventh are closing in on them, they are willing to collaborate with other powers as a last resort. They have not been able to kill the Eighth Mother, but their hope is rekindled if they learn about Poansa’s magical dagger. 

## **VINHELM KAAL** 

**STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 4, WITS 5, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 3, Move 2, Lore 3, Insight 3, Manipulation 3 

**TALENTS:** Inner Peace, Mentalism 3, Sword Fighter 2, Oneiromancy 2 

**GEAR:** Broadsword, large shield, chainmail
