## KONANA 

When the horse clans first rode into Aslene from the west, the land was dominated by the Vasnians, a human people with some orc blood in their veins. Bloody battles were fought for many years until the Vasnians fled to the outskirts of the country which were of no interest to the clans. There they remain to this day, dreaming of reclaiming their homeland. 

When the demons poured into Aslene through Shadowgate Pass three hundred years ago, the Vasnians were overjoyed to see the horse clans slaughtered by 

monsters. However, it soon became clear that the demons did not discriminate between human peoples, but attacked anyone they could get their hands on. The Vasnians were once again forced to take refuge in the mountains, where they watched with horror the depredations of the invaders. 

Now something has happened. The demons are growing fewer and new outsiders are entering the country. At the same time a capable leader has emerged named Konana. She has heard that various powers are seeking the same hateful weapons that the horse clans once used to drive her people from their homes. Realizing that Horn’s Astra comes with great power, Konana wants them for herself. 

The orcs and Vasnians of Aslene believe that their people were the country’s original inhabitants. They do not think of orcs and humans as different kin, but see “orcishness” as a sign of possessing honorable qualities. Konana is regarded as the embodiment of those ideals combined with human cunning and agility. She is therefore held in high esteem. 

91 

**key players** 

## LEGEND 

_Konana is the salt of the mountains. The blood of the country flows through her veins with the power of the ancients. She is the coming of spring after a bitter winter, the ray of sunlight that cuts like a sharp blade through the fleeing darkness of the storm. In her I put my trust, for she will lead the Vasnians back to our stolen plains, break the legs of the horses, and cave in the skulls of their riders._ 

## APPEARANCE 

Konana is a tall and heavy- set woman with short ash- blonde hair, dressed in simple but effective soldier’s attire. She knows how to use a sword and shield, and also carries a crossbow on her back. She is almost a full- blooded orc and accepted as one by the Aslene orcs based on her scent, but she can just as well pass for a human among humans, though her canines are just a little too long and sharp. 

## OVERT GOALS 

Officially, Konana presents herself as a professional warrior without scruples, willing to take on missions for the highest bidder. 

## SECRET GOALS 

Konana is secretly trying to find Horn’s Astra and bring them to Vasnia. She is also looking for a group of orcs from Ravenland – it is the Urhur clan who has sent an envoy to propose an alliance. The collaboration is hampered by the fact that Aslene orcs traditionally despise their fellow kin in Ravenland ever since they allowed themselves to be enslaved by elves and dwarves. 

## MEETING KONANA 

Konana is based in Vasnia in northern Aslene. She and her closest adjutants are posing as mercenaries 

who have accidentally blown in from the Drawn Lands in the west. Stranded in Aslene, they are now looking for work. In reality they are seeking Horn’s Astra. They know nothing about the Drawn Lands and make things up when necessary. 

## RELATIONS 

Konana can collaborate with anyone she thinks could be useful in the restoration of Vasnia, including the Seventh. However, she is reluctant to hand over Horn’s Astra and would rather keep them as a trump card. Konana will never work with the horse clans or their allies – too much blood has been spilled between them for that to be an option.

## VAEREFOR VAN REIBEN 

Vaerefor appears in the guise of an elven druid, but is really one of the immortal demon princes left behind in Aslene when Zygofer 

sealed his nexus 300 years 

ago. Vaerefor does not long to get back to his homeworld Harachne and would quite like living in Aslene if there were not so many colors. 

In the lush jungles of Harachne everything is red, and Vaerefor’s plan is to rule Aslene and its neighboring countries, while rearranging them to his taste. For many human generations he has been cultivating and adjusting plants from Harachne so they can finally grow and branch out in the human world as well. Now he is spreading them over larger and larger areas. 

Vaerefor has tricked the elves of Aslene into believing that he is a prophet of the Red Wanderer, the star from which the elves once fell into the world in the form of rubies. Now the elves think that the demonic red vegetation is primordial and must be spread throughout the world under Vaerefor’s leadership. Vaerefor claims that the Red Wanderer is synonymous with the god Rubor. 

## LEGEND 

_I once saw the lord of the red elves with my own eyes. He stood on top of a giant slug that crawled its way through the pass where cousin Welia and I were picking bird’s eggs on the mountainside. Vaere­ for van Reiben they call him, and that face of his might as well be made of bark. They say he lives among the growths of the crimson for­ ests – the ones that have gotten bigger lately, as you’ve prob­ ably heard. I believe he has something to do with it!_ 

93 

**key players** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## **RUBOR AND KOLOR** 

Rubor is another name for the red wanderer of the sky – the origin of elvenkind. In Aslene the demon prince Vaerefor van Reiben has created a religion around Rubor, the red god. Rubor’s counter- force, Kolor, stands for evil and is represented by all colors other than red (white and black do not count as colors in this context). 

Vaerefor presents himself as an ancient elf and claims to be Rubor’s prophet on earth. The elves of Aslene have allowed themselves to be duped and follow him blindly. According to Vaerefor, the sky god Rubor sowed the earth with red seeds to cover the world in crimson forest where he could rest in between his wanderings. 

But Kolor, the evil colorer, tainted his creation and twisted it into one of multiple colors. The multicolored 

world is called Polychromia, and it is the task of the red elves to turn it back into Monochromia. Until this task is completed Rubor will wander restlessly across the sky, but once the whole world has turned crimson and Kolor has been defeated, the red god will descend onto the earth and live among his true believers in everlasting love, joy, and harmony. 

Until that day, hate, duty, and cleansing will reign, with the red elves of Aslene as the god’s instruments. The red elves are suspicious of everyone else, especially those who add other colors than red to the world. Such creatures are the evil henchmen of Kolor, whose worldly manifestation is the rainbow. Red elves are sworn enemies of the goddess Have with her blue tar and blue fire. 

## APPEARANCE 

Vaerefor has assumed the form of a lanky elf of indeterminate age, with red skin and eyes. He is often in an excellent mood and likes to throatsing to a strange, serpentine dance which he is happy to perform in front of others. 

His skin is furrowed and his hair consists of grass, leaves, and vines, which could mean that Vaerefor is part plant. He himself claims to 

have chosen this appearance because he serves all living things on behalf of the Red Wanderer. Vaerefor has even formed a ruby in his chest, modeled after the elven ones. 

Vaerefor van Reiben dresses in luxurious but practical garments and often wears red glasses to avoid the risk of seeing other colors, as they make him nauseous, particularly outside the crimson forests. His servants 

94 **chapter 8** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

always carry spray bottles with red paint so that they will quickly be able to cover other offensive colors. 

The adventurers are unlikely to encounter Vaerefor outside the demonic vegetation. To find the demon, they will probably need elves to take them to him. However, he does show up in the adventure site Salterstay. 

## OVERT GOALS 

Vaerefor’s stated objective is to cover Aslene and eventually the entire world in demonic red vegetation according to the Red Wanderer’s divine plan. Nature, in its present condition, has been corrupted by Kolor, the evil colorer. All colors other than red must be suppressed and erased. Only when the world deserves it will the sunlight shine clear and red once more. 

## SECRET GOALS 

In reality, Vaerefor just wants to rule a world that is pleasant to him; a red world covered in vegetation from Harachne. He is an aesthete through and through. He would like to get his hands on one of Horn’s Astra – the one called the Goblet of Blood (the Goblet Staff of Have) – as it would help him achieve his goals. 

## RELATIONS 

Vaerefor can form alliances with other key players as long as they accept him as their superior and support his plans to spread the forests of Rubor throughout the world as the god has mandated. He also realizes that he needs troops in order to conquer larger territories such as Ravenland and Alderland. If anyone accepts this, he is happy to help them in return. But he steers clear of the horse clans, knowing that an alliance with them would never last. 

Vaerefor is friends with the Dreamstress. 

Vaerefor can convince the Seventh that the Crimson Forest is a precursor to the god Meat and thus form an alliance with them. 

## **VAEREFOR VAN REIBEN** 

**STRENGTH 9, AGILITY 5, WITS 4, EMPATHY 5** 

## MEETING VAEREFOR 

Vaerefor is often found in areas with crimson forests, lands which the red elves collectively refer to as Reiben. He can leave the demonic vegetation in an emergency, but not without disgust and only if it is absolutely necessary. 

If he needs to contact anyone, Vaerefor usually sends elves as messengers. It is said that he spends most of his time in the living, moving castle of Kasabatoda. Now and then he visits the Dreamstress in Oxengelder who dreams her friend to and from her temple. 

**SKILLS :** Insight 4, Manipulation 4 

**REGENERATION:** Recovers D3 Strength per round. 

**SYMBIOTIC:** Vaerefor’s home is the giant snail Kasabatoda with a castle on its back. The two of them are really a single creature who has placed its intelligence in Vaerefor. If Vaerefor dies, he can be re- created by having the ruby inside him pass through the giant snail. Should this be impossible, the snail will assume Vaerefor’s face, huge and twisted, and run amok to devour all living things in the world and turn them into crimson forest through its digestive system.

sits cross- legged or lies on her side to sleep and dream. She always has her eyes half- closed and her lips curved in a serene little smile. The Dreamstress’ skin is bronze, as is the hair on her head which would reach her waist if she did not arrange it in such an elaborate style. Her massive body is wrapped in multicolored fabrics with glittering metal ornaments and gems given to her by her visitors. 

## THE DREAMSTRESS 

The Dreamstress is giant demon of philosophical disposition who founded oneiromancy – dream magic. She has built a beautiful temple with a dream tower in Oxengelder where pupils of different kin come to learn oneiromancy. The Dreamstress’ temple is one of the most peaceful and revered sites in Aslene – perhaps the one place its people are truly proud of. 

## OVERT GOALS 

The Dreamstress has no real enemies. She does not act or take sides in political issues, but treats everyone she meets with kindness, speaking to them and offering advice based on their own goals. She has a reputation for being completely trustworthy and never betraying a confidence. 

The Dreamstress says she wants to help people understand themselves and take control of their thoughts and dreams by visualizing them. She claims that dreams are the soil from which reality grows and that nothing becomes real without first being dreamed. The world, as she sees it, is simply the sleeping vision of the Worlddreamer. Life itself is made up of aspirational dreams and the results that come from them. 

## LEGEND 

_Your dog ran away, eh? Well then, you should see the Dreamstress in Oxengelder who knows where everything is and where everyone’s gone. There, in the limestone region just below the mountains, she runs a school for dreamers. They say the Dream­ stress is a giant, bigger than a house but milder than cream, teaching the so­ inclined how to galder dream, so that the dreams inform them about the world and can even alter things that displease them. But me, I prefer a good night’s sleep and to leave the world as it is._ 

## SECRET GOALS 

The Dreamstress came to Aslene during the Demon Flood, and all she really wants is to get back to her homeworld Eoni. She wants to gather and train the dreamers required to dream this into reality. While teaching them she plants insidious psychic messages that will give her complete control over them when the time comes to collectively dream her away forever. 

The Dreamstress hopes to use her contacts to obtain one or more of Horn’s Astra. She will then dissolve them and charge her dream tower with their power when the day comes to 

## APPEARANCE 

The Dreamstress looks human but is fifteen meters tall standing up, though she usually

dream herself away. Perhaps the Dreamstress’ escape from the world turns out to be a dream. As a backup plan she would be willing to rule this world with Vaerefor and spend her time dreaming beauty into existence. 

## MEETING THE DREAMSTRESS 

The adventurers can visit the Dreamstress in Oxengelder, but she could also show up in a random encounter when she has dreamed herself to somewhere else in Aslene. She then invites interested adventurers to her temple to teach them oneiromancy. 

## RELATIONS 

Several key players have been in contact with the Dreamstress and she treats them all with equal kindness, but does not take sides one way or the other. 

Only Vaerefor van Reiben knows about the Dreamstress’ true plans. They are friends and have no quarrel with each other. The Dreamstress can sometimes dream Vaerefor to and - from her temple, in which case she dresser her self entirely in red to honor her guest.

**STRENGTH 50, AGILITY 2, WITS 4, EMPATHY 4 MOVEMENT:** 1 **ARMOR RATING:** 4 

## **MONSTER ATTACKS** 

- **D6 ATTACK** 

- 1 **CRUSHING BLOW!** The Dreamstress swings her fist at an unfortunate adventurer. Roll for the attack with twelve Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). If hit, the victim is hurled to the ground and rendered prone. 

- 2 **NIGHTMARE ATTACK!** The Dreamstress unleashes nightmares upon all adventures within NEAR range. Roll a fear attack with eight Base Dice. 

- 3 **STOMP ATTACK!** The Dreamstress tries to stomp two adventurers within NEAR range. Roll for the attacks with ten Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). Everyone hit by the attack is knocked down and rendered prone. 

- 4 **SWEEPING BLOW!** The Dreamstress swings her arms at all adventurers within NEAR range. Roll for the attack with nine Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2. Everyone hit by the attack is knocked to the ground and rendered prone. 

- 5 **THROW!** The Dreamstress has had enough of an adventurer within NEAR range and tries to throw them away. Roll for the attack with eleven Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. If hit, the victim is thrown to SHORT range and lands prone. 

- 6 **WHIRL ATTACK!** The Dreamstress tries to stomp and smash the adventurers with her feet and fists in a frenzy of swift blows. Everyone within NEAR range suffers an attack with ten Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). Anyone hit by the attack is knocked prone.

## OCTOMADRIA – THE EIGHTH MOTHER 

Octomadria – the Eighth Mother – is the would- be queen of the Seventh, the worm people from Anoma, and was supposed to birth the eighth generation of worm creatures on their way to becoming the god Meat. How the Eighth Mother fits into the final stage of the campaign becomes clear at the adventure site Agnostica. 

## LEGEND 

_Did you hear the rumor that Wyrm will be reborn in Aslene? The Prophet Fangler in Alderland, that fellow with the all­ seeing third eye, supposedly had a clear­ cut vision from the gods. Mark my words, this will only bring calamity upon us. They say the Congregation of the Serpent has already sent troops to Aslene to receive their god and that Bretien the Procreator is mobilizing a defense in Kantamar. Calamity, I say, and calamity there will be! It’s best when things return to normal and all that’s turned upside down is the soil under the farmer’s plow._ 

killed the moment they come into contact with any of Horn’s Astra. 

Octomadria’s final form appears as a twelvemeter- long snake that ends in a women’s torso and head with regal posture. She is completely naked, but demands to be dressed in elegant clothes. If no one has any clothes for her, she shuts her eyes and projects exotic garments and jewelry worthy of an empress or goddess. 

## OVERT GOALS 

The Eighth Mother’s six intermediate forms all have a burning desire to know who they are. They have no memory and are stressed, frightened, and aggressive. 

In her final form, Octomadria views herself as an obvious goddess. She does not consider herself a leader, since everything and everyone around her is unworthy of divine leadership, and wants to be worshiped the way she deserves. She will enter whatever alliance seems like the quickest route to this exalted position. She will happily let someone else govern the country as long as she is treated as a supreme being, with all that entails. 

## SECRET GOALS 

## APPEARANCE 

The Eighth Mother’s first form resembles a three- meter- long snake with the upper body of a small child. Her six subsequent forms appear in turn as twisted women with similar features, of the following kin: human, dwarf, orc, goblin, wolfkin, and moon elf. Each form that dies is replaced by the next. All forms have the same stats, and they are 

Octomadria has no secret goals. As a god she has no reason to lie, pretend, or manipulate, since that would imply that others are her equals. 

## MEETING OCTOMADRIA 

Octomadria does not appear until the final stage of the campaign, when the adventurers open her chamber in the moon elves’ temple Agnostica.

Only after going through her first seven forms does she become aware of who she is and enter her final form. If the encounter plays out peacefully, Octomadria will go through her different forms over the course of a day and reach her final form without bloodshed. 

Octomadria must be killed eight times – one for each form. The final form of Octomadria can only be harmed by the dagger Glasstooth (page 66).

## **THE FINAL FORM’S MONSTER ATTACKS** 

- **D6 ATTACK** 

- 1 **SUMMON FORMS!** Octomadria summons her first seven forms who move towards an enemy within NEAR range and attack from ARM’S LENGTH range. Her forms can only exist one at a time, each emerging when the previous form is defeated. Repeating this monster attack while a form still exists summons a new creature which contains the slain forms from the previous summoning. 

- 2 **WHIRLING BLADES!** Octomadria grows eight arms, each holding a sword. She attacks all enemies within NEAR range with nine Base Dice and Damage 2 (slash wound). Roll one attack for each enemy. 

- 3 **POISON SHOWER!** Octomadria showers everyone within NEAR range with poison. Roll an attack with eight Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (nontypical) against each victim. Victims damaged by the attack also suffer the effect of a paralyzing poison with Potency 5. Any of Octomadria’s summoned forms who are within range of the attack are killed instantly. 

- 4 **TAIL ATTACK!** Octomadria uses her tail to whip an enemy within NEAR range. Roll for the attack with twelve Base Dice and Damage 1 (blunt force). Any creatures smaller than her who are hit by the attack are hurled to an adjacent zone, space permitting, and land prone. 

- 5 **LETHAL EMBRACE!** Octomadria wraps herself around a victim and tries to crush them. Roll for the attack with ten Base Dice – the effectiveness of armor is reduced by half, exception for the Stonechest of Horn which provides full protection. If the attack inflicts damage, the victim remains in Octomadria’s grip and the attack is automatically repeated in the next round on Octomadria’s initiative. A victim Broken by this attack is crushed to death. The attack continues until either the victim or Octomadria is dead. While the attack is active, Octomadria can also perform an attack with six Base Dice, Damage 1, at an enemy within ARM’S LENGTH range. 

- 6 **DIVINE JUDGMENT!** Octomadria invokes her status as a god and condemns an enemy within SHORT range to death. This person must succeed with a roll for their best attribute or disintegrate into a pile of dust. The victim may add one D6 to the roll for each of Horn’s Astra they carry.

## SMOLDERER 

Smolderers are small, dark gray, humanoid creatures with glowing eyes who have thus far only been found in ashlands. Their warm bodies give off faint wisps of smoke and smell of sulfur, but are still cold to the touch, since they draw heat from their surroundings. 

Some scholars claim that they are related to whiners, which others dismiss as unfounded 

speculation. The two creatures are indeed of similar size and shape, but that is where the similarity 

ends. The smolderers are believed to be intelligent, and the wheezing laughter one sometimes hears when they show up is most likely their language. 

The fact of the matter is that smolderers are not at all related to the other kin of the Forbidden Lands, but sprung from the depths of the earth instead. They live off heat and may attack all warm- blooded creatures to suck up their body heat – not because they need it, since the heat of the earth is quite sufficient, but living warmth is clearly considered a delicacy among their kind. 

Smolderers tend to attack in great hordes, often riding on the backs of ktones (page 110). If they take damage, they disappear into the ashland and do not return. A killed smolderer disintegrates into ashes. 

## **SMOLDERER** 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 1, AGILITY 3, WITS 2, EMPATHY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 3, Move 2, Scouting 3 

**MOVEMENT:** 1 

**GEAR:** Short spear 

**SULFURIC ODOR:** Smolderers reek of sulfur, which makes them unpleasant to be around. An adventurer who finishes their turn within ARM’S LENGTH range of a smolderer suffers an attack with three Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. The attack deals damage to Empathy. 

**DRAW HEAT:** Whenever smolderers succeed with an unarmed melee attack, they can (in addition to inflicting damage) drain their victims’ body heat and thereby render them COLD

## BLOODBEECH 

In the outskirts of the Crimson Forest grow the bloodbeeches – large red trees with beautiful white flowers. But their beauty is deceptive. When their flowers detect the presence of warm- blooded creatures, they flap their petals so that they come off and fly down to attack and draw blood. Each 

flower requires a few drops of blood to sustain their seed production. The bloodbeech seems to be aware of how many flowers it has lost and only sends a suitable amount of them. That way the same tree can attack again if more creatures approach.

## **MOVEMENT:** —

## PRUNE 

Prunes are red elves whom the priests of Ashenstead have sought to “reform” through anatomical procedures and the pumping of blue tar into their bodies. The results are scrawny, blue- black creatures who wander around doing what they are told. Prunes have varying levels of consciousness, but whatever consciousness they have left is filled with hatred towards their creators and the world in general. 

Some prunes have escaped or been released, probably to mock or intimidate the red elves, and can therefore be found in the Bloodmarch near Ashenstead. Elves from Ravenland are hunting them to cut out the rubies still embedded in their chests and thereby recreate the original elves in the Stillmist in Ravenland. 

## **PRUNE** 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 2, WITS 1, EMPATHY 1 MOVEMENT:** 1 

**SKILLS:** Scouting 1 **ARMOR RATING:** — 

**EXPLOSIVE:** Prunes explode on contact with fire. The prune is consumed by the flames and detonates in a cascade of burning blue tar. Roll for the attack with eight Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 against all creatures within NEAR range.

## WINGSTEED 

On rare occasions a horse in the Wailing and Kreysel, almost always a gray one, is born with wings. The Sabirians value these horses immensely and view them as gifts from the goddess Wail, sent as atonement for betraying the horse clans when they needed her the most during Sella’s military campaign. 

offspring were all born wingless. Mommodar is considered too old and fat to ride the horse, and some believe that Barganos should be left in more suitable hands. 

The Sabirian wingsteeds can take flight upwind with a few powerful wingbeats, but otherwise they ride the winds like eagles and rely on updrafts to gain altitude. They cannot carry out precise maneuvers or fight in the air, and will fall if they stop moving. Their ability to fly is therefore mainly used for exhibitions, delivering messages, and as transportation over obstacles on the ground. They move as well on the ground as any other horse. 

The most famous of the twenty wingsteeds currently found in the Bloodmarch is the stallion Barganos, owned by the Sabirian chieftain Mommodar the Gnawer. Mommodar has tried to breed more wingsteeds, but Barganos’

## FRAEGE 

106 **chapter 9** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

Fraeges are heat- stealing ice demons who always attack the warmest target around – first lamps, torches, and campfires, then warm bodies. They tend to hide in dense vegetation, but their cold can be felt even a few meters away. 

Some scholars claim that fraeges are demon- tainted smolderers, and both creatures do feed on heat, but fraeges are much bigger and more dangerous and sink their teeth into victims to drain their warmth. They can freeze and paralyze limbs with their grip and quickly kill a human with their embrace. Furthermore, they grow in strength after 

absorbing heat and will also split into two once they reach a certain size.

## GRAVE LILY 

The Crimson Forests of the Bloodmarch are not only home to carnivorous beasts, but also to predatory plants. One of the more dangerous varieties is the Grave Lily, which some believe to be a cross between plant and insect. The Grave Lily feeds on animals it catches and dissolves in the corrosive liquid produced in its flower- cups. 

108 **chapter 9** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The Grave Lily can move around on four insect- like legs, but usually stands completely still, sometimes in clusters, waiting for prey with its tentacles raised. When something comes near, the Grave Lily catches its prey with the tentacles whose thorns secrete a paralyzing poison. Once the prey stops struggling, the flower pushes it into its flower- cups using the hand- like claws. The thorns can also be pulled back and forth against each other, which means that creatures that are too big can be sawed into smaller pieces. 

If attacked, the Grave Lily defends itself with its tentacles and sharp claws. It can also spew its digestive acid at the attacker.

## KTON AND FIRE WYRM 

Aslene fire wyrms are closely related to abyss worms. The smaller ones, called ktones, are particularly ravenous. They are mainly found in ashlands where they can move very fast when hunting prey. If the wind is right, their characteristic odor of acetone can be noticed from afar. 

As ktones get bigger, they are called fire wyrms and dig themselves down to the magma beneath the volcanic plains, where they can grow to immense sizes. Fire wyrms rarely go above ground, since at 

this stage they mainly feed on fiery minerals. They are only dangerous to surface- dwelling creatures who go near an open lava fissure or when summoned by magma singers. 

Really old fire wyrms, like the legendary Erinya, can grow much bigger and are even stronger and more heavily armored. 

Ktones and fire wyrms share the same monster attacks, but otherwise they have different stats.

If successful, the beast grabs and shakes the adventurer before they are thrown to NEAR range and land prone. 

- 2 **UNDERGROUND ROAR!** The beast lets out a deafening roar and all adventurers within NEAR range suffer a fear attack with four Base Dice (six for fire wyrms). 

- 3 **DARKENED SKY!** The beast rears up and darkens the sky with its tremendous mass. It then drops back down to crush all adventurers within ARM’S LENGTH range. Each adventurer must make a MOVE roll to evade the beast (not an action). Anyone who fails suffers an attack (blunt force) with ten Base Dice (twelve for fire wyrms). 

- 4 **THREAT FROM BELOW!** The giant beast digs itself into the ground and disappears. A few seconds later you feel the ground starting to shake and then give way under your feet! Everyone within NEAR range must make a MOVE roll (not an action) not to fall into the beast’s mouth, which inflicts one point of damage to Strength per round (three points for fire wyrms). The victim can keep attacking the wyrm from within, where it lacks armor. If the victim inflicts at least four points of damage with a single attack, the adventurer cuts their way out of the wyrm. 

- 5 **SWEEPING ATTACK!** The beast uses its long body to make a sweeping attack against all adventurers within NEAR range. Roll for the attack with six Base Dice (eight for fire wyrms) and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). Everyone hit by the attack is knocked down. 

- 6 **COLLAPSING GROUND!** The beast burrows down into the earth and its sheer weight causes the ground to collapse around the adventurers. Everyone within NEAR range falls 2D6 meters into a sinkhole.

## MECHA 

The dwarven mastersmiths of Firestead constructed mechas to perform heavy tasks that did not require more than simple instructions. Some mechas are still in working order in Tribolia, and probably in other parts of Firestead as well. 

Mechas resemble dwarves in size and proportion, but are purely mechanical – built of bronze, steel, leather, and other materials, optical crystals for eyes and hydraulic mechanisms for muscles. The spring that drives them must be wound up every twelve hours with a key or some other tool that fits into the hole in the back. 

The mechas can be equipped with weapons and instructed to use them, but the ones in Tribolia have nothing but hand drills and fists to fight with, should they be ordered to. Only dwarven mastersmiths like Kogler can give them instructions, since doing so requires precise tinkering with the mechanisms hidden behind the hatch in the mecha’s back.

## **MECHA** 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 9, AGILITY 2** 

**MOVEMENT:** 1 

**SKILLS:** Scouting 2 

**ARMOR RATING:** 8 

**GEAR:** Standard equipment for mechas are drills (Weapon Damage 2, stab wound) and fists (Weapon Damage 1, blunt force). Some mechas have been equipped with weapons such as swords and axes. 

**MECHANICAL:** Mechas only take half damage from fire and cold, as their metal bodies protects them from the elements. Their artificial eyes can see in the dark and are not affected by complete darkness. 

**DILAPIDATED:** The mechas are old and wornout. Even with the functional ones, there is a risk that all the grime and dirt have taken a toll on the machinery. Roll D6 each time a mecha is about to act. Rolling a **l** means that something goes wrong – roll on the Malfunction table to see what happens.

## SALAMANDER 

Aslene saurians are only found in firelands and occasionally on the plains, as they are heatloving creatures. They are called salamanders and resemble the saurians of Ravenland, but are cold- blooded, faster, and weaker. 

Salamanders can only use simple tools, but have their own language of primitive sounds. They like to hunt in packs and love hot- blooded flesh, though they cannot chew it and must leave it to rot in the ground before they can rip it to shreds. One of their common hunting 

methods is to have a few fast runners drive the prey towards other salamanders burrowed in the ground, who jump up as the victim gets tired and comes close. 

A salamander nest can hold up to 60 individuals and consists of stacked branches, stones, bones from various prey, and more or less anything that can be used as building material. It is not unusual for salamanders to occupy abandoned buildings. They often have scouts and hunters lurking in the ground a short distance from their nests. 

## **SALAMANDER** 

**ATTRIBUTES:** 

**STRENGTH 2, AGILITY 4, WITS 1, EMPATHY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Endurance 1, Melee 2, Scouting 2, Survival 3 

**TALENTS:** — 

**ARMOR RATING:** 3 (scales) 

**CLAW ATTACK:** Weapon Damage 2 (slash wound)

In combat they can bite, scratch with their front legs, squirt acid at their opponents, or try to crush them under their weight.

The smallest ones, called nymphs, only have six legs and cannot squirt acid. They still weight several hundred kilos.

## FOREST STAR 

In the Crimson Forests of the Bloodmarch dwell many bloodthirsty creatures. One of the most dangerous ones is the forest star – a huge, five- armed monstrosity that sweeps between the trees at great speed in the hunt for prey. At the end of each arm is a head with fangs. 

These heads act together, but are independent of each other, which means that the creature counts as five monsters in combat. 

Some forest stars are covered in snake scales, others in fur. Forest stars can cross the plains at night and temporarily leave the Crimson Forest to hunt, but only hunt in forested terrain where they can move around more easily. Large forest stars hunt alone or in pairs, while the smaller ones can appear in packs. 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

**THE HEADS OF THE FOREST STAR:** The forest star has five heads, each with its own Strength value. The forest star has as many attacks as it has heads. Draw an initiative cards for each head at the beginning of combat. A head whose Strength is reduced to zero is severed or crushed. 

## **FOREST STAR** 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 8** (per head) **, AGILITY 4 MOVEMENT:** 2 **ARMOR RATING:** 5

## SLIME SNAIL 

Vaerefor van Reiben’s wandering fortress Kasabatoda is a giant slime snail, but the adventurers may also encounter smaller versions of the animal in packs near crimson forests. Slime snails devour all organic matter in their path without chewing, including living creatures, and turn it into a trail of red slime which in turn spreads crimson forest. 

Since they are not technically predators, the slime snails move slowly without stalking their prey, but will shoot strings of slime at something moving around in front of them and try to drag it into their big mouths. Their shells and the rubbery bodies make slime snails highly resistant to physical violence. 

## **SLIME SNAIL** 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 15, AGILITY 3 MOVEMENT:** 1 **SKILLS:** Scouting 2 **ARMOR RATING:** 9 (shell) 

**SLIME STRING:** A slime snail can shoot strings of slime at a victim within NEAR range. Roll for the attack with eight Base Dice. If hit, the victim is GRAPPLED . The slime strings have Strength 6. If the victim is still trapped on the slime snail’s next turn, the beast pulls them into its mouth and swallows them, inflicting one point of damage to Strength until the victim escapes by killing the snail from within. An adventurer who is broken inside a slime snail dies instantly.

## SLITHERNET 

The Slithernet is a terrestrial, carnivorous plant whose large leaves have turned into thick nets which lie hidden among leaves and twigs on the ground. When someone steps into the net, the leaves snap shut like a mouse trap so that the prey is held down on the ground. 

Sharp shoots immediately sprout through the mesh of leaves from the plant’s ground tissue. The shoots pierce and kill the victim within a day or two, after which it is left to rot until the plant can suck up the juices. 

The slithernet’s leaves can be cut off and used as a durable net or rope. If one detects 

them in time, it is possible to make the leaves snap shut by poking them with a long stick or something similar. 

## **SLITHERNET** 

## **ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 12, AGILITY 1** 

**SKILLS:** Scouting 5 

**ARMOR RATING:** 3 (only bladed weapons can damage the net) 

**NET ATTACK:** The slithernet attacks all victims that come into NEAR range. A person with a knife or sword can cut themselves loose with a successful MOVE roll, but is otherwise restrained so tightly that they cannot move their arms. The net can also be cut from the outside. A victim trapped in the net suffers one point of damage to Strength each Quarter Day, and dies if broken.

## SPOREWALKER 

Sporewalkers look like skinny, red humans with vague facial features. In their arms they carry dripping bundles of spore substances out of swamps and thickets to spread the crimson forest. After putting down their burden, they lie down and decay to nourish the vegetation. Sporewalkers cannot be regarded as monsters, since they neither attack nor defend themselves. They are easily killed, but always keep coming. 

## **SPOREWALKER** 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 2, AGILITY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Scouting 1 **ARMOR RATING:** 1 

**SPORES:** One in six sporewalkers ( **l** on a D6) explodes upon taking damage, spreading crimson slime and body parts around itself. Roll an attack with five Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (non- typical) against everyone within NEAR range. The attack can be PARRIED with a shield, but not dodged. Anyone hit by the attack risks contracting Crimson Sickness (page 42).

## HOVERFROG 

The birds of the Bloodmarch risk being caught and eaten by hoverfrogs, a predatory amphibian that cannot fly, but hover, lifted into the air by the antennae on its back which seem to suspend the law of gravity. The hoverfrog is left hanging in the air, where it slowly paddles its way forward with its webbed claws, catching birds by shooting out its sticky tongue whenever one comes close enough. 

holding six broken hoverfrog antennae in each hand, or twelve tucked inside their clothes, can float off the ground. Unbroken antennae can be stored for about a month after being harvested from the hoverfrogs, and then they stop working. 2D6 antennae can be harvested from each hoverfrog. 

## **HOVERFROG** 

The animal is no threat to humans and similar kin, but hunted by them for the antennae on its back which allow them to float in the air. In order to suspend the law of gravity, the antennae must be broken in half, after which the effect lasts a Quarter Day. A human 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 5 SKILLS:** Scouting 5 **MOVEMENT:** 2 (hover) **ARMOR RATING:** 1

## PEARLYVEINE 

Many view each unbroken patch of crimson forest as a single living creature. Few know for certain, but it is true that interconnected veins of viscous red slime are spreading on the ground in mature crimson forests, and that these veins converge into a glistening white structure that resembles a large pearl or eyeball with neither retina nor pupil. 

These pearlyveines are often hard to find, since they are invariably located in the densest possible vegetation, preferably in some crevice or underneath a fallen tree trunk. Some believe them to be intelligent, and red elves worship them as the root galls of Rubor from which the god’s body will one day grow. 

It has been suggested that pearlyveines can direct other creatures in the crimson forest, open and close passages to lead intruders astray or 

into traps or give their adherents clear passage, but no one knows for sure. The red elves believe that Vaerefor van Reiben is the first aggregate of root galls that have formed the god’s prophet.

## TYPICAL NPCS 

This section describes a number of typical NPCs in the Bloodmarch. 

## **TALENTS:** — 

**GEAR:** Broadsword, shield, chainmail, riding horse 

## AGENT OF AD VIPERA 

Ad Vipera’s agents in the Bloodmarch are led by Poansa Moulter, and some are traveling with her. Ad Vipera has also enlisted soldiers with stats like Alderlander Soldiers, as well as local pathfinders with stats like Hunters or Clan Riders. The stats below are for Ad Vipera’s elite agents – the Sisterhood of the Black Mamba. . 

## **SOLDIER** 

**STRENGTH 4, AGILITY 3, WITS 3, EMPATHY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Endurance 2, Melee 2, Marksmanship 2, Move 1 

**TALENTS:** — 

**GEAR:** Broadsword, throwing spear, shield, chainmail

## **STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 4, WITS 3, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Stealth 2, Move 2, Marksmanship 2, Lore 2, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 

**TALENTS:** Path of Poison 2 

**GEAR:** Dagger, shortsword, short bow, leather armor 

**IMMUNITY:** All Sisters of the Black Mamba are immune to all poisons. 

## EGRESSOR 

Egressors are members of a necromantic cult dedicated to the practical handling of the restless dead, whom they often refer to as their “clients”. Egressors are usually accompanied by a couple of these walking dead whose interests they serve, as well as a couple of miscreants (now undead) who are serving the Egressor as punishment for their crimes. The miscreants fight to protect the Egressor, but the clients do not – it is the Egressor who serves them, not the other way around. 

## ALDERLANDER SOLDIER 

The Rust Church and the Congregation of the Serpent have both sent forces to the Bloodmarch under the respective command of Colonel Grudenstaal and Poansa Moulter. 

## **EGRESSOR** 

**STRENGTH 2, AGILITY 2, WITS 4, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Slight of Hand 2, Lore 3, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 

**TALENTS:** Death Magic 2 

## **OFFICER** 

## **STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 3, WITS 3, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Marksmanship 1, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 

**FEARLESS:** Egressors do not roll on the fear table when dealing with undead. 

**TRUEHEARING:** Egressors can automatically determine whether someone is telling the truth in matters concerning their clients.

## GRARG 

In the Vasnia region, orcs, humans, and wolfkin co- exist in relative harmony. Crossbreeds are not uncommon and the indigenous Vasnians have traces of orc blood in their veins. Crosses between orcs and wolfkin are called grargs. Grargs are more heavy- set and animalistic than ordinary wolfkin, equipped with tusks and feared in battle. They can only speak and understand a small number of words. 

Only male grargs are born. The females probably die at birth, and the males are born sterile. This means that grargs do not form their own kin, but are kept as warriors, preferably a bit apart from more civilized folk because of their unpredictable temper. 

## **GRARG**

There they have largely adopted the settlers’ lifestyles and learned their skills.

## RAIDER 

Many Ravenlanders, as well as Alderlanders and Aslenes, are pursuing a career as raiders, seeking quick riches through grave robbing, fraud, or good old- fashioned thievery. Some mercenaries also have a background as raiders, or at least share their attitude and skills.

## **STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 4, WITS 2, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 1, Marksmanship 2, Survival 1, Manipulation 1 

**GEAR:** Short spear, short bow, leather armor 

## RED ELF 

Red elves are transformed ordinary elves living in the crimson forests of Alsene, which they call Reiben. They sometimes leave their forests for various reasons, always in groups. They are fanatical followers of the god Rubor – the principle of the color red and enemy of all other colors, which are considered the miscreations of the evil god Kolor. They revere Vaerefor van Reiben as Rubor’s prophet and their leader. Red elves are sensitive (and sometimes addicted) to concentrated salt, which for them has the effect of a hallucinogenic poison with Potency 6. An ordinary elven ruby – with or without flesh attached to it – that passes through a slime snail’s insides becomes a red elf. 

## PYROLYTE 

Pyrolytes are fanatical, fire- worshiping priests of Have who banish demons through the use of magma song, blue tar, and lyco podium powder.

_clacking their jaws, twisting or bending their backs to the side, and straightening up again, before scratching their bony fingers across their chests with a rattling sound. The dancing skeletons are remarkably shiny, as if cleaned and lacquered. A living dwarf woman is tending to some cooking pots. A bit further away from the fire is a black, covered wagon hitched to the skeleton of an ox._ 

Draug of the White is a necromancer and member of the Egressors. He has taken it upon himself to follow the Seventh and resurrect the skeletons they leave in their wake, with the intention of gathering a rattling army large enough to wreak vengeance upon the flesh-eaters. 

The shiny skeletons have all been picked clean by the Seventh and glazed by the worms’ juices. If Draug gets the impression that the player characters are enemies of the Seventh, he is happy to tell them where the worm people came from and other things they need to know. Draug knows a great deal about the Seventh, since the porcelain skeletons provide him with the details. 

The player characters can encounter Draug of the White multiple times during the game, and the number of skeletons increases by D6 for each phase of the campaign. 

## **DRAUG** 

## **STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 2, WITS 4, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 1, Lore 3, Insight 4, Manipulation 2 

**TALENTS:** Death Magic 3 

## **GEAR:** Dagger 

**MECHANICAL HEART:** Draug has a second, mechanical heart in his chest, constructed by a dwarven blacksmith named Kormald. It repairs his body and brings him back to life one turn after his death. Crushing the heart kills him. 

## MILDELLA 

Mildella was Kormald’s wife. She offered to serve Draug during her five-year mourning period following the death of her husband on the condition that he would put the late Kormald to rest, as he had turned into a death knight after dismantling a cursed item. Draug managed to lift the curse, and as a reward the mastersmith crafted the mechanical heart before withdrawing into the family crypt. Mildella knows the Kogler in Tribolia, should it come up, and can tell the player characters where to find Tribolia.

## **2** THE  RUNAWAY BRIDE 

_A screaming cloud of dust approaches across the plains. Closer up you see a human being dragged by a large horse, stuck in one of the stirrups. The dragged person curses the animal in surprisingly creative language._ 

In order to help the fallen rider, the player characters must first stop and deal with the highly agitated horse which is kicking and biting. 

The unfortunate individual turns out to be an Aslene woman of the Galdane clan named Kromabella. If the adventurers save her, she cries a little and explains that she is to be “married” to one of the Sons of Horn, meaning that she will be sacrificed to the nearest active volcano. If none of them have been activated yet, she will be thrown into a lava fissure near the volcano. The “bride” would very much like to be rescued by the adventurers, and preferably dropped off somewhere safe. 

If the adventurers gain her trust, Kromabella turns out to be well-informed about the clans’ activities as she travels extensively and is welcome anywhere (except among the Galdanes) with her filthy songs. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Ashlands, Hills 

## **KROMABELLA** 

## **STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 4, WITS 3, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Marksmanship 2, Move 2, Manipulation 5, Animal Handling 2 

**TALENTS:** Horseback Fighter 2 

**GEAR:** Leather armor 

## **3** COLDWOOD 

_You reach a small wood whose chilly temperature is as welcome as it is inexplicable, with the possibility of finding water. The trees are a darker shade of green than those one usually sees in the Bloodmarch, and the type of tree is unfamiliar. Moving closer, you notice the temperature dropping and silence spreading, as an oppressive stillness settles around you. In the distance you hear a dragging sound, like the bridal train of the dead, from something that seems to be moving among the trees_ 

A successful LORE roll reveals that the trees are direglooms, said to hold the souls of creatures who died unhappy in love. The horse clans believe that such woods are haunted and best avoided. The trees are also commonly known as “coldpines”. 

An adventurer who makes a successful SUR - VIVAL roll can instead have heard that good water can often be found deeper in such coldwoods and that the talk of dead souls is mere superstition spread by druids when they want to be left alone. 

The fact of the matter is that direglooms are not linked to the dead at all, but simply have the ability to lower the temperature inside their trunk and branches, which causes condensation to trickle down to their roots and keep them watered. Indeed, there is usually a freshwater lake at the center of such woods. 

Unfortunately, caution is warranted in this particular case, since the woods have become home to two fur-covered forest stars (page 118). They have come to the woods to enjoy the cooler environment and feed on deer, humans, and other creatures drawn to the water. It is them clambering between the trees that sounds like a bridal train. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Forest, Crimson Forest

## **4** DENSE WATER 

_A group of eight horned dwarves have made camp by a stream where they have set up strange mechanical equipment of wood, leather, and brass. A paddle wheel placed in the stream is tipping a set of one­meter­long brass rods back and forth. Some of the dwarves reach for their weapons as you approach, but an older dwarf calms them down and calls his greetings._ 

The dwarves have come from Firestead, led by Master Menasse. They are making dense water from the stream, which contains just the right mixture of rain from the south, fallen from a sky dominated by the wandering star called the Eye of Dew. 

Master Menasse has a problem: he needs a fresh kidney, taken directly from a living creature, to filter an important component of his dense water. The guests would not happen to have a kidney to spare? Otherwise they could help him catch a living beast no smaller than a wolf, in which case he would be very grateful and provide them with dense water (see the Drugs and Potions section). A living whiner would also work. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Forest, Plains, Hills

## **5** THE SLITHERING HORROR 

_In the distance you notice what looks like a haystack moving slowly, surrounded by screaming humans. Closer up you see a shapeless red mass of tentacles, eyes, hands, and sharp outgrowths slithering across the plains. The humans seem to be a handful of peasants armed with pitchforks, cudgels, and sharp sticks, trying to drive the creature in some indeter­ minate direction._ 

If the adventurers move closer, the peasants cry for help. The red mass is a spore ball from the nearest crimson forest. Spore balls are creatures that crawl off and explode twelve kilometers or so away from the forest, thereby spreading the demonic vegetation. The peasants are trying to push the ball towards a deep ravine nearby to get rid of it before it bursts. Shielding it from sunlight will delay the process. 

If the spore ball explodes, everyone within NEAR range suffers an attack with five Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. Anyone hit by the attack risks contracting Crimson Sickness (page 42). The ball’s parts are spread across the area and start to settle into the earth. The hex where the 

134 

**chapter 10** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

spore ball exploded turns into crimson forest after one moth. The event may be repeated. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Hills 

## **6** SLAVERS 

_From a distance you see a handful of Aslene riders out on the plain. They seem to have noticed you as well, and they are coming for you_ 

The slavers come from a different horse clan than the one controlling this territory. In a drunken rage they killed a couple of local clan members and must now pay restitution, which means that they must deliver five slaves to the local chieftain who is holding a couple of their friends hostage. The slavers (twice as many as the adventurers) are desperate and might attack the adventurers to capture them, but they are also ashamed and open to other solutions. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Hills 

going on in the area. If the player characters reveal that they are carrying one or more of Horn’s Astra, she becomes very interested. Otherwise she is more interested in seemingly trivial details than in major events, such as the color of some flowers or the squirrels sniffing the air before they leave. She welcomes them to Oxengelder (page 185) if they should ever pass that way, and can point them in the direction of the adventure site. 

The Dreamstress says that she often travels in other people’s dreams to have a look around. If the adventurers attack her or point out that this is not a dream, everything fades away and they wake up in the same place as they did that morning. Everything that happened since they last slept turns out to have been a dream. 

When the adventurers begin their day anew, everything that happened last time repeats itself, but the adventurers may act differently this time and any die rolls are rerolled. The Dreamstress does not appear this time, but the encounter may be repeated at a later stage. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Hills, Ashlands

## **8** THE  PENITENT IN THE MAGMA 

_In the distance you see a remarkably pointy hill – it is shaped like a large pastry. As the hill begins to move, you realize that it is alive_ 

Adventurers who move closer will encounter the key player known as the Dreamstress (page 96), who greets them warmly, tells them about herself and oneiromancy, and wants to know what is 

_A voice is calling to the adventurers (possibly in their dreams) as they pass an open lava flow and asks for their help. The lava rises and forms a vaguely human­like figure that introduces itself as Krasylla._ 

The creature is actually the demon Krasylla whom the adventurers may already have met in Ravenland during _Raven’s Purge_ . In that 

135 

**random encounters** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

case the demon might recognize them or make references to events that took place back then. 

Krasylla describes how he was dragged into the magma as punishment for his involvement in the Demon Flood. Now he has atoned for his sin, but remains unjustly imprisoned in this torturous heat. If the adventurers help him escape, the demon offers them his sworn friendship and one of Horn’s Astra in return. 

In order to leave the magma that burns him unceasingly, Krasylla must take over the fresh body of a Horn priest. The priest must be killed at the edge of the lava for this to succeed. It would actually work just as well with any body, but Krasylla knows that the fire wyrm Erinya would not touch an ordained Horn priest and would therefore prefer one of those. 

Krasylla can offer them the Arrow of the Fire Wyrm with which he was branded in _Raven’s Purge_ (or later, when the adventurers were not around). If the player characters accept the offer, the demon will probably wait until he is free until he tells them that his fire arrow is only one of the three they need. Once he is free, Krasylla heads to the adventure site Oxengelder and its limestone plains where Horn cannot reach him. Krasylla’s Arrow of the Fire Wyrm could attract Erinya, if the GM so desires. 

Krasylla will then help the Dreamstress return to her homeworld Eoni. He might choose to go with her or, as an honest penitent, or enter an alliance with Selligar Horne of Havenmark to liberate the Bloodmarch and thus atone for his crimes against the country. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Ashlands, Firelands

## **9** THE DEAD FOAL 

_Up ahead you see a scrawny animal, perhaps a deer, wandering around alone and afraid. It is behaving strangely and seems lost. Now and then it raises its head and lets out a hoarse, pitiful cry, and then seems to listen for a response. You soon notice a group of jackals prowling nearby, probably eyeing the animal as their next meal. Then you realize that the lone animal is the undead skeleton of a horse, probably the remains of a foal. And the jackals are drawing near._ 

Adventurers who move even closer will have to fight the jackals, which outnumber them by two. The jackals will flee once three of them have been slain. The skeleton foal is agitated and unruly, but once it calms down it seems to trust the adventurers and follows them around. 

Later in the day, an extravagant black carriage emerges from a cloud of dust. It is heading straight for the foal, rather than the adventurers. Closer up they see that the carriage is pulled by two adult horse skeletons. The carriage stops and the skeletons immediately collapse into a heap of bones through their harnesses. The foal whinnies happily as it runs over there and collapses into the same heap. 

Out of the carriage steps Mortilla (page 177) whom the adventurers may already have met, or might see again, in the adventure site Salterstay. She explains how worried she has been ever since 

136 

**chapter 10** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

little Clatterhoof went missing, and wants to reward the adventurers for taking care of the foal. 

The person she likes most (i.e. the one with the highest Empathy) receives what looks like a fishing lure with a hook, enamel plates, and colored feathers. She explains that hanging it on the ribs of an undead creature will make it obey the person who put it there as long as the hook remains in place. 

Mortilla has a keen interest in exciting and spectacular deaths and questions the adventurers to find out whether they have something promising coming up on that front. If so, she could accompany them for a while; otherwise she is on the way to watch the last specimens of an endangered lizard die. 

These humans are pyrolytes (page 129) – fanatical, fire-worshiping priests of Have who are harvesting lycopodium powder from the giant trees of the grove. The woman on the rock is called Picteca and is not a prisoner at all, but another pyrolyte who is having ritual burns inflicted upon her by her colleagues. The leather straps are there to hold her still during the procedure. If the adventurers intervene, Picteca will weep with rage, not knowing whether she will be able to muster enough courage for another burning. 

One resource die of blue tar can be purchased from the pyrolytes, who can also provide assistance in combating demons. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Hills

## **11** GLORY STEALERS 

_PIn the distance you see a group of Vasnians (or horned dwarves, depending on the location) who have made camp and are roasting pieces of dread raptor over a fire. They seem to be in high spirits as they help themselves to the fermented beverages in their waterskins. You are welcome to share their camp and meal, since there is more meat than they can possibly eat or even carry home._ 

_There are fires raging at the edge of a grove with tall, slightly strange­looking trees. Humans are climbing the trees and seem to be harvesting some­ thing into their satchels. Lying on a flat rock is a naked woman tied up with leather straps in a supine position. Some other people are torturing the poor woman with torches and red­hot irons. She writhes in agony, but does not make a sound._ 

The group is in an excellent mood and has no desire to fight unless one provokes them in some way. If the adventurers join them, they start explaining how last week they encountered some other wanderers who had lived through amazing adventures. As they recount the story, the adventurers soon realize that the exploits being described are their own, albeit embellished 

137 

**random encounters** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

with fantastic details. Someone has clearly taken credit for their feats. 

The event can be repeated with others who have heard similar stories of other people’s heroic deeds that were actually performed by the adventurers. The people taking credit for the adventurers’ acts can be any of the minor characters they will encounter in later adventure sites. As long as the glory-stealers are allowed to continue, the adventurers cannot – for better or worse – increase their Reputation since others are taking credit for everything they do. Confronting and defeating the glory-stealers immediately raises their Reputation score by one step. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Crimson Forest, Hills, Mountains

## **12** THE SHRIKE 

**MIXED KIN:** Shrikes can be either halflings or goblins and have the matching kin talent. 

_While you are out gathering firewood, a halfling suddenly appears from the vegetation, wearing clothes that look like they have grown to his skin. He begs you to spare the living trees and only pick dead sticks for the fire._ 

The halfling is a member of the Shrikes, a group of reclusive druids who have taken it upon themselves to preserve the forests of Aslene. The druid will not openly attack the strangers even if they offend him, but he and his rather numerous group of like-minded halflings and goblins will make their lives difficult as long as they are in the same forest area. Militant members might even seek revenge by treating wrongdoers as they treated the trees: cutting 

## **13** WHINER’S NEST 

_Two huge dread raptors are pecking at what seems to be an overgrown termite nest. Squeaky screams and curses are heard from the nest. Small creatures come jumping out of holes in the ground and throw little darts at the great birds, without harming them at all. The dread raptors lunge at them. Whenever they catch one, they slam their prey unconscious against the ground, throw it into the air, and swallow it whole._ 

The raptors (page 23 of _Book of Beasts_ ) are trying to peck their way into some whiners’ burrows 

138 **chapter 10** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

in order to eat them. There is no escape for the whiners down there, and their friends can do little to stop the attackers. The adventurer can choose to engage the birds or walk away. Either way there is a risk that the birds will notice them and come for them instead. 

If the dread raptors are defeated, the whiners will leave something of value outside their burrows (after conferring with each other for a few hours). But they are afraid to speak with or show themselves to tall folk unless they make camp in the area, prove themselves harmless, or leave food for the whiners, thereby gaining their trust. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Hills

## **14** SWEET DREAMS 

_You see a man and a woman cooking food by a camp­ fire. Next to them is a wagon to which a tail­wag­ ging little dog is tied. The beautiful couple intro­ duce themselves as Noesius and Noema and invite you to share their meal, which smells wonderful. They claim to be oneiromancer merchants from the Drawn Lands, and are curious about your dreams._ 

in Oxengelder. They know what they are, but want nothing more than to be real and often ponder their own existence and how it could become reality. 

Meanwhile, they are doing everything they can to blend in among real creatures and learn from them. They can tell stories about the marvelous Drawn Lands, answer questions, and explain where to find various places and items, but it is all made up – they barely know anything at all. While they cannot read minds, they can read other people’s desires and simply give everyone what they want. 

The food they serve is delicious, but provides no nourishment as it is not real. Their information seems accurate, and occasionally it might be, but only by coincidence. It is all fabricated and adjusted to please the person asking about it. They might even offer to teach the adventurers oneiromancy, and the lessons seem serious, but nobody learns anything. 

If attacked, the couple appear to sustain a critical injury, since that is what the attacker desires, but they soon return to normal. 

If the adventurers sleep in the creatures’ presence, they will at some point during the night have one of their dreams stolen by the illusions – not out of malice, but to become more real. 

Later in the campaign, Noema and Noesis will seek out the Dreamstress in Oxengelder where they can be encountered a second time. The Dreamstress will help them become real as she feels a bit guilty for their dilemma. In return, Noema and Noesis will be loyal to her. 

Noesis and Noema look real, but are in fact permanent illusions created during experiments

## **15** FOR DECAY 

_You come across a seemingly peaceful little settle­ ment of twenty houses surrounded by a palisade. Soon you notice that its people are unusually quiet and going about their business with downcast eyes. Furthermore, they are all wearing hoods and gloves or have their faces and bare skin painted red. Before you even step through the gate, a couple of men block your path and bid you welcome, before asking you to either cover your skin or paint it red. For this purpose they keep a large bucket of red slime close at hand. A faint but foul smell of death comes drifting from the east. To the west of the village runs a stream, lined by tall trees entwined with vines._ 

**THE MASS GRAVE:** Adventurers who follow the stench to a sandy spot east of the village will find a hastily filled-in mass grave con- taining the remains of the murdered villag ers. There the adventurers must fight a pack of wild dogs digging in the earth. There is also mad old lady crawling around naked and screaming. The woman is utterly insane and has a worm from the Seventh in her right armpit, which makes the wild dogs afraid of her. The Seventh (page 85) will know what happens to the old woman and what is said in her presence. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Hills 

The village of Redhurst is being watched by Vaerefor van Reiben’s red elves. Each night there is a risk (a roll of 1 or 2 on D6) of red elves (twice as many as the adventurers) arriving to make sure that everyone follows the rules and that no other color than red can be seen. More red elves are fetched if they find anything inappropriate. 

The red elves gave Redhurst to a group of starving refugees from Ravenland a few months ago so that the pepper harvest could continue. The previous inhabitants were all slaughtered. The pepper vines are winding their way up the trees around the village. 

In order to keep their new guests in line, the red elves claim that the previous inhabitants were killed because they violated the rules of Rubor which they had sworn to obey, but it was really about avenging the death of some red elves who were killed near the village. 

In any event, the villagers are terrified, but had no choice but to obey the red elves’ rules as the only alternative was starvation. 

## **16** RESURRECTION 

_In the middle of nowhere you encounter ten horned dwarves gathered around a square rock. Lying on the rock is another dwarf. Some of the others are brushing flies off her face. A dwarf who has clearly been keeping watch asks you to be quiet and not disrupt the ritual._ 

The woman on the rock is dead. She was a highborn horned dwarf from Firestead named Morgena van Lodeglaas. If the adventurers move closer, they will learn that Morgena was very wealthy and left specific instructions in her will that the funeral mass should be performed in this exact location on this particular altar. 

The stone altar bears ancient inscriptions, and above Morgena’s head is a carved-out depression the size of a soup bowl. Once the singing is over, a black obsidian orb is brought out from 

140 

**chapter 10** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

a gilded box. The orb is placed in the hands of the deceased, and the two Horn priests present resume their chant. The corpse is not entirely fresh, as indicated by the smell and flies. An elegant young dwarf discretely asks to speak with the adventurers. He introduces himself as Morgenbard van Lodeglaas, Morgena’s nephew. Morgenbard claims to have learned that the ritual is not a funeral mass at all, but meant to bring Morgena back to life. The rock is a time altar and the orb is the magical artifact required to activate the altar if placed correctly in the depression. Morgenbard sees no need whatsoever to resurrect his aunt, who was a stingy old hag when she was alive. In return for the adventurers’ help, he offers them the orb as well as payment in gold. 

Next time this random event occurs, the adventurers encounter a group of horned dwarves who are out to kill them, since Morgenbard does not want any living witnesses. 

_chasing a herd of wild horses, fights two of their stallions, gets beaten up, and remains standing a bit further away, stamping impatiently and graz­ ing in anger._ 

The flying horse (page 105) is Barganos, owned by the Sabirian chieftain Mommodar the Gnawer. Barganos escaped after catching the scent of a mare in heat among the wild herd. Used to being served a meal every evening, he is now tired and hungry. He allows himself to be caught after rearing a few times to mark his rank. 

Eight Sabirian riders are tracking Barganos to bring him home. They will not be understanding if someone else tries to claim him. Even if the adventurers manage to keep the animal, most members of the horse clans will know whose horse it is when they see it.

## **17** THE FLYING HORSE 

_You see a white, riderless wingsteed glide across the sky and land a bit further away, where it starts_ 

The first time: _In the distance you see carrion birds feasting on bloodied corpses. If you move closer, the birds reluctantly take flight with angry squawks. A gruesome sight meets your eyes: the heads of five red elves are impaled on spikes with their maimed bodies lying nearby. A healer can see that the bodies have been cut open and robbed of their elven rubies. On a stone among the bodies, someone has written in wax: “The Inglourious Butchars”._ 

141 

**random encounters** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The second time: _You see ten creatures sitting by a campfire, hands bloody, playing a board game. They are laughing and feasting and do not seem entirely sober, although two of them are standing apart from the others. Three red elf heads on spikes can be seen a bit further away from the fire. Every now and then the group raise their glasses at them. The red elves’ bodies lie discarded in some bushes. Carrion birds are cawing angrily around the feast, as if telling the living to leave._ 

Olive, Ash, and Amboyna. They are accompanied by Minia, a learned elven druid from Ravenland, and Gaunt, a semi-intelligent prune whom the Butchars use to scare and torture their victims before killing them. Only Minia can tell whether an elven ruby is real – that is, whether it can be turned into an ordinary elf in the Stillmist – or whether the ruby was created by Vaerefor with the Goblet Staff of Have. 

The group calls itself “the Inglourious Butchars” – a signature they leave wherever they carry out their bloody work. The Inglourious Butchars are hunting red elves to kill them and cut out their rubies. If the elves are seen as potential recruits, their rubies are brought to the masters in the Stillmist for recycling. The others are sold for money to humiliate their former owners. With a few exceptions, the group’s members are themselves former red elves who have been restored – but too quickly, which caused certain defects in their bodies and minds. Minia, an ordinary elf who has decided to accompany the group, says that the Butchars’ rubies were so furious that they hastened the regrowth of the flesh so that they could immediately return to the Bloodmarch and take revenge. 

The Butchars drug themselves with salt to cope with their shame and pain. They hate the crimson forest, the red elves, and above all, Vaerefor van Reiben. The leader of the Butchars, who calls himself Mahogany, has a special knife saved for Vaerefor. The dagger is called Glasstooth and could be one of Horn’s Astra – unless you have given that dagger to the key player Poansa. In that case Mahogany’s dagger is a replica. 

There are ten former red elves among the Butchars, all with wood-related names: Mahogany, Birch, Burl, Merbau, Wenge, Jatoba, Katalox, 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Crimson Forest, Hills, Mountains

## **19** LAST NIGHT OF THE JESTERS 

_You hear music and hooting coming from a horse­ drawn wagon where a troupe of jesters has made camp. The jesters are performing, but their audi­ ence of a dozen soldiers in field clothes do not seem very amused. An officer gets up and starts ranting that the blasphemers must be punished. The soldiers respond by chanting “Funnier! Funnier!” The jest­ ers are crying, but try their best to entertain them. Some soldiers start throwing stones at the performers._ 

Closer up you notice that the soldiers are wearing Alderlander clothes and, judging by the religious symbols they wear, seem to worship 

142 **chapter 10** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

either Wyrm or Rust (depending on what best suits the game). Two Aslene riders are sitting by a campfire a bit further away. They are pathfinders – one hired by the soldiers and one (who also serves as a bodyguard) by the jesters. They welcome the adventurers and explain that the whole thing is an unfortunate affair. The jesters were careless enough to make fun of the Alderlanders’ god. The troupe has come to the Bloodmarch to spread joy. Their leader, Pottus Tynk, has an almost religious faith in laughter as a cathartic salvation and creator of friendships. 

The officer, Mengendorm of Trella, disapproves of the ruckus and wants the player characters to beat up the meanest of the soldiers so the others stop harassing the performers. The officer can lead the adventurer to their commander – either Colonel Grudenstaal (Rust Church) or Poansa Moulter (Congregation of the Serpent). 

If they help the jesters, Pottus Tynk will be most grateful. He does not have any money, but can offer the adventurers his left shoe, which allows its wearer to maintain perfect balance in any situation, even on a slack rope. This is Sella’s Dragonboot (page 65), one of Horn’s Astra, though Pottus does not know it. He has had a matching right shoe made, which obviously is not made of dragon leather.

## **21** RUST 

_From a distance you see what looks like a large rock shaped like the upper body of a human. Someone has made camp next to the rock and a thin column of smoke rises from a fire._ 

The rock is in fact a three-meter-tall iron statue – the head and torso of the god Rust as depicted on page 39 in the _Gamemaster’s Guide_ . The only person there is a hermit named Suderman. Whenever someone comes near, he opens a door in the statue and locks himself in. He has a spear that he can thrust through various cracks in the statue, but it is not a very effective defense. 

If someone manages to speak with Suderman, for example by offering him some food, he says that he is a rust priest from Alderland and part of Colonel Grudenstaal’s expedition in the Bloodmarch. The damned Ironbrows got tired of carrying the holy likeness of Rust, put the god down, and walked away, in spite of Suderman cursing them and invoking Rust’s punishment on them. The worst of them is Colonel Grudenstaal himself who is a believer in name only. 

Suderman can tell the adventurers about the Ironbrows and the Colonel (page 79), and their 

## **22** THE SHIELD WALL 

_From a ridge you see two groups of Aslene riders mocking each other on the plain and challenging each other to battle by making pirouettes, rearing their horses, and shaking their weapons in the air. After a particularly gross insult, one side lowers their lances and starts rushing at their malign­ ers. Just as they are about to clash, something unexpected happens: the attackers charge full­tilt into a rock face that was not there a moment ago. Their lances shatter, as probably do their bones and everything else in great turmoil. The opponents are nowhere to be seen, but you can hear laughter coming from somewhere further away. The riders have heard it too and are looking in your direction._ 

It is Vinhelm Kaal (page 89) who has summoned a Fata Morgana (page 33) that tricked the riders. One of the horse warriors spotted him before the mentalist had a chance to disappear and mentioned seeing what looked like a fat little pig on two legs – a description Vinhelm Kaal could not leave unpunished. 

Vinhelm will slip away again if possible. A couple of horse warriors are badly injured and

## **23** THE DWARF IN THE STONE 

_You happen to come upon a monolith and notice that the veining of the stone resembles a human crea­ ture looking out with its hands pressed against the surface. At the foot of the stone there are some half­ wilted flowers next to a tankard of ale in which two slugs have drowned. It might be a tombstone._ 

**FREEING SVOLDER:** Svolder can be freed by tapping his wand against the stone, which causes it to crumble. That is precisely what he is trying to say, but in order to understand him one must sit in front of the stone for three whole days and be proficient in either lip reading or sign language. 

Gronena keeps the wand buried near her cottage not far from the stone. The fact is that she figured out how to free Svolder fifteen years ago, but does not want to do it since she has grown old while he has remained young inside the stone. 

Gronena cannot stand the thought of him leaving her, which is why she lets him stay there while telling herself that she is a good and faithful woman. If he is freed, Svolder will yell at and maybe even kill Gronena before leaving the area. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Hills 

There is no grave, but imprisoned inside the stone itself is the dwarf Svolder, a stone singer who suffered a magical mishap long ago. Adventurers who look at the stone the next day will find that he has moved a bit, but he moves so slowly that it is barely noticeable. 

**THE OLD LADY:** If the adventurers stay where they are, an old human woman will arrive within a Quarter Day with new flowers and perhaps a herb-filled pipe that she places by the
