## **25** THE CONTEST 

_A crowd of riders has dismounted and gathered in a circle. A knowledgeable person can tell that they are Galdanes. Perhaps they are holding council? Moving closer, you hear a woman singing in the middle of the circle, accompanied by someone playing the lyre._ 

## **26** THE RED RENEGADE 

_A red elf is fleeing on foot, and you can see a dozen others in pursuit a few hundred meters behind her. As you get closer you see that the red elf is bloody. She waves at you and screams for help._ 

Two people lay claim to leadership of the Galdanes. One is Kormella Mira of Moldena from Ravenland, descendant of Kanender the Mad who, during the Demon Flood three hundred years ago, cut through the invading hordes and led his followers east through Shadowgate Pass. The other is Trandesso Havaman who was born in the Bloodmarch. Now the two of them wish to challenge each other for the title of chieftain. They compete in horseback riding, single combat, and now musical performance, cheered on by their respective supporters. 

**IMPARTIAL JUDGES:** If the group spots the adventurers, the strangers are asked to judge the contest in an impartial manner. Regardless of your decision, the losing side will question the judges’ competence, suitability, appearance, and intelligence, and remember this affront to justice. 

The winners, on the other hand, will praise the adventurers’ excellent taste in music and want to make friends with them. In the evening everyone feasts on roasted game meat and fermented horse milk. The losers still hold a grudge and might challenge the adventurers or serve their revenge cold at a later time. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Hills, Ashlands 

The seemingly exhausted red elf calls herself Rubilla. She claims to be a former redrunner from Ravenland who was captured and turned into a red elf by Vaerefor van Reiben. For some reason she herself does not understand, she was able to retain her memories and has been collecting the rubies of former friends who would also be turned. She carries them in a belt with compartments. 

She has been looking for a group of elves from Ravenland who call themselves the Inglourious Butchars, but the pursuing red elves caught up with her. She begs the adventurers to help her escape or, should that be impossible, make sure that the elven rubies are somehow brought to the Butchars or directly to the Stillmist so that her friends can be restored. 

**RUBILLA SAVED:** If the adventurers manage to escape the pursuers with Rubilla, she is very grateful and can tell them more about Vaerefor, the crimson forest, and the red elves. After a week or two she leaves them under some pretext or other, but would preferably have them take over responsibility for the fate of the rubies. 

**THE PURSUERS:** If the pursuers catch up to the adventurers, they demand that Rubilla be handed over to them. They do not seem to know 

146 

**chapter 10** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

about the rubies she carries, and Rubilla whispers for her saviors to take the precious stones to safety and hand her over. 

If they surrender her, Rubilla will be taken to the nearest crimson forest under the pretext that Vaerefor wants to examine why she kept her memories. If combat ensues, the red elves will retreat fairly quickly even if they seem to be the stronger party. 

In that case Rubilla suggests that the adventurers let them go, claiming that the enemy is uncomfortable outside the crimson forest and therefore disinclined to fight. If the adventurers hunt them down, the red elves reluctantly allow themselves to be killed for the cause. 

**THE TRUTH:** The whole thing is an act to smuggle a set of prepared elven rubies to the Stillmist. There the rubies will grow into murderous freaks meant to kill the elven elders. The plan was devised by Vaerefor himself. 

The rubies look just like real elven rubies, albeit of fairly low quality. If they are brought to the Kogler in Tribolia, he cannot use them to craft new Astra and can immediately tell that they are fake. A peddler can also see that the rubies are of low quality. 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Forest, Crimson Forest, Hills, Mountains

## **27** THE  GUARDIAN TEMPLE 

_In the distance you see a cliff shaped like a 15­meter­ high ramp rising into the sky. At the top of the cliff is a statue of a horse that gazes out over the plains. At the foot of the cliff are a few small buildings, and beyond them some blue woods. Around the cliff and the temple runs a path that appears not to go any­ where, but forms a loop. Some humans on foot and horseback can be seen nearby, as well as some yurts._ 

This is the Guardian Temple of the Aslene riders, dedicated to horses and horsemanship. Youths from different clans often come here to learn and compete in horsemanship, and not least to court each other and have some fun. 

As a rule, there are about ten riders nearby, two of them experienced warriors and honor guards. Inside the small temple is a priest of Have named Valliman and his wife, the Horn priestess Insinera. In addition to the temple itself there is a smithy and a simple marketplace for exchanging goods in the area. There is also the Motherpine, an ancient blue pine that exudes high-quality blue tar which is collected by the priests. The adventurers can barter for blue tar at the temple, but it requires a significant donation or favor. 

The horse on the cliff is believed to be an old statue of Sella the Liberator’s horse, Wither beam. It is painted and in very good condition. Witherbeam is actually a fully functioning mechanical horse. See the Artifacts chapter (page 69). 

**TERRAIN TYPES:** Plains, Hills, Mountains, Ashlands

TAREGYLL<br>**----- End of picture text -----**<br>


**TYPE OF ADVENTURE SITE:** Settlement 

_You follow the brook upstream through a ravine whose sides rise steeper and steeper from the surrounding moor. The brook flows restlessly, its water so steam­ ing hot that veils of mist fill the ravine. Tall, blue pine trees line the road, and many of their trunks are smeared with tar. Soon you come upon the living dead – some of them sitting slumped on rocks and tree stumps, others leaning against a black building that seems to be their home while they wait for the grave._ 

_You proceed in silence, and soon the ravine opens into a long valley between the wooded ridges. Rows of houses emerge from the mist, some of them new and sloppily constructed, others built from ancient timber. The sounds of singing, hammering, and the slaughter of animals reach your ears. Even here among the living, the stench of tar and sulfur is omnipresent. The mist clears as you arrive and allows you to gaze beyond the wooden shacks. On_ 

_a hilltop at the far end of the valley you see some buildings of stone next to a large fire. People of dif­ ferent origins are going about their daily lives in the village, shamelessly sizing you up as they walk past._ 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## BACKGROUND 

The village of Taregyll is designed to be the players’ introduction to Aslene, with good opportunities to highlight some of the main conflicts in the country. 

Taregyll is situated on the slopes west of Shadowgate Pass which once connected Ravenland and Aslene. The original village, Gully, was for a long time a remote temple site where restless dead were laid to rest in the lava vents. A 

148 **chapter 11** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

temple to the fire goddess Have has stood here since time immemorial, with priests tending the kilns and the tar distillery in which the sacred tar of Have is produced from the local blue pines. 

Since the reopening of Shadowgate Pass, members of all the different kin have been showing up unexpectedly in the village – foreigners with unusual accents and tidings so strange that nothing surprises the locals anymore. All newcomers seem curious about the Bloodmarch and what it has to offer them. 

Two executioners’ guilds have smelled a business opportunity and started selling protection services. Taregyll has quickly become an almost lawless place of trade between the 

countries, for much is scarce in Aslene, while the craving for excitement, land, and ownerless treasures is great in Ravenland. The native population mainly consists of decent, religious mountain farmers and foresters who often disapprove of the foreign rabble coming and going in their village. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## GETTING HERE 

The player characters will likely reach Taregyll from Ravenland through Shadowgate Pass. They could also be native Aslenes, but in that case they will probably start the game closer to home. 

## **THE ORDER OF EGRESSION** 

In Aslene as well as in Ravenland, it is not uncommon for deceased individuals to linger on as restless dead. The Order of Egression is a loose gathering of necromancers, called Egressors, whose primary purpose is to help creatures pass from life to death. 

In the villages they visit, the Egressors are respected and feared in equal measure. They often spend their nights conversing with the restless dead in funeral grounds. They might also raise the actual dead if they sense unfinished business. These conversations often result in the dead coming to rest, but sometimes the Egressor realizes that certain matters must first be concluded. 

If the restless dead has been wronged in any way, the necromancer might exact revenge in their client’s name. 

In addition to their “clients,” Egressors often keep a retinue of undead who serve them as penance for their sins. People in Aslene generally believe that whoever kills or obstructs an Egressor will answer to the Nightwalker after their death. 

The Order of Egression serves the Nightwalker, but also honors the moon goddess Eor and Wyrm, whom they call “Eating Wyrm.” He is represented by the maggots feasting on the flesh of the dead, and Egressors carry such maggots with them to be ritually sprinkled on the dead they put to rest. 

149 

**taregyll** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

**SHADOWGATE PASS:** Shadowgate Pass is a desolate mountain pass between Ravenland and Aslene. The route was impassible only a few years ago, since the geometry itself was distorted, but during the Battle of Vond the pass lost its power and whatever guarded it disappeared. All that remains are creeping remnants of strange vegetation and odd little brooks flowing between bare rocks and persistent, sighing veils of mist. It is impossible to heal or sleep in the pass, which takes a Quarter Day to cross. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## LEGEND 

_It is said that Taregyll beyond Shadowgate Pass is the last stop of the dead, where demon­ tainted wretches come from all over Aslene to find peace in the lava. Some even say that the burning fissures are pathways between life and death._ 

_Visitors who have returned across the pass ­ claim that this is all nonsense meant to keep for eigners away from the riches that lie abandoned and ripe for the picking in the devastated western regions of Aslene._ 

_The red forests in those parts are scary alright, but mostly for demons and other unnatural filth, which makes it a good place for adventurers to prepare for expeditions into the unknown._ 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## LOCATIONS 

In addition to the settlements on the map, the area is home to a few farms with meager crops and sheep. Taregyll is surrounded by sparse 

coniferous forests of spruce and gray- blue pines, whose trunks have been smeared with tar to ward off demons. The village is located at high altitude in a fairly cool climate. The entire settlement smells of tar and sulfur, and is often shrouded in rain or fog. The locals are often completely or partially smeared with blue tar to protect against disease or possession. The tar is generally viewed as a universal remedy and does actually grant Armor Rating 2 against fear attacks and magic. 

## 1. THE HOUSE OF THE DEAD 

_Undead humans with empty stares are sitting or standing within a fenced area around a tarred log house. Runes have been painted in tar on their exposed body parts. Living loved ones are helping them and performing chores while shedding silent tears. Someone is playing a mournful tune on a large bronze horn._ 

Dead humans from all over Ashenvale are brought to Taregyll to be lowered into the lava further up the mountain or be taken to the Silent Tower at the Temple of Have (#14). The House of the Dead is the first stop. 

The place is run by Kurmena, a necromancer belonging to the Order of Egression (page 149). She speaks with the restless dead, clearing up uncertainties about their lives and preparing them for death. The tar symbols on the dead are notes written directly on the clients’ bodies to keep track of them and their stories – a common practice among Egressors. 

Relatives of the deceased traditionally help Kurmena with cooking, cleaning, and the like. People come and go in silence. Inside the house are compartments that look more like shelves than beds, 

150 **chapter 11** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

where the dead are stacked. Kurmena and Eisendor live in the small residential part of the building. 

Once a handful of living dead are ready, they are brought in solemn procession to the lava vents and pushed into them, followed by a few confused words from the Horn priest Strunde. Guests accompany the procession playing kettledrums and horns or singing laments. If the guests cannot play, it is usually handled by Captain Brock and Hergella of Madam Munna’s Inn. 

## KURMENA 

Kurmena is a stout, middle- aged woman in rough and practical clothes that smell of death. She has wispy blonde hair and does not seem to care much about her looks. Kurmena is calm and gentle in her work. At most she might express indignation with a twitch in her eyebrow. 

## **KURMENA** 

**STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 2, WITS 4, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Sleight of Hand 2, Move 1, Lore 3, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 

**TALENTS:** Death Magic 2 

**FEARLESS:** Egressors are immune to fear attacks from undead. 

**TRUE HEARING:** Egressors can automatically tell whether someone is telling the truth in matters relating to their clients, without making an INSIGHT roll. 

**SACRED MISSION:** While on a sacred mission, Egressors grow stronger and temporarily gain the FAST FOOTWORK talent (rank 3). Their conviction also makes them less vulnerable to magic and all forms of manipulation. The Power Level of all magic cast at Egressors is reduced by D3, and all attempts at MANIPULATION get a –2 penalty. 

**GEAR:** Staff, SIMPLE find. 

## EISENDOR 

The big, bald man Eisendor is Kurmena’s assistant and most resembles a sad dog with his sagging cheeks and red- rimmed eyes. He seems burdened by memories and torn between various duties. Eisendor dresses like a monk in black linen under which tattoos of Rust Church symbols can be glimpsed. 

## 2. THE TEMPLE OF HORN AND THE LAVA VENT 

_An old and worn­ down chapel is situated danger­ ously close to a couple of lava vents at the top of a hill. A stone ramp stretches over the lava. The hill is bare and windy, with no vegetation other than some heather and struggling shrubs._ 

Since Horn priests are required to build temples wherever lava sees daylight, a small chapel of stone has dutifully been raised next to the lava vents. No Horn priest would willingly serve in this godforsaken spot, but the horned dwarf Strunde has been transferred there as an alternative to being exiled in disgraced. The ramp over the lava can be lowered with a lever if the dead standing there should get cold feet and can then be winched back up again. 

## STRUNDE THE HORN PRIEST 

Strunde has broken his left horn, which is a source of unbearable shame for horned dwarves. Chunks of curly copper hair cover most of his body and he is missing several teeth. Strunde copes with the shame by drinking. When drunk he laughs, performs his duties paradoxically, and gets on people’s nerves. On rare occasions 

151 

**taregyll** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

of sobriety he trembles, hallucinates, and drags himself through life. 

given the chance to atone for their transgressions by paying fines to the guilds or performing services that someone else is willing to pay for.

## 3. THE EXECUTIONERS’ GUILD OF THE IRONCLAD 

_Outside a dilapidated old merchant house some spectacular soldiers are posing in a kind of grotesque parade armor made of shiny black leather, metal plates, and oversized shoulder pieces. With pomp­ ous faces, they are eying everyone who walks past._ 

## CAPTAIN BROCK 

Captain Brock is from Ravenland and careful to dress as a smart- looking officer. He trims his hair and beard every day and dyes it with black pomade that makes it gleam like his leather armor with silver buckles. It is unclear which side Brock fought on in Ravenland, and some say he deserted, but nobody dares to speak openly about it. 

## **CAPTAIN BROCK** 

**STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 3, WITS 3, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Marksmanship 1, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 

**GEAR:** Broadsword, shield, chainmail 

Taregyll’s two executioners’ guilds serve as the village’s law enforcement and judicial authorities. Guests and permanent establishments in the village usually pay one of the guilds for protection. Those who do not must fend for themselves. 

The guilds insult and shake fists at each other, but are actually working together under the secret leadership of Captain Brock. Brock wants two competing guilds to create some trouble and insecurity, which is good for business. All permanent residents know this, but no one talks about it. The guilds always join forces against external threats. 

The soldiers are mercenaries or troublemakers and are often replaced. Their equipment is mostly for show. It is not uncommon for caravan guards escorting travelers in the Bloodmarch to do some work for the guilds between assignments, which makes this a good place to ask for a guide. People who commit crimes in Taregyll are often 

## 4. TRADER’S HOLD 

_There are some surprisingly well­ stocked shops in the area west of the steaming brook that runs through the village._ 

The shops are called Trader’s Hold and have flourished in recent years as adventurers and settlers started coming in. The merchants at Trader’s Hold have all come from other parts of the world and can provide most of the wares listed in chapter 9 of the _Player’s Handbook_ . 

**CORIANDER’S GENERAL STORE:** Offers a wide variety of equipment. 

**BARTELLA BOLM’S STUFFERY:** Sells fresh, salted, and dried food, beverages, and healing herbs of varying quality. 

152 **chapter 11** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

**WELAND’S SMITHY:** Weland the dwarf crafts weapons as well as everyday tools which, while useful, are not very remarkable. He also makes bogus jewelry without much success. Unlike Aslene dwarves, Weland has no horns, but comes from the Meromannian clan in Ravenland. He supplies the Horn priest Strunde with liquor and in return he is allowed to use the lava outside the temple in his work, which more serious Horn priests would hardly allow. 

**PINDELLO’S DYEHOUSE:** Weaves, dyes, and sews, working in textiles as well as leather. Pindello comes from the refined Varina in the south and exaggerates his native accent. The dyeing is performed with colorants, alum, and ox bile by the warm stream of Galme. 

## 5. MADAM MUNNA’S INN 

_Below a chalk­ white slope stands a well­ kept log house. The gaps in the log walls are caulked with moss, the roof shingles beautifully installed, and the paved spot outside the doors is well swept. A middle­ aged woman with a pointy nose is standing by the door, eyeing everyone who passes by. She mumbles a prayer and fiddles with a couple of religious symbols._ 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Madam Munna and her daughters Sutia and Hergella, as well as a few tenants. 

## 6. FATHER’S POCKET 

_Between the shops and the stables stand the ruins of an old stone fortress that has been renovated and repurposed into a storage facility. The ground floor has half a dozen vaults with locked double doors. At one edge of the building is a small wooden annex where the midnight oil burns late into the night._ 

The fortress of Father’s Pocket once guarded the village’s western entrance, but was later razed by Galdanes. Frisikos Pyrra of Vivend bought it cheap when he arrived in Taregyll and turned it into a warehouse. It is possible to rent a secure space in the building where many of the merchants keep their stock. Frisikos is also the village banker and pawnbroker, and serves as bookkeeper and scribe whenever important documents are to be drawn up. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Frisikos Pyrra. 

Madam Munna is a religious widow who rents out rooms and other accommodations. Her labor force is made up of two browbeaten daughters who both dream of running away, but keep a close watch on each other so as not to get left behind. Munna is picky when it comes to her guests. She has a special fondness for priests and other godfearing people. It does not matter which god they worship, as long as they are true believers. The rooms are clean and the food is good. Revelry is forbidden and no alcohol is served. 

## 7. THE STABLES AND SLAUGHTERHOUSE 

_In the western end of the village, just before the narrow passage onto the moors, is a shapeless struc­ ture made up of interconnected stables and barns. From here comes the constant noise of various ani­ mals, mixed with death cries of slaughter. The smell of livestock, manure, and blood fills the air. Near the village’s western ridgeline is a meadow with paddocks and pens where cows, partridges, pigs, horses, and sheep are kept._

**taregyll** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The old farmer Traberos buys and sells animals, for both slaughter and utility. Here the adventurers can purchase horses or stable the ones they already have. But they will have to be careful, as the butcher Oxnesa has a feeble mind and sometimes has trouble distinguishing one animal from the other, or even a cow from a horse. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** The butcher Oxnesa and the old farmer Traberos and his farmhands.

## 8. THE TAREGYLL FENCES’ GUILD 

_In front of a stone house with barred windows hangs a gilded boot. No one knows why, since it is no cobbler’s shop. The door is locked and does not open until the owner has had a look at the visitor through a hatch._ 

The Taregyll Fences’ Guild will buy anything, no questions asked, particularly valuables, curiosities, and artifacts. The head of the guild, Antmar Stickyfingers, is always looking for information and desired items for prominent customers and can serve as an intermediary for various key players. 

10 

Every few months Stickyfingers sends a secret caravan of goods to collectors in Alderland. The caravans are led by the hunter and sorcerer Finezza via the Alder Road, the mine of Hode, and the Galder Gorge. 

## ANTMAR STICKYFINGERS 

Antmar Stickyfingers is a rat- like, middle- aged man with a pointy nose, sand- colored mustache, and greasy combed- back hair. He dresses well but not ostentatiously, talks fast, thinks quickly, and constantly shifts his eyes to take in everything of interest. Stickyfingers gives the immediate impression of being a bootlicking salesman, but it soon becomes

## **ANTMAR STICKYFINGERS** 

**STRENGTH 2, AGILITY 3, WITS 3, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Sleight of Hand 2, Lore 1, Insight 3, Manipulation 4 **GEAR:** Dagger, VALUABLE find 

Only the southern tower, the Tower of Horn, is still in use. Someone from the executioners’ guilds keeps watch in the night, partly looking for movement on the western moors, partly to detect any fires in the village. In case of danger, the watchman sounds the alarm on a hanging bronze cylinder. 

## 9. MEAGER MODLER’S TAVERN 

_The sound of laughter and revelry cascades from an old log building in the village square. Inside, people are dancing rhythmically to the screech of local musicians. The fast host stands howling on the stage with his tamed animals or moves among the guests, slapping backs and telling crude jokes_ 

Taregyll only has a single, fairly large tavern where wild times are had most evenings. Some competitors have tried to open other establishments, but vanished without a trace, in some cases after their homes burned down. The tavern’s proprietor, Modler, used to be an animal tamer and jester in Ravenland, which is why he likes to perform in the evenings with his dancing bear and goat. He is happy to hire visiting bards and storytellers. Bartella Bolm manages the kitchen in the evenings and Modler brews his own beer, which is drinkable but not very tasty. The furniture is as crude as the walls. 

## 10. THE TOWERS 

## OF HAVE AND HORN 

_Two crumbling watchtowers flank the ravine lead­ ing into the village. A statue once stood by the north tower. Its legs have broken off, but what remains_ 

## 11. THE SHACKS AND THE TABULA SLOPE 

_A row of sloppily assembled shacks and bunk­ houses has been built below the white limestone slope that makes up the southeastern wall of the ravine. During daytime this area bustles with life as various expeditions are being prepared. Strang­ ers of all kinds mingle with local craftsmen and inquisitive children._ 

It is possible to rent a shack owned by various people in the village: Madam Munna, Antmar Stickyfingers, Meager Modler, and the Merchants. The standard is atrocious with cold drafts, dirt floors, and flea- infested straw benches to sleep on. The prices are kept up by the competition and correspond to those listed in the _Player’s Handbook_ (page 180). 

## 12. JAGRA’S EXECUTIONERS’ GUILD 

_Outside the well­ timbered house at the north end of the village is a small arena with wooden benches._ 

Soldiers often spar here during the day and in the evenings bloodless fencing and wrestling 

156 **chapter 11** 

matches are arranged, with people placing bets on the winner. Visitors are welcome to participate. 

Jagra’s Executioners’ Guild is the second law enforcement organization in the village and officially competes with the Ironclad. The establishment is run by the heavily scarred war veteran Jagra of the Galdane rider clan, but everyone knows that she really works for Captain Brock. She introduces herself as a fencing teacher. 

The guards of Jagra’s guild all wear an ugly little tin brooch with a sword over a shield as badges of affiliation. They use their own equipment, which is less spectacular than the Ironclad uniforms. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Jagra, 6–8 soldiers. 

manageable sizes. The flour is always dark from the charcoal mixed into it, which is considered a local specialty. Behind the mill is a path up the mountain to the temple of Have.

## 14. THE FIRE TEMPLE OF HAVE 

_The great bonfire on the mountain burns with blue flames that cast their cold light on the masonry temple buildings where a few priests scurry qui­ etly between their duties. The priests of Have seem troubled and the looks they give you are more anxious than welcoming._

## 13. THE COAL MILL 

_The village mill straddles the stream of Galme, which turns its creaking water wheel throughout the day. Flocks of birds are constantly swarming in the steam around the area in search of spillage. A miller throws a stone at the birds and runs around with his cane._ 

Depending on the day, the mill grinds grain, nuts, or dried bulrush roots into flour, but also charcoaled logs which are broken into 

The blue flames are fetched directly from the holy fire constantly raging in the temple of Ashenstead on Mount Faltenvil, many days’ journey from here. Taregyll’s temple of Have has stood there since time immemorial to oversee the production of the sacred blue tar that the local pines give off when kilned. The temple was completely destroyed during the first wave of the Demon Flood, but the place was reclaimed as the blue tar turned out to be a potent weapon against demons. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Second Keeper Ansika, the priests Fursik, Dilak, and Anscha, and the Houn warriors Karnikos and Berhelma. 

## 15. THE SILENT TOWER 

_A thirty­ meter­ wide brick cylinder crowned by a wooden palisade dominates the mountaintop. The only entrance is locked with chains and a sturdy padlock. The foul stench of death surrounds the tower and carrion birds circle ceaselessly up above. Moving closer, you can hear faint groans and fleshy thuds coming from inside._

**taregyll** 

On the tables of the Silent Tower, dead bodies have since ancient times been exposed to birds until only the skeletons remain, but no corpses are received there anymore. When the First Keeper of the temple, Gest, became demon- tainted, he had himself locked up in the tower, and now the growing demonic mass threatens to burst its walls. 

## ✥ **CREATURES:** The demonic mass Gest, sometimes one of the priests of Have.

## **GEST** 

## **STRENGTH 20, AGILITY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Scouting 3 

**ARMOR RATING:** 8 (demon skin) 

**FOUL:** Gest’s shape and stench are so foul that everyone within ARM’S LENGTH must succeed with an ENDURANCE roll at the start of their initiative (not an action) or get a –1 penalty to all actions on their next turn. **WEAKNESS:** Holy fire causes Gest to retreat. 

**MONSTER:** In combat Gest counts as a monster (see page 73 in the _Gamemaster’s Guide_ ). 

## **MONSTER ATTACKS** 

- **D6 MONSTER ATTACK** 

- 1 **TENTACLE LASH!** Half a dozen tentacles shoot out from the demonic mass and hit D3 adventurers within NEAR range. Roll an attack with six Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force) against each target. The attack can be parried. Victims damaged by the attack are knocked down. 

## **D6 MONSTER ATTACK** 

- 2 **ABSORB FLESH!** The demonic mass grows around an adventurer within ARM’S LENGTH in an attempt to absorb the victim. Roll for the attack with eight Base Dice and Weapon damage 1 (blunt force). If hit, the adventurer is grappled and trapped in the demonic mass and takes one point of damage to STRENGTH each turn until the adventurer breaks free. Gest has STRENGTH 10 during attempts to break free. 

- 3 **EXPLODING GROWTH!** Gest’s pulsating skin swells into growths which immediately explode in a cascade of flesh and blood. All adventurers within NEAR range suffer an attack with nine Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. The attack deals damage to EMPATHY rather than STRENGTH , and armor does not protect against it. 

- 4 **DESTROY WEAPON!** The mass grows and forms a hammer- like arm that strikes at an adventurer within NEAR range. Roll for the attack with seven Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). If the attack inflicts damage, the adventurer is disarmed. The hammer arm absorbs the weapon and crushes it into pieces. 

- 5 **PUPPET!** Human shapes form in the demonic mass, dangling from strings like puppets. D3+1 shapes are formed and sent to attack the adventurers. The shapes cannot move further than ARM’S LENGTH from Gest. The puppets have STRENGTH 2, AGILITY 1, MELEE 1, and punch for Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). 

- 6 **FLESH BOMB!** Two orbs the size of human heads shoot out in a sticky cascade from the demonic mass and land near two different adventurers. The orbs grow and explode as they are squashed by the connective tissue. Adventurers within ARM’S LENGTH of an orb suffer an attack with eight Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (stab wound), while everyone within NEAR range suffers an attack with six Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (stab wound).

## 16. TAR DISTILLERIES 

_Smoke drifts steadily through the pine trees, around charcoal kilns and brick tar pots. The smell is pungent and causes the unaccustomed to cough. Logs are sorted by tar yield in different stacks and new logs are constantly being added from sledges pulled by massive work horses. Some thirty men and women live and work at the kilns. A primitive rope bridge is strung across the ravine._ 

Here charcoal is kilned and blue tar boiled. In the final years before they are felled, the blue pines around Taregyll are treated to yield more tar. 

grabs them. A seemingly restless dead (page 122 in the _Gamemaster’s Guide_ ) tries to say something, but fails to utter an intelligible word. The creature seems afraid and is very persistent. Finally he manages to say “ _help._ ” 

With the relevant knowledge, the adventurer realizes that the hand is not cold with undeath, but rather with fever. The rust priests have drugged an Alderlander Wyrm priest serving under Poansa Moulter and hidden him among the living dead to be taken away and interrogated at a later time. ## 17. THE STREAM OF GALME 

_The stream that runs through Taregyll is steaming hot and reeks of sulfur. Red fire fish are sometimes seen shimmering in the water and flare up as they jump into the air._ 

The Stream of Galme springs from the underground a couple of hundred meters north of the village. Its water is undrinkable but used for a variety of other purposes, such as cooking, dyeing textiles, and washing laundry. The fire fish in the stream are delicious, but devoted to Horn and protected from fishing by law. They can only live in hot water. It is impossible to wade through the water without taking damage, which is why simple bridges have been constructed over the stream.

## ROCKSLIDE 

A major rockslide is suddenly triggered as the limestone is separated from the ground of the Tabula Slope. Several houses are brought down with it. Any adventurers inside the houses or at the bottom of the slope must succeed at a MOVE roll or suffer an attack with six Base Dice (blunt force). 

Witnesses to the event later claim to have seen the dwarven priest Strunde at the top of the slope before the rockslide, and it is soon concluded that the dwarf must have triggered the rockslide on purpose through magma singing. An angry mob gathers outside the temple of Horn to lynch Strunde. The dwarf senses danger and tries to hide. With a successful LORE roll, the adventurers realize that magma song does not even work on limestone. 

## EVENTS 

Here are a number of events that might occur in Taregyll. 

## BACK FROM THE DEAD 

One of the adventurers is startled by a hand that suddenly reaches out from a dark corner and 

## THE WRATH OF RUBOR 

A group of red elves (page 129) attacks Taregyll to burn down this temple of evil dedicated to the impure manifestations of Kolor, the multicolored god. Unless they are stopped, the elves will rout the guards before setting fire to anything that will burn – including the blue pines around the village. 

159 

**taregyll** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

A captured red elf will ramble about the prophet Vaerefor in holy Reiben and spew religious nonsense about the god Rubor and his enemy Kolor. These fanatics are insensitive to pain and impervious to reason, and would gladly become martyrs. 

This event can occur multiple times, each time with a larger force of red elves, and could eventually result in a large- scale assault on the village. 

## ELF AGAINST ELF 

A battle involving elves breaks out near Taregyll and the adventurers. It is a clash between red elves are redrunners, where the latter weep while fighting because they are so upset by the fanaticism of their fellow elves. 

Redrunner patrols are positioned near Taregyll to capture red elves, either alive or by cutting out their rubies and bringing them back to the Stillmist where the scholars can analyze what has happened. This time the hunted red elf received reinforcements and fighting ensued. 

The redrunners have spoken with the priests of Have, but not with anyone else in the village. They might collaborate with villagers if it suits their own purposes, but do not really care about the fate of the village. 

## MURDER, MADAM! 

The player characters find Madam Munna murdered; she is hanging by her feet with her torso in the boiling hot Stream of Galda from one of the washing jetties outside Pindello’s Dyehouse. There are a pair of thin and well- worn leather gloves not far from the corpse. Her daughter Sutia asks the adventurers to catch the killer. 

Captain Brock is also summoned to investigate the matter since Madam Munna was under 

the protection of Jagra’s Executioners’ Guild. But Sutia does not trust Brock since he asked both her and her late mother to marry him. 

There is a rumor in the village that Madam Munna was planning to open a high- quality tavern at her inn called “the Finehouse,” which would not go down very well with Meager Modler. Another rumor suggests that Antmar Stickyfingers wants to take over the lucrative inn business in the village by both overt and covert means. 

The truth is that Bartella Bolm is sick of Meager Modler and wants to open her own establishment. She knows that the innkeeper would sabotage her plans and is trying to get rid of him by framing him for the murder. 

## THE VASNIANS’ DEAD 

A large group of Vasnians (page 19) arrives from the north, escorted by three hired orc warriors. A necromancer of the Order of Egression has told them that a restless dead chieftain will not come to rest until he is placed in the Silent Tower like in the olden days. The Vasnians are very persistent. 

The priests of Have refuse to see them or open the tower, and the atmosphere becomes increasingly hostile. Finally the delegation decides to take matters into their own hands and breaks into the tower. They might try to enlist the player characters as additional muscle. If you want, Konana (page 91) could be their leader. 

If the situation turns violent and the Vasnians lose or are thrown out, they might return later on with a larger force to take revenge. If the Vasnians manage to break into the Silent Tower, the priests’ secret about the demon- tainted First Keeper will be revealed. See the event “The Flowing First Keeper.” 

160 **chapter 11** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## A MEETING OF RUSTY LINKS 

A mysterious group of quiet strangers who show clear signs of being Rust worshipers gathers in the village and rents one of the houses below the Tabula Slope which they then fortify and secure with guards. Then a couple of covered wagons arrive, whose passengers discreetly enter the house. 

The Rust Churches of Ravenland and Alderland have convened in Taregyll to negotiate an alliance and make plans for the future. Colonel Grudenstaal is there, preferably along with some prominent Rust worshiper from Ravenland whom the adventurers have previously encountered and perhaps thought to be dead. 

## DEMON CATCH 

Three demons, chained and smeared with blue tar, are brought into the village under heavy guard by a group of Galdane riders, to be sacrificed to Horn in the lava vents the next day. The demons are cursing and shouting obscenities. 

One of them can read minds, in a primitive sort of way, and reveals embarrassing secrets about the spectators. The same demon offers the adventurers important information if they help him escape, and can give them a small sample. Choose some suitable clues it can give the adventurers in return for their help. It might be allied with Vaerefor van Reiben or know that Krasylla is alive in the magma and yearning to be free. The demons will be kept at Father’s Pocket for the night. 

## THE PENITENT IN THE MAGMA 

One of the adventurers dreams of a creature down in the magma. The creature tells the adventurer to buy some liquor for the Horn priest Strunde, 

wait until he is blind drunk, and take him to the lava vents. There Krasylla introduces himself (see random event #8 on page 135), but realizes that Strunde’s body is not good enough for him, since the dwarf is not properly ordained. The demon wants another Horn priest. 

## THE FLOWING FIRST KEEPER 

The adventurers are passing near the Silent Tower when one of the doors flings open and demonic tissue comes flowing out! Villagers and Horn priests hurry there to fight the demonic mass and force it back into the tower. Finally the Horn priest Strunde comes running with a lantern of holy fire and manages to push the demon back inside – at the cost of the lantern going out. 

Now the Horn priests have no holy fire in Taregyll, and Second Keeper Ansi must urgently send someone to Ashenstead temple on Mount Faltenvil to fetch holy fire in a special lantern, which she hands to the adventurers if they accept the mission. The lantern is actually very useful to adventurers as its holy fire turns blue tar into an even stronger weapon against demons (page 38). The adventurers may decide to keep it, but would then leave the priests of Taregyll to their fate. 

Unless something is done (whenever you deem appropriate), the demonic mass Gest will cause the Silent Tower to burst. At that point the demon weighs several tons and is completely shapeless, but will continue to grow by absorbing animals and other organic life that comes close enough. After a week or two of freedom it will slide down into the village like a disgusting avalanche of flesh and gradually occupy the entire valley. The demonic mass can be stopped if the adventurers return with holy fire in time.

ASHENSTEAD<br>**----- End of picture text -----**<br>


**TYPE OF ADVENTURE SITE:** Settlement 

_Your eyes rejoice at the sight of a distant mountain that breaks the monotony of the Aslene plains. It rises like a massive anvil, crowned by its flat top and with its horn pointing east. Only when you get closer do you realize how high and virtually impregnable the mountain really is, and yet there is a climbing route winding its way to the top from the village at its base. Lots of people and horses seem to be moving about the village._ 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## BACKGROUND 

Ashenstead is situated on Faltenvil, a mountain whose silhouette abruptly breaks up the surrounding plains. Below is the village of Dunfalt 

with its tent camps and pastures. When the adventurers first arrive, the village is full of visiting Aslenes – mainly Sabirians and Quards – who have come to attend the wedding of their chieftains’ children. 

Faltenvil is the holy mountain of the horse clans, a place of unity and the center of their worship of Horn and Have, as well as a center of Aslene learning. Here burns the original holy fire of Have and blue tar is purified to the highest quality. Violence is forbidden both on the mountain and in the village, which also applies to people heading there on religious errands with their weapons tucked away in their knapsacks. 

Each month a market is held in the village at the foot of the mountain, where horses and other valuable goods are bought and sold. 

162 **chapter 12** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## **THE PYROLYTES** 

The Pyrolytes are warrior priests within the Church of Have, sworn to purge all unworldly elements from existence with their holy fire, especially demons. Pyrolytes are often unreasonable fanatics and pyromaniacs with a tendency to set things on fire without second thought. They view fire as the cure for all ills, including diseases, delusions, and toxins in food and water. 

Many Pyrolytes have disfiguring scars from self- inflicted burns, since they think they can predict and influence the future by reading and shaping the hypertrophic scars from deep 

burn wounds – that is, the resulting contractions, discolorations, and ridges. They alter the scars by having pearls and silver thread embedded in the wound. 

Such scars are seen as living maps of the future and studied carefully by Pyrolyte scholars. Famous scars are flayed from the body after the bearer’s death, framed, and put on display in the Pyrolyte temple as guidance and inspiration for future generations. Naturally, the burn and its healing process must be endured without betraying the slightest emotion to be considered a valid document. 

Councils and negotiations, as well as duels and weddings, are traditionally held on the faces – the mountain’s three plateaus. Strangers are welcome as Faltenvil is a mountain of peace, but they better keep a low profile. 

All ordained priests at the temple are married couples where one spouse worships Horn and the other Have, but these roles are not gender specific. Novices are paired up by the high priests as they take their vows. 

In hopeless situations it is considered honorable to choose death by throwing oneself off the mountain, and to place one’s dead on the mountain’s plateaus to be eaten by the birds. Other solutions are often sought for the restless 

dead, as getting them up on the mountain is difficult, and often dangerous, work. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## GETTING HERE 

The adventurers can either come upon Mount Faltenvil by accident on their travels through the Aslene plains or go there deliberately, for example to seek knowledge and advice at the temple, fetch holy fire, or attend a council. They might also encounter and accompany one of the horse clans’ wedding processions on their way to Faltenvil.

## LEGEND 

_When Horn wished to forge the holy Astra as weapons for Sella the Liberator to use against the Vasnian hordes, the volcano god raised Mount Fal­ tenvil like an anvil from the plains and summoned his blacksmith Ungule from deep beneath the earth. On the face of Faltenvil the Astra were forged, but so great was the heat of the smoldering blacksmith that none other than Horn himself could assist him, and so bright was the forge’s light that no one_ 

_could even look at the mountain. But once Ungule returned underground and the mountain cooled, the horse clans built the temple of Ashenstead on its summit so the priests could watch over the country and safeguard its unity. So arduous was Ungule’s work that his hands were blistered and bloodied, and his blue blood remained on the plains. Horn’s wife, Have, gathered the blood in a bucket and used it to water her garden so that it would not go to waste, and from it grew the blue pines of the Bloodmarch, whose sap becomes holy blue tar._

## LOCATIONS 

Described below are the most important sites around Ashenstead. 

## 1. DUNFALT VILLAGE 

_At the foot of the mountain is a village with a few stone buildings and a small number of permanent residents. You see tents and yurts pitched around the village. Lots of riders are moving about and the place seems to be decorated for festivities. The vil­ lage is surrounded by pastures full of grazing horses._ 

The village has every horse- related facility one could wish for, such as blacksmiths, veterinarians and butchers (often the same person), stables, and feeding barns. There is also a well, a general store, and the Horn’s Hammer inn which is run by the wealthy Morvandos Mormer from the clan in whose territory the mountain is located. Most of the guests are riders from clans Sabira and Quard, as well as temple novices and pilgrims. Each clan has its own tent camp outside the village. Rough games, drinking bouts, and riding competitions are always going on among them. Strangers are tolerated, but better watch their tongue. Jesters, musicians, and traders are also found among the more or less festive clan riders. 

## 2. VERGA’S SLOPE 

_The trail winds its way up the steep slope of Mount Faltenvil through the haze of low clouds._ 

The mountain trail leading up to Faltenvil’s plateaus is called Verga’s Slope after a Sabirian chieftain who was forced to crawl like a dog all the way to the top to placate the gods after breaking an oath. After this ordeal, Verga supposedly had the more difficult stretches reinforced and leveled. 

The hike is roughly five kilometers with a total ascent of 800 meters. Completing it in a Quarter Day requires a successful ENDUR ANCE roll, otherwise it takes two Quarter Days. Horses cannot make it at all, except for the Sabirian wingsteeds. 

Closer to the top the trail runs through a ravine which at night, or in case of danger, is blocked by log gates that can be quickly hoisted open or shut. On the other side, the trail branches off to the forested plateaus. 

## 3. THE COUNCIL FACE 

_A completely flat and almost polished­ looking surface where negotiations and councils are held between groups from the horse clans._ 

This is the lowest of the three plateaus known as the faces. It is customary for visitors to enter the Council Face naked before the gods – that is, without weapons or other types of gear. Caberians tend to remove their clothes as well and sit fully naked on the windswept rock, shivering but stoic. Duels to the death are sometimes fought on the Council Face, in which case the high priest grants an exemption to weapons ban.

## 4. THE HORN FACE 

_The wedge­ shaped plateau points to the east, cov­ ered in bushes, herbs, and windswept trees. In the middle of the plateau is a ten­ meter­ high step pyra­ mid of stone with an open pavilion at the top. A few wooden shacks have been built near the pyramid._ 

skin is lined and tanned like old leather under a mop of dark hair, and his bright eyes are as sharp as a sword. The leader of the powerful and warlike Caberian clan is used to taking what he wants and does not back down from anyone. On the contrary, he likes to provoke people. 

The Horn Face is not named after the volcano god Horn, but is so called because it represents the horn of an anvil. On the plateau are some simple shacks where pilgrims and other guests spend the night, but normally there is no food or service available. The entire platform is now decorated for the upcoming wedding and people are kind and generous with their supplies, even to strangers.

## HIGH PRIEST LIKLAUDOS 

The fair- haired and fair- bearded high priest Liklaudos seems quite content with his life at Ashenstead. Mild- tempered and diplomatically inclined, he is used to mediating between the hotheaded horse clans. He is an easy man to like, although he talks a bit too much and too preachily about life. Liklaudos wears a simple, light- colored cowl painted with the symbols of Horn in the national colors. His wife is Prunofilia, a priestess of Have.

## SILTELLOS, SON OF MERDEKAI 

Siltellos, son of Merdekai, is a weak and curlyhaired young man who is utterly oppressed by his dominant father and therefore barely has the courage to look other people in the eye. At the same time he knows what is required of him and does his best to appear strong, which gives a conflicting and nervous impression.

## MOMMODAR THE RODENT 

Mommodar, chieftain of the Sabirians, was once a powerful warrior, but has now grown old and fat. He is still considered one of the clan’s shrewdest minds, hence his nickname: 

166 **chapter 12**

the Rodent. Mommodar is a stout man and usually gives a genial, somewhat grandfatherly impression, but his calculating gaze is always there beneath the surface, and Mommodar never loses his cool or composure.

## GERBERA 

Mommodar’s daughter Gerbera is considered a dark beauty, which she herself does not want to hear about. On the contrary, she lives for her horses and dresses like a man to escape the expectations of being a decorative flower. 

## **GERBERA** 

## **STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 4, WITS 3, EMPATHY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Marksmanship 1, Move 2, Animal Handling 2 

The ancient tower is built on a cliff overhanging a chasm hundreds of meters deep. According to legend, the priest Kardenas watched Sella’s decisive battle against the Vasnians from the tower. From there he also saw the demon horde pour into the Bloodmarch from Shadowgate Pass three hundred years ago. Melgir, high priestess of Have, often comes here to sing hymns from the tower. 

## MELGIR SKYLARK 

High Priestess Melgir Skylark is a singer as well as a skilled rider, even though she has yet to reach the age of thirty. As a priestess she is a highly valued advisor and quickly gains people’s trust through her intelligence and perceptiveness. Melgir is married to the Horn priest Tordenost. 

## 6. THE STRIKE FACE 

_On the largest plateau of the mountain you are met by a vile stench of dead bodies, and flocks of carrion birds are cawing angrily from the bare, northern part of the plateau where a few sheds can be seen. To the west, beyond a small pool of water, rises the highest face of the mountain with the temple com­ plex and a blue fire._ 

**TALENTS:** Horseback Fighter 2 

**GEAR:** Short spear, short bow, leather armor, riding horse 

## 5. KARDENAS’ WATCH 

_On the edge of the Horn Face’s eastern tip a tower balances over the abyss. A lovely female voice is heard from the top of the tower, singing so loudly that it reaches the ground on the leeward side of the mountain._ 

In the northern part of the Strike Face, corpses are being eaten by the carrion birds constantly squawking and squabbling in the area. The bodies are stored and prepared in the sheds. A short, rotund priest is running around chasing all the birds with red feathers, as they are not deemed worthy of the feast. In contrast, the rest of the face is very beautiful with creeping trees and bushes and flourishing flowers, berries, and fruits. The gnawed- up skeletons are pulverized and used as fertilizer for the plants. 

167 

**ashenstead** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## 7. THE FIRE FACE 

_Out of the western part of the Strike Face rises another plateau with the temple complex and a beautiful blue fire. Besides the priests, there are a few slender human­ like creatures on the plateau. There seems to be a masonry pool further down on the south side of the plateau._ 

Perched on the Fire Face is the temple Ashenstead – the nation’s center for worship of Horn and Have. Blue tar is hauled here to be purified and made more potent at the temple. This is also where the holy blue fire of Have burns, which is ritually brought from here to temples of Have all over Aslene. Three vazuls – specially initiated priestesses of Have – are responsible for keeping the fire burning. 

## BLAUDER OF HAVE 

Blauder of Have and other prunes look like the blue- black remains of a human corpse left to dry in the sun for a month or two. The flesh on the emaciated body is drawn tightly over its bones, and the creature has glossy orbs of obsidian for eyes. He is surrounded by the smell of turpentine. For stats, see page 104. 

## 8. THE TEMPLE GATE 

_A three­ meter­ high wall surrounds the temple area. Standing on either side of the double gate are blue marble statues of Horn and Have. Inside the gate are grass and some free­ range chickens and geese._ 

The wall is not for defense, since it is assumed that no foe will be able to reach this far up the mountain. All buildings are made of stone with roofs of straw or wood. At night the gate is closed and, like during the day, guarded by an honor guard from one of the horse clans. 

## 9. STABLES 

_You hear animal sounds coming from a stable inside the temple gate and smell a strange animal scent._ 

Inside the stable are a handful of mountain gnus – large climbing goats – which are used to transport goods up the mountain. They cannot be ridden. There are also three donkeys pulling mills and such. These were carried here as foals and cannot be brought down from the mountain. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Mountain gnus, donkeys, chickens, and geese. 

## 10. LABORATORY 

_To the left of the temple gate is a building from which unpleasant wails and acrid odors seep out. The entrances are guarded by a couple of strange human­ like creatures. They are tall and wiry with dark blue skin and almost no facial features at all._ 

The alchemist Mongelo lives and works at the laboratory. He supervises the purification of blue tar and conducts experiments, including the transformation of captured red elves into prunes. Only he and the Horn priest Tordenost are allowed access, except for the temporary assistants who are sometimes called in. 

The northern part of the building has three cells, one of which holds a captured red elf in poor condition. The southern part is the laboratory itself, with vials and containers of glass and copper. On a shelf are jars of blue tar of various qualities. The alchemist sleeps on a simple cot standing in a corner. Outside, standing by the southern wall, is a large copper still that steams and splutters. This is where Mongelo is refining blue tar.

## THE ALCHEMIST MONGELO 

Mongelo lives for alchemy and could not care less about his appearance. His curly hair is therefore a constant mess and his teeth are corroded by the sweet roots that give him energy during long nights of work. Mongelo wears a black cowl, pockmarked and blemished by sparks and acids from his art.

**TALENTS:** Poisoner 2, Alchemist 2 (see _Book of Beasts_ ) 

**GEAR:** Dagger, SIMPLE find, leather pouch of sweet roots

THE HORN PRIEST TORDENOST The Horn priest Tordenost has short dark hair, a beard, and a gaze so intense that it is said to burn with the fire of Horn when he gets angry. He is very devoted to his magma singing and likes to practice it openly to impress those around him. Tordenost is married to High Priestess Melgir Skylark. 

## **TORDENOST** 

## **STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 3, WITS 4, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 1, Lore 4, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 

**TALENTS:** Magma Song 3 

Prayer and worship are held in temple hall every morning and afternoon, with participants seated on cushions on the floor. There is a horned dwarf lying on his stomach in front of the altar, day and night, when he is not kissing visitors’ feet. To the left is a staircase leading up to the vazul quarters where no man who is not a priest may enter. The red and yellow paintings turn out to be burn scars flayed from the skin of dead pyrolytes. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Novices, a horse warrior from any clan. 

**GEAR:** Warhammer, SIMPLE find 

## 13. VAZUL QUARTERS 

## 11. KITCHEN AND DINING ROOM 

_The pleasant fragrance of meat stew comes seep­ ing from this building with a fuming smoke hole, mixing with the other smells of the temple court­ yard. Outside is a bloodstained chopping block. Herbs have been hung to dry under the roof._ 

This is the temple’s kitchen. There is a table with enough space for a few people, but the novices usually grab a bowl of food and go somewhere else to eat. The temple chef, Master Kolme, is most interested in his work and likes to swap recipes as well as ingredients and favors. 

## 12. TEMPLE HALL 

_You enter a comfortably warm temple hall with wooden pillars and bast cushions on the floor for visi­ tors to sit on. The walls are covered with ornaments as well as horse skins and weapons. Abstract paintings in red and yellow are hung here and there. Muffled singing is heard from some praying novices. A horse warrior seeks spiritual guidance by one of the walls._ 

_Upstairs is the residence of three women who do not speak to strangers. A novice comes running to admonish anyone who goes near the staircase. They explain that the vazuls are tending the sacred fire on the roof of the temple and must not be disturbed._ 

The vazuls are three holy women who devote their entire lives to the sacred fire of Have. Allowing the fire to go out would cost them their lives. They live in celibacy and speak only to the priests. On the roof outside their quarters, above the temple gate, the blue flames of the sacred fire burn on logs of golden oak. Golden oak is a wood that burns for a very long time and is completely consumed by the flames. 

✥ **CREATURES:** The nameless vazuls. 

## 14. TEMPLE GARDEN 

_In the middle of the temple is a beautiful pond with blue carps. It is lined with well­ trimmed bushes and herbs._ 

170 

**chapter 12** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The temple garden is managed by Chef Kolme who grows his spices here. The fish may only be eaten during the New Year’s meal. Priests and novices often sit on the garden benches in prayer or quiet conversation. It is a peaceful place. 

## 15. STORE ROOM 

_A space with tools and barrels of blue tar._ 

A couple of gliding suits are also stored here. These allow messengers to throw themselves off the mountain and get a quick start on their journey. When necessary, a gliding suit can also be used to quickly flee the temple. 

## 16. WASH HOUSE 

_By the stream beyond the wash house some logs have been set up where the temple residents relieve them­ selves into the water below._ 

_their toes as revenge for disturbing them. The far wall of the room is adorned with a cracked and crumbled fresco. It depicts a rider on horseback._ 

The library contains educational literature, religious observations and instructions, historical accounts, studies of nature, and some poetry. 

The fresco depicts Sella the Liberator equipped with Horn’s Astra, the weapons he received from Horn to free the country. For more information, see page 62. 

The four most senior priests know all the legends about the Astra, except the cost of using them. They all agree that Aslene must be united under Selligar Horne of Havenmark to drive off the forces threatening the country. To accomplish this, the Astra must be found and handed to Selligar, for together they become an invincible weapon that turns their bearer into a divine being. 

If the adventurers seem worthy of their trust, the priests also explain that the Astra can be identified and claimed by smearing them with blue tar. 

The wash house gets its water upstream of the logs. 

## PRUNOFILIA 

## 17. THE NOVICES’ HALL 

_­ An oblong wooden annex that is reached by a stair case in the garden._ 

This is the novices’ dormitory, where guests can also be accommodated if extra space is needed. 

Prunofilia, priestess of Have, is a thin woman with long dark hair that almost covers her large, piercing eyes. She seems reluctant to open her tight- lipped mouth, whether for food or conversation. She is deeply religious and goes about her duties with downcast eyes. Prunofilia wears a kimono- like robe of silk embroidered with greenery – passed down from her predecessor. She is married to High Priest Liklaudos. 

## 18. LIBRARY 

_The somewhat cluttered room smells of mold, sweat, and ink. Its walls are covered in shelves filled with scrolls and tomes. A group of novices are standing by some lecterns, copying or studying various texts, while tiny book scorpions scurry about and pinch_ 

## **PRUNOFILIA** 

**STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 3, WITS 4, EMPATHY 5 SKILLS:** Lore 3, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 **TALENTS:** Magma Song 2 

**GEAR:** Staff, PRECIOUS find

## 19. BED CHAMBER 

_A modest room with a large double bed. In a corner stands a locked bookcase. The door is locked during the day._ 

This is where Melgir Skylark and Tordenost sleep. The bookcase contains the apocryphal scriptures of Horn which only Liklaudos and Melgir may read. Hanging over the bed are the symbol of Have crafted in silver and a framed piece of human skin with an impressive burn scar – a gift from a nameless pilgrim. 

## 20. DORMITORY AND MEETING ROOM 

_Behind the door is a table with eight high chairs. In the back of the room are some beds and a couple of desks._ 

This is where the priests sleep. The large wall inside the door is painted with a general map of central Aslene. There is a stove to keep the priests warm during the winter season. 

## 22. POOL 

_A stairway leads from the temple garden down the rock to a ledge whose edges have been built up into a pool. The pool is full of blue­ black tar. There are novices singing songs and stirring with large spatulas. A crevice leads into a cave with more tubs of blue tar._ 

The pool is where the blue tar from the kilns in the forests is prepared and sanctified. The purified tar is then poured into barrels or refined further at Master Mongelo’s laboratory. The priests believe that the process must take place up in the clear air at the temple. Beyond the pool is a precipice of great depth. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Novices and possibly the alchemist Mongelo.

## EVENTS 

Described below are some events that might occur at Ashenstead. 

## 21. BED CHAMBER 

_A beautiful room with window shutters facing south. There is a large double bed and shelves with texts, as well as a small meeting table with two chairs._ 

This is where Liklaudos and Prunofilia sleep. The room also serves as the temple’s administrative office, and there is often a scribe sitting here during the day. Above the bed hangs the symbol of Horn in hammered silver. 

## THE BOOK OF WINDS 

The priestess and librarian Prunofilia timidly approaches the adventurers. Since she never leaves the temple herself, Prunofilia asks the guests to retrieve the Book of Winds which is said to be found at the Watch of the Sisters (page 207), an ancient temple to the goddess Wail. Old writings describe the Book of Winds as the oldest book in Aslene, dating back to the time when the horse clans first came to be. The 

172 **chapter 12** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

book defines their mission and explains why the route to the Drawn Lands is blocked by a fierce hurricane. The adventurers will be richly rewarded if they find the Book of Winds and bring it to Ashenstead. Prunofilia can point out the approximate location of the Watch of the Sisters on their map. 

## THE SLIME SEEKER 

The alchemist Mongelo could use some red matrix, the red slime in which the crimson forest grows, for analysis. He asks the adventurers to bring him some in a container he hands them. If they would also bring back one of the slime- producing snails, he would be beyond grateful. In return he offers them his knowledge, as a teacher or practitioner. 

The container Mongelo gives them seems always to be cold inside and is in fact a very useful vessel for cooling beverages. 

## THE GIFTED CLOAK 

The Sabirian chieftain Mommodar tried to invite Selligar Horne of Havenmark to his daughter’s wedding, but sadly the messengers could not find him since Selligar is always on the move. Now the chieftain asks if the adventurers would be so kind as to deliver a gift to Selligar, as is tradition, since they travel around so much anyway and will no doubt encounter the Houn chieftain sooner or later. Perhaps 

they already have? They will be given a good horse or gold for their trouble. 

The gift is a splendidly woven cloak, folded and wrapped in golden rope held together by what Mommodar claims to be the Sabirian seal. A simpler fabric shields it from dust and dirt. 

The cloak is in fact poisoned and intended to kill Selligar, since neither Sabirians nor Caberians wish to unite under that upstart. A person versed in clan customs ( LORE roll) knows that there is no such tradition of giving gifts and that the Sabirians have no seal. 

## FIREBRANDS 

One night after their arrival, the adventurers hear muffled screams coming from the vazul quarters and can see shadows chasing each other up there. It is the prune Blauder who has decided to attack along with a number of other prunes. They are on their way to the sacred fire on the roof of the temple and are about to strangle the novice and the vazuls trying to stop them. 

Blauder’s plan is to push the burning golden logs into the pool of blue tar on the southern side of the temple. That would start a great fire that could easily spread and cause explosions. The temple would lose their stores of blue tar and the sacred fire would go out. It takes a couple of months to fetch new fire, which must be lit on the volcano Horn, and just as long to transport a batch of blue tar to the temple from Taregyll or some other place.

SALTERSTAY<br>**----- End of picture text -----**<br>


**TYPE OF ADVENTURE SITE:** Settlement 

_Traveling through the Bloodmarch, you notice a strange sight in the distance. After a heavy rain the end of a vibrant rainbow shoots up from an area of mixed crimson forest, but instead of arching across the sky as one would expect, it writhes like a tortured serpent with its free end flailing among the clouds. There is a village nearby and some mine adits can be seen in the mountainside. Between the village and the rainbow runs a stream that flows into a lake._ 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## BACKGROUND 

Vaerefor van Reiben presents himself as the prophet and leader of the Aslene red elves, but is really a demon seeking to paint the whole world red. Now his loyal red elves have trapped one end 

of a giant rainbow through rune singing. Vaerefor is on his way there with his moving castle Kasabatoda to paint the rainbow completely red with the creature’s slime, thinking that doing so will permanently damage the other colors so that all rainbows in the sky will be red from then on. 

The captured rainbow has come to the attention of the Order of Egression who believe that it is a bridge by which dead souls can cross over to the underworld and that it must therefore be preserved. A group of drakewyrms are equally upset, since they view the rainbow as their god Tincturus who must be saved. Moreover, rumors of a treasure at the end of the rainbow are attracting all manner of scum to the area. The red elves and their sarcoptes guard the place against intruders while awaiting their master’s arrival. 

174 

**chapter 13** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## GETTING HERE 

The player characters could come upon Salterstay by accident or meet someone who is headed there after hearing about the captured rainbow. It might be Egressors, Zhozhach’s drakewyrms, the dye thief Arjebee, the hedoness Mortilla, or robbers. The robbers can imply that a treasure, or even one of Horn’s Astra, can be found at the end of the rainbow. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## LEGEND 

_It is said that the nameless sky goddess is too proud to take any notice of the ground below. And yet she wants to see it beautiful to emphasize her own beauty. To put some color on the canvass of the world she created the rainbow Tincturus, whose colors are released with the rain to paint the flow­ ers, trees, and rocks in splendid hues. Tincturus thrives in the sky, so the sky goddess had to lure the great painter down by hiding treasures in the earth which the rainbow is searching for like a truffle pig. One who acts quickly might get there first to claim the treasure, but would be compet­ ing with drakewyrms and must beware of their ravenous greed._ 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## LOCATIONS 

Described below are the most important sites around Salterstay. 

## 1. THE CAPTURED RAINBOW 

_A large number of red elves are moving in the sparse woods where the rainbow touches the ground, and you see some of them riding on bloated eight­ legged beasts. Strangely enough, the rainbow splits into six distinct color bands nearest to the ground. Each luminous band seems to end in some kind of pulsing, blood­ red tree stump whose roots tether the color to the ground. Three captured elves from Ravenland are bound to a demonic tree by living vines. One of them notices you and shouts something inaudible. The red elves spot you as well and wave for you to scram to the other side of the stream. One of their archers puts an arrow to their bow._ 

The six demonic stumps form a hexagon and tether the six color bands to the ground. The colors into which the rainbow has been split are red, yellow, turquoise, green, blue, and violet. The tethers break if hit by a solid object of their respective complementary color (red–turquoise, blue–yellow, green–violet). 

The red elves can restore a severed band in one Quarter Day through uninterrupted rune singing. Severing all six of them frees the rainbow, which vanishes into the sky and slowly fades away. You may want to give the players a clue by having a salt- drugged red elf fire a red arrow at the turquoise band which then breaks. The other red elves yell at the archer and collectively start rune singing to reattach the band. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** At any given time there are fifty red elves and a few sarcoptes in the area.

## 2. TORGENWALD’S FARM 

_Several farms on the east side of the stream have been hastily abandoned with food still on the_ 

_tables and window shutters flapping in the wind. Some other farms are burned down and still smoldering. Only one seems to still be inhabited. A couple of dozen sheep are grazing around the farmhouses where a peas­ ant family is hurrying about their chores. Parked outside the house is a strange, extravagant carriage with a large pile of white bones between its shafts._ 

The farm is home to the sheep farmer Torgenwald, his wife Fnella, and their three children aged eight to twelve. They hardly dare to look at each other. The question is whom they fear the most: the red elves or the hedoness Mortilla who has made their cottage her bed chamber and now demands to be fed and pampered. The family would much rather be 

in Salterstay village with the others, but since Mortilla kindly but firmly has ordered them to stay, they dare not leave the farm. 

- ❖ **CREATURES** : The sheep farmers Torgenwald and Fnella and their children. 

## 3. THE ELDERHILL BURIAL SITE 

_A burial mound rises beside the southern road. At the top of the mound is a crumbling temple ruin and some standing stones, a few of which have fallen over. Their ancient inscriptions have faded_ 

_with age. Curiously, all the graves are open and seem to have been dug up from underneath._ 

The burial mound was used before the Demon Flood and was there when the current inhabitants of Salterstay village arrived only a few years ago. It is not their ancestors who are buried here, but old chieftains and warriors from the nearest horse clan. The villagers have avoided the mound ever since Mortilla showed up and started raising the dead to keep her company. Mortilla laughs and dances with the dead as if mingling with aristocracy. The undead clack their jaws or play their mummified tendons to produce some form of music. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** The hedoness  Mortilla and D6 undead, including Chieftain Haudaros. 

## THE HEDONESS MORTILLA 

The hedoness Mortilla claims to be a goddess of death who stumbled into Aslene during the Demon Flood three centuries ago. By her own account, she and her sisters were so efficient that they stripped an entire continent of life back in their home world and were banished for it by the gods who created it. 

Mortilla has accepted her fate and is quite happy in the Bloodmarch. The creator gods left her without the tools of her trade, but she had grown tired of all the killing anyway. Now she spends her time as a noble lady of indeterminate lineage whose greatest pleasure is to travel around and witness unusual and beautiful deaths. That is why she has come to Salterstay – to watch the rainbow die. 

177 

**salterstay** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

Mortilla looks like a woman in her forties with curly dark hair and exquisite but strangelooking clothes. She is usually in a cheerful mood and enjoys poetry, music, and good company, but above all she loves to watch creatures die in interesting ways. Mortilla travels in an elegant carriage drawn by two skeletal horses. When they are not pulling the carriage, the animals collapse into a heap of bones between the shafts. 

Mortilla is near immortal and made of a rubbery substance that is soft to the touch, but extremely durable, which causes swords to simply bounce off her. Magic does not take full effect on her either. She has no weapons, but is almost unimaginably strong. 

Mortilla wears a silver necklace with Lapis Lazuli, stones that can be used to dissolve the red color band of the rainbow, and a brooch with a violet amethyst that can dissolve the green color band. 

## HAUDAROS 

Haudaros is a long dead chieftain of the horse clan that controls the area. He was buried in the Elderhill along with twenty relatives and his closest warriors, but they were all raised from the dead by Mortilla to keep her company when she arrived. They are not pleased with this and would much rather rest in peace. Whenever Mortilla is occupied with other things, the undead are sitting lethargically on the burial mound or wandering aimlessly in search of longgone places and people. 

Haudaros was tall in life and wears the remnants of his chainmail and helmet, but his sword has rusted into the scabbard. He is gray, broken, and moldy, with a slightly skew back as a result of a collapse inside the burial mound. The chieftain speaks in a hoarse, drawling voice that often cracks, but only Mortilla or an Egressor can understand him. If Haudaros dies, his undead companions also collapse into piles of bones. 

## **MORTILLA** 

**STRENGTH 12, AGILITY 4, WITS 8, EMPATHY 5** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 1, Lore 5, Insight 3, Manipulation 3 

**TALENTS:** Death Magic 5 

**SOFT SUBSTANCE:** Due to her rubbery substance, arrows and POINTED weapons can never cause more than one point of damage with a single attack. 

**MAGIC RESILIENCE:** The Power Level of any spell cast at Mortilla is automatically decreased by D6 

**GEAR:** Opulent clothes, PRECIOUS find, silver necklace with Lapis Lazuli and brooch with violet amethyst. 

## **HAUDAROS** 

**STRENGTH 5, AGILITY 3, WITS 2** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 3, Marksmanship 2, Move 1, Scouting 3 

**ATTACKS:** Haudaros can fight with his sword in its scabbard, but such attacks only inflict half damage (blunt force), rounded up. 

**LINGERING CONSCIOUSNESS:** Haudaros can communicate with Egressors and Mortilla, but cannot be broken by damage to WITS . 

**BONY:** Arrows and POINTED weapons can never cause more than one point of damage with a single attack. 

**GEAR:** Rusty broadsword in a scabbard, broken chainmail, broken open helmet.

**chapter 13**
