## 4. THE BRIDGE OVER THE SEINDEL 

_A crude log bridge stretches across the gently flowing stream known as the Seindel, right by the collapsed span of an old stone bridge. A couple of red elves stand guard on the east side._ 

The red elves have driven everyone in Salterstay to the west side of the bridge to keep them away from the rainbow and told them to stay there without explaining why. However, they need the bridge intact in order to cross the stream when necessary. Since no one dares argue with them, there are only two guards on the bridge, but they have birch bark horns with which to sound the alarm. The stream flows into Lake Suna. 

## 5. SALTERSTAY VILLAGE 

_Beyond a simple log palisade is a village of about twenty houses on a hill. The humble but sturdy houses are made of fresh logs – clearly a settler community. Ruins of stone suggest that there was a village here long before the current one. The village is beautifully situated on the shore of a lake, where you can see jetties with small fishing boats and a larger barge._ 

managing the nets, tackles, and boats without venturing out on the water. The sheep farmers have driven their flocks into the village, whose streets are now slippery with sheep dung. During daytime they herd their animals in the meager, hilly pastures west of the village, careful not to stray too far from home. 

## VILLAGE HEADMAN TURENFORT 

The middle- aged Headman Turenfort is indecision personified; a large body around a tiny soul. He is a portly man, dressed in official garb which he has dyed red to please the red elves and a soiled Alderlander wig that he acquired from the merchant Kresos in exchange for a ridiculous amount of salt. He wears the wig backwards simply because he does not know any better. Turenfort has a habit of wringing his hands and rolling his bulging eyes whenever decisions need to be made, and does whatever he can to compromise and procrastinate. 

## **TURENFORT** 

**STRENGTH 3, AGILITY 4, WITS 3, EMPATHY 2 SKILLS:** Crafting 1, Manipulation 2 **GEAR:** Knife, leather pouch of salt 

The people of Salterstay are settlers from Harga in Ravenland. The village is made up of fifty adults with children and animals. They have recolonized the area which even before the Demon Flood was a salt mine and fishing village. Now they are distressed and frightened by the captured rainbow on the other side of the stream and the aggressive red elves who drove them from the east side. 

The village has no inn, but there is an assembly hall where miners sit around and get drunk on wine from the temple ridge. The fishermen spend their days down at the harbor, 

## MINING CHIEF TALULLIA 

Talullia is a tough mining chief in her thirties. She has a firm hand, but is always in a good mood – even when swinging her pickaxe or scolding some slacker or troublemaker. She usually wears her ash blonde hair down, but pulls it into a bun while underground. Her body is broad and muscular like that of an ox. Talullia’s greatest passion is gambling, and if anyone asks for a favor, she proposes that they settle the matter with a game of chance.

## **TALULLIA** 

## **STRENGTH 5, AGILITY 4, WITS 3, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 1, Crafting 3, Insight 1, Manipulation 2 

**TALENTS:** Builder 1, Sharp Tongue 1 

**GEAR:** Pickaxe and knife 

## DYE MERCHANT ARJEBEE 

Arjebee is an aesthete from Alderland who has come to collect the pure dyes of Aslene and bring them back to the weaving mills in his homeland. He is a handsome man in his forties with artfully styled hair and a well- trimmed beard. Arjebee stays clear of red elves, who view him as a henchman of the evil god Kolor. 

## **ARJEBEE** 

## **STRENGTH 4, AGILITY 3, WITS 3, EMPATHY 4** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 1, Crafting 1, Sleight of Hand 2, Insight 1, Manipulation 2 **TALENTS:** Sharp Tongue 1 

**GEAR:** A beautiful dagger, brushes, cosmetics 

reluctantly granted their request in exchange for one third of the profits, since the mine is situated in their territory. The horse folk themselves have no interest in mining and are obliged to avoid salt, which the Church of Have regards as an evil mineral from the moon. 

The priests were indeed opposed to the idea, but Headman Turenfort convinced them that the land of Have would be cleansed by removing the salt from the area. They explained that the salt was to be transported in sealed barrels to be sold in Ravenland and Alderland. The church finally agreed, on the condition that the villagers would help renovate the temple there so the priest could supervise the process and seal each barrel with the seal of Have so it cannot be sold in Aslene. 

The priestess Evianda has two barrels of blue tar at the temple, but no holy fire. Arrowheads dipped in blue tar can sever the yellow color band of the rainbow. The winemaker Karmenere uses yellow sulfur crystals to sulfurize his wine. These can be used to detach the blue color band. 

## 6. THE TEMPLE RIDGE 

_On the ridge along the east bank of the stream is a simple wooden temple built over ruins of white stone. The wood is blue, painted with the sacred blue tar of Have. There is also a vineyard with plantations on the southern and eastern slopes of the ridge. Small quantities of stone are quarried from a marble cliff that juts out from the sandy ridge. At night there is a fire burning._ 

## THE PRIESTESS EVIANDA 

The silver- haired Have priestess Evianda does her best to maintain civilization in this remote location. She is fastidious about her appearance and apparel, and strictly observes the formalities of social etiquette and religion. Her greatest joy is arranging suppers with wine from Karmenere’s vineyard and inviting guests of adequate sophistication. Underneath the strict facade is a good heart. 

The settlers contacted the nearest horse clan for permission to re- open the salt mine. The clan

## ZHOZHACH 

Zhozhach is a drakewyrm and glittercrawler – the drakewyrms’ designation for those favored by the rainbow god Tincturus. He arrived in Salterstay with eight of his kind, but two were killed when the group attacked the red elves guarding the rainbow, so a total of seven slightly scruffy- looking drakewyrms have now settled near the temple of Have. Zhozhach’s status is displayed by his metal collar which glitters in the six colors of the rainbow. The collar can serve as a clue about which colors complement each other. Zhozhach and his group have slithered their way to Salterstay after hearing about their god Tincturus being captured by red elves. They intend to free him, but do not know how to go about it now that their frontal assault has been repelled. 

## **ZHOZHACH** 

**STRENGTH 16, AGILITY 5, WITS 5, EMPATHY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Scouting 3, Move 2 

**MOVEMENT:** 2 

**ARMOR RATING:** 8 

**DRAWN TO GLITTER:** If the glittercrawler spots anyone wearing or holding anything that glitters (such as polished armor or a hand- held mirror), it will immediately attack that person. If this person leaves combat, the glittercrawler will pursue. 

## **MONSTER ATTACKS** 

- **D6 ATTACK** 

- 1 **BITE ATTACK!** The beast targets the adventurer with the highest STRENGTH and bites them with sharp teeth. Roll for the attack with nine Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (slash wound). 

- 2 **ICY ROAR!** The glittercrawler lets out a terrifying roar that affects all adventurers within NEAR range. Roll for a fear attack with five Base Dice. 

- 3 **CLAW ATTACK!** The beast swipes its claws at two adventurers within NEAR range. Roll for the attacks with eight Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (slash wound) each. 

- 4 **ACID ATTACK!** The glittercrawler spews a cloud of corrosive acid at all adventurers within NEAR range. Roll for the attack with six Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. Anyone hit by the attack suffers one additional point of damage per round for D3 rounds. 

- 5 **TAIL ATTACK!** The glittercrawler uses its long body to make a sweeping attack against all adventurers within NEAR range. Roll for the attacks with six Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). Anyone hit by the attack is also thrown to the ground. 

- 6 **TEARING ATTACK!** This weapon attack is performed by two glittercrawlers and consumes both of their turns in the round. They attack an adventurer and try to tear their victim apart. Roll for the attack with ten Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. An adventurer who is Broken by the attack dies immediately as their body is ripped in two.

## 7. THE SALT MINE 

_Three mine adits can be seen in the mountainside west of the valley. There is an open­ pit mine next to the adits, as well as a couple of sheds. In front of the mine are five carts on wooden rails that lead down to the harbor. There seems to be no work going on._ 

The mining operation has been suspended since the red elves captured the rainbow on the other side of the stream. The five carts are full of salt and covered with oil- soaked cloth. Tools and simple beds are available in the sheds. The adits are only twenty meters deep, and inside the mountain they grow into caves with ladders and landings. The walls sparkle with salt. 

## 8. THE NORTHERN ROAD 

_The northern route to Salterstay runs through a beautiful ravine between the mountain in the west and the temple ridge as it approaches the village. The road itself is very simple with two wheel ruts. The muddier sections have been reinforced with gravel or chipped bark._ 

## THE ELF IN THE MINE 

The Ravenlander elf Zender approaches the adventurers. He has been hiding in the salt mine ever since his three companions were captured by red elves. He asks them to help free his friends and can in return tell them about the red elves’ madness and about Vaerefor and Kasabatoda perverting everything they touch, which has caused concern among the elves of Ravenland. 

Zender knows that elves who pass through Kasabatoda turn into red elves, but not that the demon and his giant snail are on their way to Salterstay. Zender has a pouch with six rubies cut from the chests of red elves, which he intends to see restored in the Stillmist. In an emergency, he can reluctantly swap them for his freed companions. The rubies can be used to dissolve the turquoise color band of the rainbow. 

Zender also explains that his group has been shooting red elves with arrows dipped in salt, a substance that disorients them. Zender carries a green jade dagger, just because it is beautiful. Its green blade can be used to dissolve the violet color band of the rainbow. 

If Vaerefor van Reiben shows up, he will arrive this way on the giant snail Kasabatoda.

They can tell the adventurers more about the red elves and sarcoptes, and that Vaerefor is on his way with Kasabatoda to decolorize the rainbow,

## THE ROBBERS 

## **SALT AND RED ELVES** 

**==> picture [30 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

Common salt has a narcotic effect on red elves. They become intoxicated, start to hallucinate, and get addicted. A red elf hit by a salt- coated arrow suffers the effects of a hallucinogenic poison with Potency 6. Vaerefor and other red elf leaders are therefore wary of concentrated salt and throw it into the water to dissolve it.

but they do not know when he will arrive. They can laughingly describe how they accidentally broke the rainbow’s turquoise color band with a red arrow and got into trouble with their leader Varchenna. 

## THE DYE MERCHANT 

The dye merchant Arjebee approaches the adventurers with a proposition: he will pay them handsomely to sneak up to the rainbow and suck some pure rainbow light into his special leather bellows. Arjebee can answer any questions they might have about complementary colors and things of that nature. 

Nika and her fellow robbers have come for the treasure at the end of the rainbow, but realize that they are not strong enough to confront the red elves on their own. They would like the adventurers to join them, and preferably sacrifice themselves in battle against the red elves while Nika and her crew do the robbing. Nika’s eyes widen as she explains that the treasure is whatever one desires the most – that is how the rainbow works – and that this is a unique opportunity, since it is so rare for anyone to reach the end of the rainbow before it disappears. Nika has bought several color powders from Arjebee and plans to cause chaos by spreading them around the rainbow. Everyone knows that red elves cannot stand other colors than red. The hard part is getting close enough. Nika gladly recounts the legend of the sky goddess and the rainbow Tincturus. In reality, there is no treasure. 

## TO HAVE OR NOT TO HAVE 

The adventurers see a group of salt- drugged red elves (twice as many as the player characters) crossing the bridge and attacking the white temple. One of them is riding a sarcopte. If the temple is burned down or the priestess killed, sixty riders from the relevant horse clan will arrive in Salterstay a week later. They will question people about what happened and likely burn down the village as punishment for not coming to the temple’s aid. The clan riders might be willing to help the adventurers attack the red elves. Otherwise they are not very keen on fighting the elves and just perform a mock charge before riding off. 

183 

**salterstay** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## DEATH AT LAST 

The undead chieftain Haudaros contacts the adventurers, but it is hard to understand what he says or wants as the sounds he produces are scratchy and strange and his movements jerky. The Egressors Nuntoonia and Dyrengaard are in the village and can serve as interpreters. The hedoness Mortilla understands him as well, but cannot help them as she is the problem. 

After speaking to the chieftain, the Egressors explain that he resents the hedoness for raising him from the dead and that he wants to die once and for all. In return for the adventurers’ help, he will assist them with a specific task. Killing Haudaros is fairly easy, but keeping it secret from Mortilla is not – and if she finds out about it, she will try to stop them. Once it is done, she will be visibly displeased but resign herself to the passage of time. 

If the adventurers helped Haudaros die, twelve ghosts on horseback will emerge from the Elderhill and try to achieve whatever the adventurers asked of Haudaros. The adventurers cannot communicate with the ghosts other than through the Egressors. The ghosts will dissolve after completing their task. 

## KASABATODA ARRIVES 

The giant snail Kasabatoda will arrive in Salterstay a few days after the adventurers, along with Vaerefor, 40 red elves, and five sarcoptes. They follow the road from the north and pass between the mountain and the temple ridge, before crawling across the stream by the bridge and finally reaching the rainbow. The adventurers can slow the giant snail down by turning over the salt- filled carts outside the mine just as it passes by. Kasabatoda will then flee in panic into Lake Suna and remain in the water for several days to rehydrate. 

Upon reaching its destination, Kasabatoda will position itself to spray red paint at the rainbow through its rear end. The demonic red stumps will then creep further up the color bands, join together, and continue up the rainbow. Once the red color has devoured the entire rainbow it dissolves, and from that day forward every rainbow in the Bloodmarch will be all red. During this process Vaerefor will deliver rousing speeches to his followers, but also to the villagers to let them know that they are all welcome in his new red world. Vaerefor can be convinced to spare the rainbow in exchange for the Goblet Staff of Have.

## BACKGROUND 

The gigantic Dreamstress (page 96) came to the Bloodmarch during the Demon Flood three hundred years ago from her homeworld Eoni. She is therefore classified as a demon, but has always seemed like a peaceful creature. She is also regarded as a prophet of the goddess Oneira. 

According to the Dreamstress, Oneira is the sleeping goddess of existence and the world is her dream in which we all live. Through oneiromancy her followers can participate in the goddess’ visions to understand and influence the world. The Dreamstress acts as a spiritual mentor to those wishing to learn oneiromancy, the art of observing, understanding, and influencing existence through disciplined dreaming (page 34).

The effect increases with the number of people who simultaneously share the same dream, so the Dreamstress accepts anyone as her apprentice to gather as large a following as possible. Together they will dream up a perfect world. 

The Dreamstress helps visitors change things they do not like. For example, she might restore a lost hand, resurrect the dead, and uncover secrets in her dreams. What she cannot see are things created by other divinities, such as Horn’s Astra. Her secret plan is to get as many people as possible to simultaneously dream of her homeworld Eoni so that she can go back there and never return. 

Oxengelder was once a Vasnian place of sacrifice named after the white bulls whose blood were ritually spilt in the limestone basins to please the allmother Volitia. The place was deserted when the Dreamstress settled there, 

as the horse clans had purged it of Vasnians. The Dreamstress’ peace reigns in Oxengelder – people of all kin come here to trade and learn what is going on in the world. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## GETTING HERE 

The adventurers can be drawn to Oxengelder in various ways: 

- ✥ They hear rumors, perhaps from a wandering oneiromancer, of a master teaching dream magic – a magic discipline they have never come across before. 

- ✥ They are told that the Dreamstress can alter reality – restoring lost limbs, raising the dead, resurrecting the dead, finding lost things or people, etcetera. 

186 **chapter 14** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

- ✥ They encounter a key player who is their way to seek the Dreamstress’ counsel. 

- ✥ If the adventurers have already teamed up with any of the key players, they could be sent as legates to Oxengelder to negotiate with representatives of another key player. 

- ✥ They are stalked by a mara (see page 194 or the _Book of Beasts_ ) and have trouble getting rid of it. 

## LEGEND 

_Have you heard of Oxengelder – the paradise of dreams? It’s said that all the country’s peace and beauty took refuge in Oxengelder as the Demon Flood raged into Aslene. There the gentle giantess welcomes anyone of pure heart and open mind to study oneiromancy, the art of dreaming up a won­ derful life! Would you rather dance than fight? Rather love than hate? Would you give life a second chance? Then let the dream come true! If you go to Oxengelder, be sure to wear flowers in your hair, for it pleases the Dreamstress._

**THE DREAMSTRESS** 

Lots of people ask for the Dreamstress’ help, and so can the adventurers. The consultation usually involves the Dreamstress and the required number of oneiromancers sitting down and dreaming in the Sky Tower for a reasonable price. The Dreamstress can among other things restore a severed limb or bring a dead person back to life – but only does the latter in exchange for an Astra. The Dreamstress can also locate any place, item, or person, except for Horn’s Astra (although she says that she can). She can also advise people on how to advance their plans.

## **ASPIRING DREAMERS** 

**==> picture [29 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

Interested adventurers can apply to study Oneiromancy at Oxengelder. The usual requirements for learning skills and spells apply. The school does not accept anyone with Wits higher than 3 or Empathy lower than 3, but criteria are kept secret and applicants are only told that they have failed the entrance test and been deemed unsuitable to study oneiromancy. However, adventurers with access to one of Horn’s Astra are automatically accepted.

_A wide stairway leads up to the entrance through an ornate pavilion. Below the stairs is a strange, music­ playing fountain. There is light radiating from the watery cascades in varying colors and intensity. The water from the fountain flows in a small stream down the slope. The area is sur­ rounded by a three­ meter­ high limestone wall with the severed heads of animals displayed at its top._ 

## 2. THE PILLARED HALL 

_Within the walls, the ornamented stairway con­ tinues up to a vast pillared hall with lots of people moving about. The marble pillars are six meters high and tilted in different directions. Small water­ falls are flowing from the ceiling. Behind the outer_ 

At night the building’s gates are closed to keep wild animals away, but only guarded by two pikemen. The guards live in the pavilion, which also houses the school’s laundry and storage room. 

**THE REVERIE:** Not far from the fountain is a simple inn called the Reverie. It consists of a single space with a dining room at one end and a dormitory at the other. Most of the guests are poor people who have come to seek the Dreamstress’ counsel.

## 3. THE GARDEN OF NOVICES 

_A lush parkland with two pavilions. Youngsters are dancing, playing, smoking, and laughing. Older individuals are sitting on the grass or at tables, watching the youths with patience or disapproval. A wagon is pulled through the paths of the park by a white ox._ 

The Garden of Novices is occupied by newcomers and guests. The latter are expected to make a small donation in exchange for food and accommodation. The pavilions are open- plan with dormitories. There is little to no privacy at the school, except in the pavilions in the Garden of Masters. 

## ✥ **CREATURES:** About twenty novices. 

The Horn of Abundance is constantly filled up with delicacies that are both tasty and nutritious and make up a large part of the school’s finer cuisine. The horn itself is not magical and the delicacies are derived from dreams. 

## ✥ **CREATURES:** Headmistress Pirimena, Coloratura Dreamer Ketre Meskalma 

## PIRIMENA 

The school’s greatest oneiromancer and headmistress is Pirimena, a half- elf from Ravenland who has quickly risen in status. Pirimena takes the utmost care to perform her duties to perfection and has no other interests than oneiromancy and running the school. She is unswervingly loyal to the Dreamstress and would come up with excuses for anything bad the giantess might do. 

## 4. THE GARDEN OF MASTERS 

_En park med promenadstigar där ett fåtal personer rör sig genom grönskan som är mycket underlig med vandrande träd, buskar med speglar som blad och bär i form av små hästar. Ett par kvinnor knyter drömfångare vid ett bord. Invid trapporna upp mot Pelarskolan står ett stort bronshorn monterat med öppningen nedåt. Frukter har fallit ur hornet och en pöl av honung runnit fram. Korgar med godsa­ ker står i närheten._ 

This park is for senior oneiromancers and more distinguished visitors. The strange plants are created from dreams as master tests or simply for the fun of it. Only the best creations are kept and often replaced as new ones are constantly being added. They dissolve into air if taken outside the school grounds. 

## **PIRIMENA**

## KETRE MESKALMA 

Ketre Meskalma is the school’s foremost coloratura dreamer, an oneiromancer specialized in the minutiae of dreams – the details that make dream creations believable and unique and therefore powerful. She spends most of her time sleeping, sitting under a lily maple from where she visits other people’s dreams to correct and embellish them. This makes her an important teacher at the school.

## 5. THE MASTERS’ PAVILION 

_Two marble pavilions with closed doors at the far end of the park._ 

The pavilion closest to the Pillar School is reserved for senior oneiromancers, while distinguished guests are housed in the other one which has three separate suites and a meeting hall. Each room in the guests’ pavilion has a dreamcatcher which records everything that is said and seen so the Dreamstress and her closest disciples will immediately find out about it. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Poansa Moulter’s doppelganger (page 78). Poansa appears to spend most of her time in the pavilion, but usually it is in fact her double. 

## 6. THE COUNCIL CIRCLE 

_The far end of the Garden of Masters holds a cir­ cular lawn with a paved center. On the far side of the circle is a horseshoe­ shaped roof over a number of seats._ 

Daytime: _A huge, heavily jeweled creature is sit­ ting with her eyes closed in the middle of the circle, surrounded by supplicants who listen devotedly to every word that rumbles from the creature’s lips._ 

Nighttime: _A huge creature is lying curled up like a small hill in the middle of the circle. Her abun­ dant jewelry rustles softly with each deep breath._ 

The Council Circle is where the Dreamstress receive guests and clients, seated in the paved center of the circle. Ordinary people come here every morning to ask questions and seek advice, while more distinguished guests are welcome to attend council meetings in the afternoons. During council sessions, all unauthorized people are expected to stay away from the Garden of Masters and keep out of earshot. At night the Dreamstress often sleeps in the circle. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** The Dreamstress (page 96). 

## 7. THE MARBLE PLAZA 

_Wide stairways on both sides lead up to a large marble roof where oneiromancers are sitting deep in meditation while youngsters play some form of ball game with a living ball that screams and laughs as they kick it around. Trees and shrubs have been planted in the semicircular wall facing the Sky Tower. Water from the limestone basins flows in shallow channels across the marble plaza and plunges in beautiful cascades over the edge into the streams of the parks below._ 

The roof of the Pillar School serves as a marble plaza that often bustles with life. On sunny days the Dreamstress herself sometimes sits here, smiling as she watches her adepts reverently bringing out mouthwatering delights and dreamed- up creations. The meditating individuals are often nudged by their more energetic peers, but are not angered by this, as ignoring disturbances is part of their training. Various exercises in sleep gymnastics are underway – for example, two teachers are quickly throwing a ball back and forth to train their apprentices’ rapid eye movements.

_Rising above the Marble Plaza is a 30­meter­ high brick tower with a spiral ramp and land­ ings. There are people walking up the ramp or sleeping on the landings._ 

The Sky Tower is built to focus the efforts of the most skilled oneiromancers. The Dreamstress herself sometimes takes her place on the highest landing at the top of the tower, which she reaches by using the coils of the spiral ramp as a ladder.

## 9. THE LIMESTONE BASINS 

_From the top of the mountain slopes behind the dream school, clear water flows through a system of limestone basins whose shimmering blue light give the entire mountainside a dreamlike quality._ 

The water continues down to the parks through the channels of the marble plaza and into the gurgling streams near the pavilions.

## THE IMPOSTOR 

A junior oneiromancer named Maroosa approaches the adventurers. She dares not talk to anyone from Oxengelder, but whispers that Poansa Moulter (location #5) is not who she says she is – that a duplicate is impersonating her. What if no one at the school is real and all existence is a cruel fiction? Maroosa is right, since Poansa really is a doppelganger. Pirimena and the Dreamstress are already aware of this and have no problem with it. 

## KALEMES 

Kalemes of the Caberian horse clan is a penniless novice tasked with cleaning the limestone basins. He and his friend Trommel have both cut ties with their clan and made significant progress as oneiromancers. But they are childish and unruly and have taken to defiling the Horn of Abundance. 

## THE CONTRACT 

The key player Grudenstaal (page 79) is here as the Dreamstress’ guest and seeks out the adventurers to request that they murder the Sabirian Chieftain Mommodar and the Caberian Chieftain Merdekai, since they in turn are planning to murder Selligar Horne, chieftain of the Houns, who is needed to unite the horse 

192 

**chapter 14** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

clans. As payment the adventurers can ask for just about anything in his power to give. They will also have all the help they need, but discretion is of the utmost importance. Grudenstaal would likely be killed if suspicion fell on him. The chieftains might also attack the school if the plan is exposed. 

## FILTH 

All of Oxengelder is outraged that the Horn of Abundance by the stairs of the Marble Plaza in recent days has been soiled with blood and other unclean substances, which meant that lots of fruits and other delicious treats had to be thrown away. The Dreamstress thinks that the soiling was caused by unsound dreams and urges everyone to be vigilant of their sleeping visions. 

A few days later Trommel of Kesla, a junior dreamer, is found dead in one of the limestone basins (location #9). His neck has been broken with such force that the head was almost torn off and the vertebrae ruptured his skin. 

Pirimena wants to hush up the whole affair and asks the adventurers to discreetly investigate what happened. They might notice that a junior oneiromancer and basin cleaner named Kalemes is very upset and packing up to leave. He could later be found dead in the same way or approach the adventurers before he leaves to explain how he and his friend Trommel dreamed filth into the Horn of Abundance, but only as a joke. 

It turns out that the Dreamstress herself killed Trommel, and possibly Kalemes as well, by snapping their necks. The demon simply deemed them useless, a stain that had to be wiped out. To her it is no different than pulling 

weeds from a garden. She might even brutally murder Kalemes in front of witnesses and seem almost surprised if it upsets anyone. 

## LOST IN SLEEP 

Pirimena seeks out the adventurers. Ketre Meskalma has disappeared into her dreams and seems unable to find her way out. The headmistress needs someone to bring her back. Unfortunately that person cannot be an oneiromancer, as Ketre would assume them to be co- dreamers which would only disorient her further. 

If the adventurers accept the mission, Pirimena asks them to sit down by Ketre’s side. She hands each of them a goblet of drugs that will help them stabilize the dreaming state. She then explains that the dream will show them a mirror which can make the dreaming Ketre remember reality if she looks into it. Part of Pirimena’s consciousness will go with them in the form of a dream hamster, but it must not be too conscious, as it might disrupt the mission. 

Once asleep, the adventurers find themselves in Ketre’s dream in a random part of an adventure site they have previously visited. But the place and its colors are inverted – a mirror image of reality. Ketre’s dreaming self can be found where the adventure site’s strongest enemy was located and looks just like them, but has Ketre’s mind and is not hostile. 

The dreaming adventurers have their usual gear, a handheld mirror, and a hamster, but the latter seems unable to do anything but squeak and point. Along the way they are attacked three times by a mara. Any adventurer who is killed or Broken immediately wakes up in the pavilion at 

193 

**oxengelder** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

Oxengelder, broken with Wits 0. It is impossible to head back into the dream. If all adventurers are neutralized, the mission has failed and Ketre is lost forever. 

When Ketre is found, she does not want to look in the mirror and splits into three versions. Each duplicate runs off in a separate direction. The adventurers must find and show the mirror to all three versions, after which the mission is completed and everyone wakes up. The adventurers cannot wake up by themselves before the mission is completed or all of them have been neutralized. Pirimena can wake them, but chooses not to, knowing that Ketre’s life depends on their success. 

## **MARA** 

## **STRENGTH 12, AGILITY 3, WITS 2** 

**MOVEMENT:** 1 

**ARMOR RATING:** None. Maras are immaterial beings and can only be hurt by magic and magic weapons. Takes half damage from fire. 

**DREAM ATTACK:** The mara attacks by manifesting itself in a nightmare and straddling the sleeper’s chest. It rides, scratches, screams, and performs other types of monster attacks (see below). The mara only attacks one person at a time, but switches target if forced to or if the victim is Broken. An adventurer attacked by a mara cannot be woken or wake up on their own. However, other adventurers can attack the mara through its shadow in the physical world: a terrifying silhouette of its demonic dream form, frenetically assaulting the sleeping victim. Adventurers in the same room as the victim can make a WITS roll once per round. On success they wake up and realize that something is attacking their friend. 

## **MONSTER ATTACKS** 

- **D6 ATTACK** 

- 1 **RIDE OF THE MARA!** The mara straddles its victim’s chest and starts riding them with violent movements that cause the person’s chest to compress. Roll for the attack with eight Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). If the attack inflicts damage, the sleeper may make a Wits roll with a +1 bonus for each round they have been assaulted by the mara. On success the victim wakes up feeling an immense weight pressing down on their chest, and the mara’s attack ceases – until the next time the adventurer falls asleep. 

- 2 **SCREAM OF THE MARA!** The deformed face becomes twisted with rage and the mara lets out a hateful scream that pierces deep into the soul of the sleeping adventurer, who suffers a fear attack with eight Base Dice. A successful attack leaves the victim COLD . 

- 3 **KISS OF THE MARA!** The mara leans over the victim’s face and presses her shriveled lips against the sleeper’s mouth. The adventurer is filled with swirling dream dust from the deepest, darkest maelstrom of nightmares. The victim must succeed with a WITS roll or suffer the effects of a lethal poison with Potency 7. 

- 4 **BITE OF THE MARA!** The mara opens her drooling jaws and bares her razor- sharp fangs, aiming a bite at the sleeper’s neck. Roll for the attack with nine Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (slash wound). The wounds are physical and can also be seen in the waking world. The victim automatically wakes up if the attack succeeds.

- **D6 ATTACK** 

- 5 **DREAM CLAWS!** The malevolent creature slams her long, twisted arms against the sides of the sleeper’s head. A wave of terror and flashing pain washes over the victim as the mara’s dream claws tear deep into their consciousness. The victim suffers a fear attack with ten Base Dice. If the attack succeeds, the victim automatically wakes up unable to do anything but scream hysterically for D10 rounds. 

- 6 **CLAWS OF THE MARA!** The mara sinks her black claws into the sleeper’s chest. The pain is excruciating as the evil creature starts digging its way towards the victim’s heart. Roll for the attack with ten Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (stab wound). The wound is physical and no armor will protect against the attack. If the victim is Broken, the heart is torn out of their body. The wound can only be healed with HEALING HANDS and other forms of healing magic.

The copies have the same stats and gear as the originals, but dissolve into thin air once any of their attributes reaches 0. About half of all NPCs at the school are killed, except the Dreamstress and the senior oneiromancers.

## THE DREAMSTRESS’ JOURNEY 

The Dreamstress’ goal is to dream herself back to her homeworld Eoni. To achieve this, she must perform a travel ritual in a single dream session that requires a large number of oneiromancers and preferably a couple of Horn’s Astra. 

The Dreamstress will try to get hold of the required Astra by asking others to look for them and convincing the owners to lend her the artifacts “just for the ritual.” But she will not take them by force. The Dreamstress will claim to need these Astra to fulfill the owners’ wishes during a ritual, for example to locate additional Astra or whatever else their goal may be. Adventurers can participate in the travel ritual if they have learned that magic at the school. 

The ritual will succeed unless the adventurers disrupt it. First the used Astra dissolve, then the Dreamstress herself fades away from the world. The participating oneiromancers will slowly wake up and wonder what happened. The Dreamstress has abandoned them, never to return. The used Astra are consumed and gone. 

Note that the travel ritual can destroy one or several Astra forever and thus have a major impact on the campaign. The consequences may include: 

- ✥ If Vaerefor was present during the ritual, he bursts out laughing, mocks the participants, and explains what the Dreamstress has done before he too fades away and returns to his crimson forest. 

- ✥ The previous owners of the Astra are furious and take it out on the oneiromancers, demanding compensation or instituting a massacre. 

- ✥ Pirimena stubbornly believes in the Dreamstress’ return and continues to run the school as an academy, now with the goal of dreaming the Dreamstress back to the world. 

- ✥ The Vasnians take the Dreamstress’ disappearance as a sign that the goddess Volitia has banished the blasphemer and that they must take back their holy sites. After a week they attack Oxengelder in great numbers. 

To stop the ritual, the adventurers must disturb many of the dreamers or steal the Astra from the Dreamstress’ hands. If the Dreamstress thereby fails to dream herself back to Eoni, she will leave Oxengelder and join Vaerefor to dominate Aslene together. If that happens the Dreamstress no longer smiles, nor helps anyone else.

## GU 

**TYPE OF ADVENTURE SITE:** Settlement 

_You approach a settlement by a strange freestanding rock. The mountain seems to consist of two entirely separate mountains that link together in the middle of the plain, locked in a constant struggle. Judging by the garbage heaps, the overgrazed paddocks, and the wooden shacks, the area has long been home to horse folk. This is unusual as Aslene riders tend to be more nomadic. There seems to be little going on in the village, but a couple of people are having an argument. As soon as they see you, however, they stop talking and go back to work as if nothing had happened. One of them angrily starts picking the hooves of their horse while the other weaves a wicker basket._ 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## BACKGROUND 

Bann Guelder is a traditional Aslene burial mound inhabited by Galdanes or Houns (choose which one it is depending on where you place the adventure site). The rock formation itself is spectacular – red sandstone with patches of darker volcanic rock, like a forest of standing stones or the hackles of a wolf. 

Most horse folk are constantly moving through their clan’s territory, but at Bann Guelder a family group called the Sons of Nesos with roughly a hundred members has been encamped for almost two years. Others see this as both incomprehensible and weak. The Elderguard with its graying riders are supposed to be the only permanent inhabitants. 

197 

**bann guelder** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The mound is named after the edible guelder rose growing in the area. Its white flowers and red berries light up the Lushday and Fallturn months. 

The great secret of Bann Guelder is that the inhabitants a few years ago discovered an immense gold deposit inside the burial mound as new passageways were being dug. The mound holds tons of pure metallic gold, and its people have since been gripped by discord and greed. 

Some want to leave the gold in the ground for their ancestors to guard; others want to mine it and use it to increase the power of their clan. Still others believe that having more possessions than can be loaded onto a horse has a corrupting effect on people. 

The issue is by no means settled when the adventurers arrive at Bann Guelder, where the atmosphere is tense and forced as everyone does their best to appear normal and keep the secret from strangers. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

left hand has been cut off. The stump is dressed, but has not yet healed and is still bleeding. The man has also had his tongue cut out. On his body the adventurers find a piece of unwrought gold equivalent to five gold coins. The Elder chopped off his hand and cut out his tongue for stealing a gold nugget from the burial mound. After a few days he managed to steal a horse and escape, but this was as far as he got. 

- ✥ The adventurers encounter the merchant Kresos and his two bodyguards. Kresos is happy to do business, but anyone interested in his wares will find that they are sold at five times the normal price. He refuses to haggle and would rather save his goods for Bann Guelder, which is where he is headed. There people are not afraid to pay up – and in pure gold, no less!

The only thing keeping the birds away is the man’s horse, which grazes by his side. He dies shortly after being found and just has time to mumble unintelligibly and point in the direction of Bann Guelder. Adventurers who examine the body will find that his 

## LEGEND 

_It’s shameful how the Sons of Nesos have softened and settled down like common peasants around the ancestral tombs in Bann Guelder. Their buried forefathers must be writhing with shame at their delicate offspring. And Nesos himself has returned from the grave, they say, probably to drive the young folk from the village and into battle as befits the sons of Horn. They’ve grown fat, red­ buttocked from a day’s ride but with plenty of coin, wherever those came from – not from honest plunder, that’s for sure. My brother­ in­ law sold ‘em a couple of hogs and was paid in pure gold, would you believe it?_

These two tall and hot- tempered clan warriors are twin sisters. They know about the gold deposit and are trying to convince the Sons of Nesos to take it and use it to strengthen the clan in these troubled times. They have called their cousin Verdegas to Bann Guelder for support.

## KRESOS 

Kresos is a Quard merchant who travels around with his wagon and recently has made good business at Bann Guelder. He has figured out that the Sons of Nesos are hiding a great treasure in the tombs of the mound, but assumes that it consists of gold coins, jewelry or hammered gold nuggets. He has therefore assembled a band of thugs who are planning a raid on the place. See the event “Brushfire”. 

The adventurers can buy things from Kresos, but the prices are five times higher than normal and not open to discussion. He only sells goods that are otherwise unavailable at Bann Guelder. Kresos has two bodyguards.

## PRINCE MERDENVIL 

Prince Merdenvil is a highly educated and charming dwarf prince from Firestead. He has gotten wind of the gold in Bann Guelder and wants to bring it home to Firestead – something that would raise his status enough to claim lordship over the region. Merdenvil poses as a botanist in order to move freely and look for the gold. He claims to look forward to ”botanizing” the area around Bann Guelder to catalog the local species of guelder rose. Merdenvil has no intention of stealing the treasure, but mainly wants to see the gold and appraise its value before making an offer for it. 

Merdenvil lives in a dwarf- made carriage pulled by a large troll set up with parasols for the sun. The carriage is guarded by six heavily

## 2. THE HALLS OF THE ELDERMEN 

_Closest to the mound are some log houses whose facades display lots of carvings, trophies, and col­ ored metal plates clinking in the wind. Some older_ 

The old men of the Elderguard occupy the decorated houses whose interiors are painted with glorious scenes from their clan and family history. The volcano Horn and its five sons in a circle are included in the motifs. One of the houses serves as a meeting hall as well as a school where the men tell children what they need to know about life. The other house is where the Elderguard eat and sleep. 

The smithy focuses on weapons and horse equipment, but also produces everyday items like simple locks and fittings, as well as horseshoes. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** About ten older Aslene warriors – some in good health, others on the verge of death. Ten children in one of the houses. 

## ELDERMAN KORLO 

Korlo is the commander of the Elderguard, a scarred and weathered fighter with skin like tanned leather. Despite his age, Korlo is a tall and imposing man who is not easily ignored. He is often chewing on betel roots which grow on the mountain. He is serious and dutiful and has piercing, scrutinizing eyes.

## 3. THE DEMONIC PRISONER

You see what looks like a human body, flayed but still alive, shack­ led to a wooden rack in a cross­ like position. There is a fire burning under the creature and some chil­ dren are laughing, throwing stones, and poking it with sticks._ 

## LEGION THE DEMON 

Legion looks like a flayed, constantly bleeding human strung up in shackles over a fire behind the Elders’ houses. The creature actually consists of several demons who could not care less about the torture, but are there to study humankind on behalf of their kind who are planning to infiltrate the country eventually. Legion often says strange things to provoke the humans and takes note of their response. 

## **LEGION** 

## **STRENGTH 12, AGILITY 3, WITS 5, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Move 3, Scouting 2, Lore 1, Insight 2, Manipulation 5 

**ARMOR RATING:** 2 (demon skin) 

The captured and tortured creature is an immortal demon named Legion. The Sons of Nesos have kept him prisoner for generations, torturing him as revenge for the Demon Flood that devastated the Bloodmarch three centuries ago. Children and mean people in general go there to give him a whack when they have nothing better to do, while the evening torture is more ritualistic with people gathered 

**SEVERAL IN ONE:** Legion consists of multiple demons and can split into four if need be. In combat each demon draws an initiative card and acts independently of the others. Legion’s Strength is freely distributed between the demons.

## 4. ENTRANCE TO THE BURIAL CAVES 

_There is a path leading up to a natural cave in the mountain. Two older men are guarding the entrance._ 

The guards will not let anyone through except members of the Elderguard and people attending funerals. Inside is a branched cave several hundred meters long. The alcoves and side rooms are littered with dried up corpses, and the smell is less than pleasant. In a cave chamber lives a blood mage named Betsom. 

At the end of the caves is the gold deposit in the form of a solid, gleaming slab of gold that continues down into the mountain. There are axes and pickaxes nearby which have been used to break off pieces of it, but progress is slow – enough for D6 gold coins can be extracted per turn with a successful MIGHT roll. The extent of the deposit cannot be determined, but there must be tons of pure gold. 

## BETSOM 

Betsom is a seventy- year- old Aslene blood mage. He haunts the mountain at night to keep nosy people away. All the villagers know he is not dead, but are forbidden to talk about it. Betsom is tired of the loneliness of the mountain, although as a blood mage he has no problem being around the dead. Korlo usually visits him every day to cheer him up, as the two of them are old friends. 

## **BETSOM** 

**STRENGTH 2, AGILITY 3, WITS 4, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Stealth 2, Insight 1, Manipulation 3 **TALENTS:** Blood Magic 3 

**GEAR:** Ragged clothes

## EVENTS 

Here are a number of events that might occur in Bann Guelder. 

## THE WHEELBARROW 

A woman walks off with a wheelbarrow full of turnips from the garden and returns with it empty. The adventurers find the turnips in a ditch where the woman has dumped them. No one wants to eat turnips anymore – they are only grown for show. 

around to torture him. He has simply completed his studies. Everyone within NEAR range is hit by slime and becomes possessed unless they make a successful Empathy roll. Roll on the table on the next page. 

Those who have shown him sympathy or kindness are unaffected. The only way of freeing the possessed is to have a blood mage cast BIND DEMON , which cures them all. A blood mage with access to blue tar gets +1 on their roll, and +2 if the blue tar burns with holy fire. Possessed individuals suffer from a random effect as set out in the following table. 

## EXPENSIVE HABITS 

The adventurers notice the villagers eating unusually good food that is not available in the region and drinking wines from Alderland and Ravenland. A few expensive items can be seen, but the owners quickly hide them if they attract attention. 

## THE MASTER OF DEATH 

Wailing and hooting can be heard from the mound at night. Luminous figures appear and disappear at the top of the mountain. Elder Korlo and his men tell bloodcurdling stories about curious people who went up there and were found the next day with their hearts torn out and unspeakable terror on their faces. Some say that the blood mage Betsom has returned as a master of death. He died last year and still consumes raw meat left for him in the burial cave. 

## I AM LEGION 

The captive demon suddenly explodes in red slime when a group of villagers have gathered 

## BRUSHFIRE 

One night the alarm is sounded and people start yelling fire. When they wake up, the adventurers see that the plain is on fire a kilometer or so away. The wind is blowing in the direction of the village. The Aslenes grab their so- called beavertails of horse leather and run off to put out the flames. The adventurers are clearly expected to help avert this calamity and the villagers will not take kindly to those who refuse. The firefighting lasts one Quarter Day. 

Once most people have left the village to put out the fire, eight riders of the Quard clan led by the merchant Kresos (page 199) attack the burial mound from the opposite direction. They kill two of the elderly guards and enter the tombs in search of the golden treasure, which Kresos believes to be hidden in the catacombs. 

The raiders have horses and Kresos’ wagon to transport the gold. They intend to strike swiftly, take what they can, and hurry off to get a head start. After finding and marveling at the treasure they manage to hew negligible

The raiders are mad at Kresos for putting them in danger without reward, but also stunned by the enormous treasure inside the mound. Now the secret of the golden treasure is out, which might trigger additional events (see below). 

## THE SABIRIANS ARRIVE 

A band of Sabirians show up to “help” their weaker neighbors guard the gold – for reasonable 

compensation, of course. Their real reason for being here is to make sure that no one else gets their hands on the treasure. 

## THE CABERIANS ARRIVE 

A troop of Caberians arrive for the same reason. Colonel Grudenstaal (page 79) wishes to form an alliance with those involved. He can get them capable troops from Alderland in exchange for some of the gold, so that their alliance can seize power from Selligar Horne. 

205 

**bann guelder** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## THE PRINCE’S PROPOSITION 

Prince Merdenvil reveals his true identity and offers to have his dwarves take the gold away to Firestead before it becomes the downfall of the Sons of Nesos. In return he can put a host of dwarven warriors at the clan’s disposal for one year and help the clan achieve whatever goals they may have. 

He does not specify exactly what kind of downfall he predicts, but explains with a laugh that the Sons of Nesos can always wait and see for themselves. The dwarves do not intend to ever spend the gold, but only use it to decorate their underground halls. Merdenvil himself wants to be remembered as the prince who inherited a city of stone, but left behind a city of gold. He has a caravan of sturdy wagons waiting only a day’s march away. 

## THE VASNIANS RETURN 

A band of Vasnians and armored orcs arrive, led by Konana (page 91). The Vasnians believe that the gold belongs to them, since the land was stolen from their ancestors and Bann Guelder was a sacred place for their people. The Vasnians realize that they are in no position to negotiate, but Grudenstaal or Poansa might include them in an alliance. 

## **GOLD INFLATION** 

**==> picture [29 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [24 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

If the gold is mined and put into circulation, the gold market will quickly become saturated and inflation will skyrocket. A gold coin is soon worth no more than a copper coin in the hex on the map where the gold spreads and a silver coin in adjacent hexes. Vast fortunes plummet in value, people stop farming their land and running their workshops since they “can just buy the food instead of working.” Goods become scarce and prices rise. Soon lots of people will starve and the ruined rich will furiously look for the culprits who “ _removed the damn gold from the tomb._ ” If the dwarves of Firestead take the gold away, no such inflation occurs.

## BACKGROUND 

The Watch of the Sisters is the name of a very old temple in the Bloodmarch, dedicated to the divine sisters Wail and Flow – the respective goddesses of winds and flowing freshwater. 

The forgotten story of the temple is that horse folk from the Drawn Lands in the west once rode into Aslene on a mission from the sister 

gods to seek a union with the local population – the Vasnians – so that their peoples could trade with one another. Instead, the riders started a war of expulsion and extermination because their proud chieftain Sella felt slighted at the meeting. 

Wail was so enraged by Sella’s arrogance that she whipped up hurricanes on the plains between the two countries so that no one has since been able to return to the Drawn Lands from Aslene. The proud Sella responded by renouncing his old goddesses and, encouraged by the Aslene dwarves, urging his brethren to worship Horn, the largest volcano in the land. 

Sella believed that Horn granted his riders victory over the Vasnians through the Astra which the dwarves had given him as gifts from the god. He divided his people into five clans, 

207 

**watch of the sisters** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

named after the five volcanoes of Aslene, and gave each of them a part of the land to farm as their own. Many battles were fought in the years that followed, between the clans as well as with Vasnians and Alderlanders from the neighboring country in the southeast, which quickly became an archenemy. 

The Watch of the Sisters was built on the Guardian Mountain as a religious outpost in Aslene back when the two countries were to be bound together in friendship. The temple still stands, but is now used to keep watch on the curse put on the horse clans by the goddesses. During the first century after the religious break, horned dwarves were hired to build a monumental clock in the shape of a bird, called the Metrochrone, which measures the time until the curse is lifted. 

When the Watch of the Sisters was built, the moon elves, who fled the worm people on Anoma (page 57), had already split their kin into halflings and goblins and built their temple Agnostica. After a moon elf trueseer dreamed that the worm people would soon find their hideout, they wanted to remove Agnostica from the world to conceal it further. It could be done, but only temporarily. As measurers of time, the moon elves decided to use the Metrochrone at the Watch of the Sisters, since the clock was already in place. For more on Agnostica, see page 232. 

When the player characters find the Watch of the Sisters, the Metrochrone has stood still for a few years and the temple is deserted. A dwarven engineer known as the Kogler, who had been in charge of maintaining the mechanism, is said to have poisoned the temple’s last three priestesses, stolen part of the Metrochrone, and disappeared. The Kogler really did steal a part of the clock, but did not murder the priestesses. In any event, the 

moon elves’ temple Agnostica cannot return to the world until the Metrochrone has been reactivated. 

The Watch of the Sisters is completely embedded in very dense crimson forest, a slithering jungle that emerged after the Demon Flood. When the eccentric prince and composer Cavaldo da Putenze arrived at the Watch of the Sisters a couple of decades ago, the holy women took him in since the winds had blown him there from their native Drawn Lands. Since then the demon forest has continued to grow and the priestesses have passed away. What no one knows is that Cavaldo’s music and passions are attracting the demonic vegetation and making it thrive. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## GETTING HERE 

There are several ways for the adventurers to find the Watch of the Sisters, by chance or otherwise. 

- ✥ The adventurers are contacted by Konana (page 91) after she finds out that the Book of Winds, an old document kept at the temple, proves that Aslene actually belongs to the Vasnians. The book is so precious to them that Konana in exchange can offer the adventurers the Goblet Staff of Have, which she keeps hidden, or something else of great value. 

- ✥ The adventurers encounter Vrendenholm, an Alderlander scholar who says he is interested in the early history of Aslene and wants to hire them as an escort to the temple. He too seeks the Book of Winds. The scholar is part of Poansa’s expedition, but acts on his own initiative 

208 **chapter 16** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

   - since none of the Alderlander agents are interested in history. 

- ✥ The adventurers encounter a bard name Vheilor and his protégé D’Irae a few hexes from the temple. Vheilor claims to have heard rumors of Cavaldo’s musical genius and seems obsessed with performing with him. Vheilor really is a bard, but is secretly also a necromancer of the Order of Egression (page 149). 

## THE SCHOLAR VRENDENHOLM 

Vrendenholm is who he claims to be – an Alderlander historian trying to learn more about the early history of the Bloodmarch. The aim is to help the Alderlanders understand and take over the country. He also seeks allies for his leader Poansa (page 76). 

## VHEILOR THE BARD 

Vheilor really is a bard, but also a sorcerer of the Order of Egression. Through D’Irae he has had vague visions of, and been spoken to, by the unquiet spirits of the murdered Donna Mardiena and Carafo. Vheilor has made it his sacred duty to restore Cavaldo’s memory so that he realizes and admits that he murdered his family. Only then will the dead find peace. 

Vheilor may then choose to kill Cavaldo himself and make him his undead servant, or leave the prince to be consumed by the shame of his crimes. Vheilor realizes that the death of the forest spirit Margarita is a step towards bringing peace to the murdered, but the Egressor has no quarrel with the forest and believes that the blame for its actions lies solely with Cavaldo.

## LEGEND 

_It is said that Aslene was once a barren desert, until the sister goddesses Wail and Flow sent their servant Have to make the land lush and green through the goddesses’ warm winds and flowing freshwater._ 

_The magma god Horn stayed below ground at the time, but as roots started to pierce down into his under­ ground domain, he grew curious and burst through the surface as a volcano. Horn was amazed at how the Bloodmarch had flourished. He delighted in the bird­ song and the dance of the butterflies, and was struck with love for Have. But when he learned that the Vas­ nians had usurped the land without appreciating the_ 

_goddesses’ creations, Horn was furious and brought forth the horse clans from his fire to banish the ingrates. He took Have as his wife and gave her fire to rule as her wedding gift. Soon their five volcanic sons sprung up from the ground, and Horn gave each of them a horse clan as their personal guard._ 

_But the goddesses Wail and Flow felt that Horn had seduced their servant and taken both her and the land out of their hands. In anger they raised an impenetrable forest around their temple. From then on, they wanted nothing to do with Aslene anymore._ 

(Note that the legend of the Watch of the Sisters is told from the horse clans’ perspective.) 

## **THE FOREST SPIRIT MARGARITA** 

**==> picture [29 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [23 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

When Margarita begins to materialize, which could occur as soon as music starts playing, the nearby jungle takes the form of living creatures and motifs from the music. The temperature rises as well. Shapes resembling Cavaldo’s family might appear, not knowing where they are, but begging to be freed from their life of captivity. 

The forest spirit Margarita is the collective subconscious of the local crimson forest. She was born out of Cavaldo’s music, lives off it, and inspires him as his most devoted listener. Only to the tunes of heartfelt music does her consciousness focus into intelligence and manifest itself in the form of a talking fruit that can communicate telepathically. 

**==> picture [29 x 211] intentionally omitted <==**

**==> picture [23 x 211] intentionally omitted <==**

If Margarita is killed, the jungle will turn into ordinary crimson forest, grow sparser, and fade away in some places. 

In this concentrated form she can be killed with any weapon if blue tar (page 38) is first thrown at her, which prevents her consciousness from once again dispersing. Normally Margarita exists throughout the jungle like a demanding, capricious, pleasure- seeking force that communicates emotionally with Cavaldo as well as with other musicians in the forest who catch her attention. 

Margarita has the stats of a ghost (see the _Gamemaster’s Guide_ , page 94). Each unit of blue tar thrown at her binds Margarita to her concentrated form for one round, after which she once again disperses her consciousness throughout the jungle. 

**==> picture [376 x 27] intentionally omitted <==**

## LOCATIONS 

The Watch of the Sisters is nestled in the embrace of a lonely mountain, on a slope surrounded on three sides by high cliffs which form a wide ravine. It has in recent years been filled with dense crimson forest. The forest has a primitive consciousness who calls itself Margarita – “the Pearl.” Prince Cavaldo has settled in the Watch of the Hermit, a smaller temple belonging to the Watch of the Sisters. 

## 1. THE STONE SISTERS 

_Two corroded stone women twice the height of a human are standing with their backs to the dense crimson forest behind them. A few vines are twined around their legs. One of the statues is holding an urn and the other a fan._ 

precisely by the dwarves long ago. But red vines and hanging branches are imposing themselves from the sides so that the adventurers are sometimes forced to crawl or push their way forward. After only twenty meters they can no longer see the sky and must press forward as if through the bowels of a huge, living body. Anyone with an animal must succeed with an ANIMAL HAN DLING roll for the animal not to panic, run off into the vegetation, and be lost forever. 

After a few hundred meters the vegetation suddenly closes behind and in front of the adventurers. Their presence has triggered peristaltic movements in the forest which cause the passage to contract and expand in waves rolling into the forest. The adventurers can move with the waves or wait and advance between them. In the latter case they are gently squeezed through the walls. It is unpleasant and scary, but does not cause any damage. 

On closer examination, the adventurers notice tears constantly and inexplicably streaming down the cheeks of one of the statues – the one of Flow holding an urn. Faint breathing is heard from the other one, which depicts Wail.

Some of them have fallen over and remnants of a rusty iron chain runs between the bases. The passage looks narrow, but not so much that you cannot get through._ 

The crimson forest has not taken hold in the gaps between the paving stones which were laid very 

## 3. THE WATCH OF THE HERMIT 

_After a kilometer or two of uphill walking, you reach a clearing by a lake. The red vines are kept up by an ancient dead tree leaning over an ornate little step pyramid with a house at the top. Exquisite music is heard from the house. The vegetation around it seems to take almost humanoid form. Some human­ like creatures are moving around the house._ 

The creatures by the house are red elves led by the elven healer Protelas. The red elves regard Cavaldo as a holy man, whose music causes Reiben – the red elves’ name for the crimson forest – to flourish. They provide him with supplies and will defend him and themselves against attack. 

The house itself is in great disarray, full of acquired or home- made musical instruments

**watch of the sisters**

and pieces of parchments with red scribbles. There are also strange pointed objects, seashells, pieces of wood, metal objects, and other things over which Cavaldo has pulled yarn back and forth in different colors and patterns. This is Cavaldo’s sheet music. 

The red elves live in huts around the clearing, like yellow- red abscesses that seems to have grown from the vegetation itself. Protelas lives in a tree cave in the large, hollow, dead tree.

## PRINCE CAVALDO OF PUTENZE 

Cavaldo is Duke of Putenze in the Drawn Lands west of the Bloodmarch, and a gifted composer to boot. When Cavaldo was told that true musicians must endure hardships to reach the height of their art, he headed out in an airship with his valet Carafo, his wife Donna Mardiena, and their three children. Unfortunately they flew too close to the windswept, forbidden plain towards Aslene and were sucked into the hurricanes. 

just started to take hold, and soon he was composing frantically, inspired by the tribulations of exile. Donna Mardiena was less enthusiastic as she missed life at the palace back home. 

Soon the crimson forest flourished around the Watch of the Hermit, but Cavaldo refused to see how his music nourished and attracted the demonic vegetation, even after it started speaking to him and twisting his mind. In a fit of madness he murdered both Carafo and Donna Mardiena. The prince grew increasingly melancholic and his music increasingly emotional, which caused the forest to grow even further. One of the children went missing after being punished and when another one vanished as well, a dismayed guest left with the youngest girl, Dhiria. 

Eight years later she has returned incognito as D’Irae, along with the necromancer Vheilor, to find or avenge her family. Cavaldo mourns his family profoundly, and has been driven deeper and deeper into madness by the jungle’s consciousness Margarita. He does not realize that it was he who killed his loved ones, but thinks it was the deceitful Carafo’s doing.

## PROTELAS 

Protelas is no red elf, but an ordinary elf from Ravenland, and yet he commands the red elves at the Watch of the Hermit. He claims that he is still not worthy of becoming one with the forest and that he wants to understand it better before taking that step. That way he has avoided assimilation. Because he is a skilled healer and an eloquent speaker, the red elves have accepted this postponement. But now there are rumors that Vaerefor himself is on his way to the Watch of the Sisters with his giant snail Kasabatoda, and the red elves are talking about Protelas finally becoming one of them. 

Time is running out for Protelas, who is actually an agent sent by the elves of Ravenland to identify the crimson forest’s weaknesses. He has noted Cavaldo’s relationship with Margarita and suspects that the spirit might be a key to the entire forest. Protelas keeps a unit of blue tar hidden in his Aesculapian Staff – a healing staff with a carved snake coiled around it – and thinks that the tar can kill the forest spirit, but lacks the musical ability to lure her out or communicate with her. Protelas can confide in the adventurers if he has reason to trust them, and together devise a plan to kill the forest. 

## **PROTELAS** 

**STRENGTH 4, AGILITY 4, WITS 3, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Move 2, Lore 1, Manipulation 2, Healing 3

## 4. THE TEMPLE GROUNDS 

_Beyond the area surrounding the Watch of the Hermit, the crimson forest is denser than it was on the way there. Through the mist on the other_ 

_side of the lake, you see a high mountain with a temple near the top. You cannot find any natural paths through the forest, but notice a narrow gap between the vegetation and the wall which leads to the temple._ 

One way of reaching the temple on the Guardian Mountain is through the temple grounds. 

**THE WALKWAY:** Beyond the stairs is a paved walkway between two walls, but it is completely clogged with crimson forest. During the day the adventurers can push through between the wall and the vegetation, but after just ten meters it becomes impossible. At night the forest contracts, allowing the player characters to crawl 50 meters to a ledge by a red pond. However, a sarcopte (page 116) stalks the passageway at night. 

**THE POND:** The fountain has overflowed and covered the round park in red water all the way to the stairs. The adventurers must swim roughly 30 meters underwater, but the water is crisscrossed with slimy strings and membranes which sometimes have to be cut down. Performing a maneuver underwater, such as swimming, requires a successful MOVE roll. Each round the adventurer must also succeed with an ENDURANCE roll not to drown. On failure the adventurer starts drowning and suffers 1 point of damage to Strength per round. Beyond the pond is the bottom of a stairway. 

**THE SLIMY STAIRWAY:** The top end of the stairway is blocked by jungle, but one can get past the vegetation by climbing a dead tree. However, there is thick slime oozing down the tree trunk, which makes it difficult for the adventurers to climb and gives them –2 on all MOVE rolls. Once at the top they find twenty or so white balls, half a meter in diameter, embedded in slime. These 

214 

**chapter 16** 

are slime snail (page 121) eggs laid by a relative of Kasabatoda. If the adventurers take an egg with them, it will hatch a week later. The snail can be tamed as a mount and reaches the size of an elephant after one year. 

**THE TEMPLE PORTAL:** At the top of the slimy stairway is a smaller overgrown passageway that leads to the temple portal, which has been partially knocked over. Halfway through the passageway, the adventurers suddenly feel a growing chill coming from the left as they are attacked by a number of Fraeges (page 106). They are as many as the adventurers. 

## 5. THE RIVER 

_A river flows into the lake by the Watch of the Hermit. The river seems to be coming from the waterfall high up on the Guardian Mountain. The crimson forest forms a tunnel across the river where it runs closest to the lake._ 

Another way to get to the temple on the Guardian Mountain is to follow the sacred Abzellan river, in which the demonic vegetation cannot grow. There is a small rowboat on the lake by the Watch of the Hermit, but it is full of water and must be repaired ( CRAFTING roll) since no one has used it for years. 

**ABZELLAN RIVER:** The vegetation is hanging down towards the river, but there is enough space to pole or row the boat across. Hiding among the plants above are D6 sporewalkers (page 123) who drop bundles of spores on the adventurers. 

**UNDERWATER TUNNEL:** There is a thirtymeter- long section where the river runs underground. The water is free from vegetation and the adventurers can swim with a successful roll 

for MOVE and ENDURANCE as per above. The swimmer resurfaces in the lake below the temple. A high waterfall crashes down into the far end of the lake. 

**THE BOATHOUSE STAIRS:** There is a collapsed boathouse by the lake. From there a narrow set of stairs leads up to the temple through crimson forest. Anyone who goes ashore is immediately attacked by a slithernet (page 122). Halfway up the stairs, the chill of fraeges can be felt in the vegetation to the right. 

## **THROUGH THE AIR**

## 6. THE TEMPLE ON THE GUARDIAN MOUNTAIN 

_Up on the mountain above the crimson forest you reach an ancient, almost completely dilapidated temple of stone. It seems to have stood on some kind of stone slab which has cracked and left half the temple leaning down into a brick pond. The build­ ings that once stood there have completely collapsed, and a massive eight­ meter­ tall statue has fallen into the pond. In front of the remaining pavilion stands a similar statue of a woman with a bird perched on her shoulder. The place seems abandoned. Doors have fallen off their hinges, buckets and other debris litter the courtyard, and crops have decayed._ 

215 

**watch of the sisters** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

The Watch of the Sisters is a thousand year old temple dedicated to the goddesses Wail and Flow. There dwell the last three of its priestesses – Hina, Costolena, and Kremiled – unaware that they are ghosts. Their skeletons rest in their respective bed chambers. Next to Costolena’s remains is a music box that still works (see the text box). Otherwise, the rooms are empty. 

A group of Vasnians has come to the temple in search of the Book of Winds, thinking it will prove that the horse clans have no claim to the land. The key player Konana could be among them. The group climbed down to the temple from higher up the mountain, and if the adventurers befriend them, the Vasnians can help them leave that way to avoid the crimson forest. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** As many Vasnians as there are player characters, and possibly the key player Konana (page 91). 

existence and relive the exact same day as the one before. If destroyed, even by someone casting PURGE UNDEAD , they still return the next day as if nothing happened. This is because they are no ordinary ghosts, but sustained by the grace of Wail and Flow because their watch has not yet ended. 

The priestesses are kind to guests, but dismissive of Aslene horse warriors. They can change their daily routine if there are visitors, but do not remember any of it the next day. 

If the Book of Winds is taken from its crypt, the ghosts of Hina, Costolena, and Kremiled will regain their memories and realize that they are dead. See the text box on page 219. Only when the Metrochrone is reactivated and the curse of the horse clan is lifted do the ghosts disappear forever. 

The priestesses are ghosts as described on page 94 in the _Gamemaster’s Guide_ . 

HINA, COSTOLENA, AND KREMILED 

The last priestesses at the Watch of the Sisters, Hina, Costolena, and Kremiled, are ghosts but do not know it. Once the Book of Winds has been retrieved, they regain their memories and think they were poisoned several years ago by the Kogler. Vengeance must be taken! 

What really happened was that Hina accidentally made a poisonous soup from mushrooms she had picked in the forest, killing her sisters and herself. The forest spirit Margarita knows this and is proud of having contributed to their deaths, since the priestesses criticized one of Cavaldo’s musical pieces. 

The priestesses’ ghosts are dismayed that the Metrochrone has stopped and want the Kogler to repair it. Early each morning they fade into 

## 7. THE HOUSE OF WINDS 

_On a rock by a precipice near the temple is a single quadrangular pavilion of stone that is open in all directions. A ten­ meter­ long ramp extends from each opening, and at the end of each ramp sits a stone animal on its hind legs with its front legs raised – a bear to the north, a frog to the east, a dragon to the south, and a bull to the west._ 

A player character who succeeds with a LORE roll knows that the animals represent the four winds in Wail’s mythology. 

The pavilion’s interior is square. On the floor is a locked metal hatch that cannot be opened by force. Inside each of the four openings is a stone relief of a tree, so that the entrance 

216 **chapter 16** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

goes through the trunk. Each tree has four stone slabs shaped like leaves set into the wall, decorated with four different symbols: a drop, an oak leaf, a fly, and a flame. The following verse is inscribed upon the pavilion’s ceiling: 

_Let the winds feed their children Let the winds fill their begging hands Let the blood of the living sate his paws May the burned bark of trees sate her claws Let the oak leaves sate his hooves_ 

_Let the flies sate her feet So we can read the writing of the wind And let time have its course_ 

**SOLUTION TO THE CIPHER:** To open the hatch in the floor, the correct stone slab in the tree in each direction must be pressed at the same time, which requires four people. The correct interpretation of the verse reveals which slab should be pressed in the respective direction 

– that is, the opposite direction to the statue outside. The correct combination is: 

✥ South – drop 

✥ West – fly 

✥ North – flame 

✥ East – oak leaf

The book is only one half of “the Book of Winds and Water.” It is also indestructible.

_tome. Along the walls lie six mummified corpses of past priestesses in their alcoves. Animals have fed on the bodies and died from the embalming fluid, so there are carcasses of rats, raccoons, and snakes as well._
