## TUPILAQ 

Tupilaqs are merged from parts of corpses and carcasses that have been risen from the dead. 

## **TUPILAQ** 

**ATTRIBUTES: STRENGTH 24, AGILITY 2, WITS 1 MOVEMENT:** 2 

**ARMOR RATING:** None, but takes half damage from non- magic weapons. 

## **MONSTER ATTACKS** 

- **D6 ATTACK** 

- 1 **NECROTIC BITE!** The abomination opens its decaying jaws, baring numerous half- rotten tooth stumps, and bites one of the adventurers. Roll for the attack with five Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (slash wound). If the attack inflicts damage, the adventurer is also affected by a paralyzing poison with Potency 6. 

- 2 **SCRATCH ATTACK!** The tupilaq extends its claws and springs furiously at an adventurer. Roll for the attack with six Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (slash wound). 

- 3 **DARKNESS ATTACK!** The abomination produces a strange hum and lets out an oily, magical darkness that settles over the adventurers. All adventurers within NEAR range must make an INSIGHT roll (not an action). On failure they are blinded by the magical darkness and have to fight with a –2 modification according to the rules in the _Player’s Handbook_ (page 112). The effect lasts until the same point in the next round’s initiative order. 

## **D6 ATTACK** 

- 4 **ROAR OF ANGUISH!** The grotesque creature contracts its body as if about to pounce and lets out a cacophonous roar of anguish from its human and animal throats. Every orifice in the revolting mass contributes to this symphony of horror, and all adventurers within NEAR range suffer a fear attack with seven Base Dice. 

- 5 **TACKLE ATTACK!** The raging beast lunges with full force at an adventurer within NEAR range. The repulsive mass of cadavers feels as heavy as lead, and the adventurer suffers an attack with eight Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). If hit, the victim is knocked down. 

- 6 **DISSOLVING ATTACK!** The tupilaq wraps one of the adventurers in an almost loving embrace and spews out a stinking, milky-white poison that soaks the victim from top to bottom. Roll for the attack with seven Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). On success the adventurer is GRAPPLED (see the rules in the _Player’s Handbook_ .) Every round they remain in the creature’s grip, the adventurer is attacked with a lethal poison with Potency 6 (9 against a designated target).

The Kogler should be somewhere in Firestead – see the adventure site Tribolia of the Kogler. But as mentioned previously, the Kogler did in fact not kill the priestesses. They just think he did.

## 9. THE METROCHRONE 

_Beyond the temple is a huge mechanical clock mounted on the rock face, vaguely shaped like a stylized eagle. Water from one of the waterfalls flows between its legs, where a waterwheel of the overshot type clearly used to be. The wheel is miss­ ing, as is its axis._ 

The Metrochrone was built long ago by horned dwarves and most recently managed by the dwarf mechanic known as the Kogler. When the 

temple’s last three priestesses met their unfortunate demise a few years ago, the dwarf took the clock’s waterwheel to his smithy, Tribolia – see the adventure site Tribolia of the Kogler. 

The temple Agnostica’s absence from the world is linked to the activity of the Metrochrone, and the temple cannot return until the clock has been reactivated. The adventurers can restart the Metrochrone by retrieving the missing part from the Tribolia of the Kogler and installing it. 

## 10. CAVALDO’S AIRSHIP 

_On the mountain slope beyond the waterfall with the giant clock lies the wreckage of a strange, open ship. Its rigging seems to have been made of thick nets, now rotten. There is a large propeller in the stern, connected to an axis which runs along the entire hull. Around the ship grow what look like large sponges. Sometimes pieces of them fall off, before rising into the sky and drifting away like gray clouds._ 

The wreck of Cavaldo’s airship is found on the mountain slope beyond the Metrochrone’s waterfall. Around it a couple of flowering bloodbeeches (page 103) grow. 

The ship can be repaired with a successful CRAFTING roll and a Quarter Day’s work, including the crank with the propeller by which the ship is driven forward and steered. The gas- producing fungi that lifted the ship have taken root on the slopes and can be collected. The net that kept them in place has rotted away, but a new one can be made from the slithernets (page 122) growing by the boathouse below the temple. 

The ship can only be maneuvered in light or no wind, but can drift in stronger winds. The 

219 

**watch of the sisters** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

fungi that lift the ship off the ground are growing and constantly producing a gas lighter than air. For the aircraft to descend, the adventurers must climb the net and release the fungi, which then fly off in the wind (and are later replaced by new ones). The ship’s anchors were lost back in the Drawn Lands.

## EVENTS 

Here are some events that might occur at the Watch of the Sisters. 

## THE FURIOUS GIRL 

One of the adventurers notices D’Irae speaking to Vheilor with restrained rage, even though she is supposed to be mute. “If you kill him, it all ends here! I’m not sticking around for that,” responds Vheilor firmly, causing the girl to hit herself in the face and run off in tears. Vheilor looks around for witnesses and can now be pressured into revealing his true reason for coming here. 

## THE RED ORCHESTRA 

Cavaldo has set up a large instrument in the crimson forest – a table equipped with ringing 

metal rods, whistles, strings, and bells. Branches, tentacles, and half- formed, human- like outgrowths will soon start playing the instrument in a growing and never- ending cacophony. Soon the adventurers notice the forest growing and closing in around the clearing that surrounds the Watch of the Hermit. The music intensifies and heat starts radiating from both the instrument and the vegetation, which makes it hard to get close. 

Unless the adventurers intervene, someone else will soon express concern about Margarita’s tunes and argue that the music might escalate and spread crimson forest throughout the country. The whole thing must be stopped. On the other hand, the red elves are delighted that things are finally happening. They will stop anyone trying to interfere. Unless something is done, all hexes adjacent to the Watch of the Sisters will turn into crimson forest, after which the instrument will explode. 

## THE REDRUNNERS 

A squad of elves from Ravenland, twice as many as the adventurers, arrive at the site. They have come to fetch the healer Protelas, but also want to wipe out all red elves in the area and bring their rubies back to Ravenland to be restored. The squad may include an old acquaintance from _Raven’s Purge_ , such as Ulmaya or Alsurso.

**TYPE OF ADVENTURE SITE:** Settlement 

_A curtain of water falls outside the mouth of the cave. Inside is a sulfur­ smelling chamber lit by a river of lava flowing down from the volcanic crater on the other side of the cave. The floor is littered with debris. Three huge spheres of stone, seemingly floating in mid­ air, are rotated against each other by gears driven by a paddle wheel in the lava. What looks like a mechanical horse is standing nearby next to a crashed airship. The cave’s ceiling seems to be made of shimmering metal above the spheres. To the left, where the floor rises towards the cave wall, human­ like creatures are locked in combat. To the right, across the lava river, is a large closed gate._

## BACKGROUND 

In ancient times, Tribolia was one of the horned dwarves’ largest smithies, but they were forced to abandon it when it was invaded by demons shortly after the Demon Flood almost 300 years ago. The place has been taboo ever since. 

The dwarf known as the Kogler has alone defied the great council’s prohibition and returned to figure out how the smithy’s machines work so that he can restart them. “The Kogler” is not a name, but a professional title. The smithy was named after the three floating stone spheres rotating with gears inside the cave. 

The Kogler has taken out some of the smithy’s demons by using blue tar and reactivating a group of mechas – mechanical, mindless, dwarf- like creatures that were constructed 

221 

**tribolia of the kogler** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

in the heyday of the smithy and did much of the work. Other than that, he spends most of his time mapping the cave’s mechanical details. 

The Kogler traveled through the great gates of Tribolia in his airship. Unfortunately this triggered an old security mechanism that brought down an iron- bound stone wall in front of the gate. The dwarven guards damaged the airship’s envelope so that the light metal seeped out and floated to the ceiling. 

The Kogler can still come and go through the same entrance as the adventurers, but he is wanted in Firestead and therefore as suspicious as he is discreet. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

_dwarves have appointed a guardian of the smithy – the so­ called Kogler. The current Kogler was a bit odd even before he was appointed, but the title really went to his head. He became obsessed with “claiming his domains” and sailed his airship into the smithy against the council’s orders. The gate slammed shut behind him and would not budge. Since no one wanted to come for him, the Kogler had to stay in his smithy and has probably died by now._

## LOCATIONS 

Described below are the most important places in Tribolia of the Kogler. 

## GETTING HERE 

Horned dwarves in the area know that a stream on the mountainside above the now closed gate of Tribolia used to flow into the smithy, but no one has tried to get in because of the prohibition. The adventurers can find the stream after a bit of searching, follow the water, and find the stairs leading down into the mist below the waterfall at location #1 on the map of Tribolia. 

## 1. THE WATERFALL 

_The stream gushes out of the rock into the lava river below, where it immediately vaporizes. You see the remains of wooden chutes on the mountain, which were probably used to redirect the water._ 

The rock reaches down to the valley floor and the trail continues across a couple of ravines via bridges of stone.

Now Tribolia is said to be abandoned and tainted by demons, so no dwarf with any sense will go there. Since the clan wars, the_

A couple of arched stone bridges lead across the lava flows.

## 3. THE FLOOR OF THE SMITHY 

_You cross the cave floor through a landscape of waste heaps, stacks of rotten timber, hard coal and ash, cracked steamers of patinated copper, flywheels, trip hammers, treadwheels, wooden chutes, thick chains, carts, twisted metal beams, and more. Everything is decrepit with age, but not untidy. The place seems carefully arranged with wide streets between heaps and machines._ 

Some of the coals can still be lit, but the cave is already warm from the lava. 

abandoned. The horse was meant to be a gift to the horse clans to celebrate the friendship between them and the horned dwarves of Firestead, but Mekefalos could never be delivered. The Kogler has installed the flywheel from the Metrochrone at the Watch of the Sisters (page 219) inside the horse’s body. That is the part which must be returned to the Metrochrone in order to activate it. The Kogler has got the horse running again, only to discover that it is stuck in hardened lava and would have to be pulled free with an amount of force he cannot muster. 

## MEKEFALOS 

## 4. THE SMELTING CHANNELS 

_Three wide channels of stone and metal lead away from the lava river where it calms down below the falls from the crater. The channels are equipped with floodgates and lined with tracks, with shovels and a couple of minecarts on both sides. There are heavy metal chains lying along the sides of the channels._ 

The floodgates can be raised with two treadwheel cranes, but the cordage is rotten and breaks if anyone tries to use them. If the cordage is replaced, the cranes can be operated by two people walking inside each wheel, provided that some solidified lava is first cleared from the floodgates. If the floodgates are raised, the channel is filled with hot lava. 

## 5. THE HORSE MEKEFALOS 

_Next to the lava river, in front of the mechanism that drives the three spheres, stands a huge mechan­ ical horse. Someone has recently been working here, judging by the fresh metal shavings, tools, and rolls of rope under the horse. There is a ladder on the inside of the horse’s front leg._ 

The horse called Mekefalos, “the Machine Head,” was one of the last constructions to be finished before the smithy was invaded and 

A giant mechanical horse. A ladder on the inside of one of its front legs leads to a space inside the horse’s chest. From there it is possible to climb back through the torso between various levers and pistons all the way to the flywheel. One can also climb forward to the horse’s head where the machine is controlled through sturdy reins and more levers and dials. The flywheel driving the horse is stolen from the Metrochrone at the Watch of the Sisters (page 219). Understanding how to operate the horse requires a successful CRAFTING or LORE roll. 

## **MEKEFALOS** 

## **STRENGTH 32** 

**ARMOR RATING:** 7 (steel) 

**MECHANICAL:** Operating Mekefalos requires a successful CRAFTING or LORE roll each round. This counts as a slow action for the driver. Only one adventurer can make the roll, but others can help. If it succeeds, the horse can _either_ move one zone _or_ perform an attack with 15 Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (blunt force) against a target within NEAR range. Whenever Mekefalos takes damage (one point or more), there is a risk that the horse will stop working. Roll D6 – if the result is 1, the horse must be repaired with a successful CRAFTING roll.

## **FREEING MEKEFALOS** 

**==> picture [30 x 43] intentionally omitted <==**

The only way to free the mechanical horse is to use Jormensul, the axle driving the three stone spheres (#8). This requires a thick chain, which can luckily be found along the nearby smelting channels. The chain must then be attached to the horse. There are plenty of strong iron beams lying around, and one of them can be passed through the horse’s jaw and a link at one end of the chain. The other end can easily be hooked to one of Jormensul’s pegs, in which case the chain will be wound around the axle and stretched tight.

Mekefalos can break the gate blocking the smithy’s main entrance by kicking with its hind legs. The horse can also step over the lava river.

## 6. THE BLOOD SWAMP 

_At the very back of the cave, where the floor rises, is a swamp seemingly bubbling with blood. Rivulets of water trickle down the rock face into the swamp. There appears to be a constant struggle between thin, crimson creatures emerging from the swamp and dwarves fully clad in metal and armed with hand drills. Some vegetation can be seen around the swamp and its damp surroundings – fungi, lichen, moss, demonic vines, shrubs, and the odd flower or two._ 

The swamp is alive and all that remains of the smithy’s demonic elements. Sporewalkers are constantly arising spontaneously from the goo, before climbing up and trying to carry the spores away to be spread throughout the cave. The Kogler’s mechas are fighting them by drilling into the creatures’ center and thereby killing them. The fighting takes place in near silence and without emotion. 

✥ **CREATURES:** D6 sporewalkers (page 123), D6 mechas (page 112). 

## 7. THE GREAT GATE 

_A wide, paved pathway leads up to a massive stone gate that blocks the smithy’s main entrance._ 

The stone gate slammed shut when the Kogler passed through it in his airship. Unfortunately it got stuck and has therefore not been used for a long time. The adventurers can still get out through the waterfall where they entered, but the only way to leave with Mekefalos, the airship, and the Metrochrone components is through the great gate. Opening the gate requires a kick from a freed Mekefalos (page 223) or help from the dwelvers in Blowdwell (#14). 

## 8. JORMENSUL – THE WORLD AXLE 

_A huge paddle wheel in the lava river causes a thick iron axle to turn. The screw at the end of the axle drives the cogs on the lowest sphere, which in turn drives the other two. Strewn around the wheel are some metal sheets and other scrap._ 

The dwarves called the axle Jormensul. Next to the wheel is a simple shed with various shoes and gloves on wooden shelves, including thermal insulated shoes with thick bast soles and iron shoes that can be used to walk on the spheres. There are also thermal insulated gloves and gloves with long metal rods ending in hooks. 

**CLIMBING JORMENSUL:** The adventurers can reach the spheres by climbing Jormensul. They must balance on the spokes of the paddle wheel ( MOVE roll where failure means falling) to get to the axle, but better wear bast shoes as the spokes are very hot (otherwise they suffer one point of damage to Strength). The drive shaft itself has protrusions and handholds which they can then climb (make a MOVE roll with +2). After that they must walk or crawl up the thread of the screw until they get close to the Grotewola sphere and can catch one of its loops using the gloves with the hooks as they rotate past, so they can get up on the sphere itself (another MOVE roll). 

## 9. THE GROTEWOLA SPHERE 

_Above the drive shaft spins the lowest and smallest of the stone spheres, seemingly floating in mid­ air, circling a horizontal axis. The equator consists of thick cogs that drive the sphere above. There are metal loops near the cogs._ 

226 

**chapter 17** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

Grotewola has a core of solar tin, a metal that becomes as light as air when heated. The cogged belt contains iron which pulls Grotewola towards the Freiwola sphere’s magnetic cogs and keeps it in place. An adventure with iron shoes can stand and walk on the magnetic surface of the sphere without falling off. Next to the cogged belt are metal loops which the hook gloves can be attached to. Without any of these aids, it takes a successful MOVE roll with –2 to climb the sphere. 

## 10. THE FREIWOLA SPHERE 

_The middle sphere, which also seems to float in mid­ air, is bigger than the one below and has two cogged belts – one offset against the bottom pole where it meshes with the sphere below, and one along the equator which meshes with the largest sphere. This sphere rotates with the same speed as the bottom one, but around a more vertical axis. It shimmers in beautiful metallic blue and consists of a smoother material than the others._ 

As with Grotewola, the metal shoes stick to the sphere’s surface. It too has a core of solar tin which is heated by the friction and makes the sphere almost weightless. Both of Freiwola’s cogged belts are magnetic, holding it in place against the other spheres’ iron belts. The beautiful blue surface is thin and purely cosmetic. 

## 11. THE WELDEWOLA SPHERE 

_The largest sphere is kept up by a thick metal axle running between the floor and the ceiling. It spins more slowly than the other two as its cogged belt has more cogs. Near the axle, at the top of the sphere, there are door and window openings from which lamplight spills out. Smoke rises from a chimney._ 

The entire upper part of Weldewola consists of a circular room that serves as the Kogler’s workshop and is full of junk. This is where the adventurers find the Kogler, who is surly and rude and resents their intrusion. However, he is not prone to violence and tries to talk and negotiate his way out of trouble, well aware of what his expertise is worth. If attacked, he defends himself with the help of two mechas he keeps as assistants at the smithy. The Kogler knows almost everything about Tribolia and will gladly show off his knowledge out of vanity. He has not figured out how to free Mekefalos. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Two mechas and the Kogler. 

## THE KOGLER OF TRIBOLIA 

Kogler is not a name, but the title of a dwarven master smith. The Kogler of Tribolia is a grouchy, elderly dwarf mechanic and inventor, who illegally trespassed into the forbidden dwarven smithy of Tribolia to devote himself to his craft. Like most Aslene dwarves, the Kogler is horned, but his left one is prosthetic due to an accident. The left part of his face along with the horn is made of brass, its eye a lens that sees in the dark, while doubling as telescope and loupe. His left arm is mechanical, which is not immediately discernible since it is covered by a sleeve and glove. The master smith always dresses and sleeps in dirty and stinking working clothes. 

The Kogler is unarmed, but in a pinch he will defend himself using his tools and his very powerful mechanical arm. As a person, the Kogler is headstrong, easily irritated, suspicious and contemptuous of the slow-witted, which to 

227 

**tribolia of the kogler** 

## **ATTACKS WITH THE MECHANICAL ARM** 

- **D6 ATTACK** 

- 1 **GRIP!** The Kogler takes firm grip on an enemy. The victim suffers an attack with six Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (blunt force). If the attack hits, the victim becomes GRAPPLED and automatically suffers the critical wound Broken Arm (blunt force 44–45). Neither the Kogler nor the victim fall to the ground. However, any weapons held by the victim are dropped. The Kogler can attack the grappled victim, or other targets, with his other hand. 

- 2 **A FIST TO THE FACE!** The mechanical arm trembles when the Kogler clutches his fist as hard as he can. With an incredible force the arm shoots through the air, attacking an enemy. Perform an attack with eight Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (blunt force). The attack can be parried with a shield. 

him basically is everyone until proven otherwise. He is cunning and knows when to negotiate and when to dominate. He feels no loyalty to the dwarven community since it rejected him, but loves construction, machines and smart solutions with a passion. 

## **THE KOGLER** 

## **STRENGTH 6, AGILITY 4, WITS 4, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Melee 2, Crafting 5, Sleight of Hand 2, Lore 3, Insight 2, Manipulation 2 **TALENTS:** Firm grip 2, Smith 3 

## **ARMOR RATING:** 6 

**MECHANICAL EYE:** The Kogler can see at DISTANT range with incredible detail, see in darkness, and through smoke and fog. 

**MECHANICAL ARM:** The mechanical arm lets the Kogler perform monster attacks. See the adjacent table. 

- 3 **THROW SCRAP!** The Kogler grabs a hold of a large piece of scrap in the room and throws it at an enemy at up to NEAR distance. Perform an attack with seven Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1 (the object determines the type of damage). The victim must pass a roll for MIGHT or fall to the ground (no action). Now stuck under the object, the victim must spend a short action to break free. 

- 4 **CRUSH WEAPON!** The mechanical fist firmly grips an enemy’s weapon, attempting to break it. Perform an attack with five Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. The attack deals damage to the weapon instead of the enemy. The attack can be parried. 

- 5 **SHOOT KNIVES!** A handful of sharp blades shoot out from the arm against D3 targets at up to NEAR distance. Each target suffers an attack with five Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (stab wound). 

- 6 **KNOCKOUT!** With a surgical strike, the Kogler punches an enemy. Perform an attack with six Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (blunt force). If the attack deals damage, the victim falls unconscious and must make a STAMINA roll (no action) at the start of every turn to wake up. 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## 12. THE LIGHT METAL LAKE 

_Directly above the three spheres, the ceiling seems to be made of shimmering metal. Those taking a closer look can see that the surface is not solid, but rather makes waves as the air moves in the cave_ 

The metal ceiling is not forged, but made of accumulated light metal. It flows like mercury, but is lighter than air. Most of it comes from the Kogler’s airship whose envelope was damaged as he sailed it through the great gate of Tribolia, which caused the metal to leak out and rise to the ceiling. The adventurers can climb up Weldewola’s axle and fetch light metal in buckets. 

## 13. THE AIRSHIP 

_Below the largest stone sphere lies what looks like a small, slightly damaged ship. The rigging appears to have consisted of a large leather bag fastened with rope. The bag is ruptured and lies flaccid next to the hull. Inside the ship is a longitudinal crankshaft that ends in a propeller._ 

The Kogler’s airship landed gently and is in good condition, but cannot fly as all the light metal has leaked out of the envelope and accumulated in the cave’s ceiling. The Kogler cannot climb up and fetch the metal by himself and the mechas are too clumsy and stupid. If the leather envelope is repaired ( CRAFTING roll and a Quarter Day’s work), it can be filled until the ship takes off again. It is driven forward by hand- cranked propellers and steered with the rudder. However, the great gate must be opened before anyone can fly outside. 

## 14. BLOWDWELL 

_Next to the lava river is a strange little house seem­ ingly filled with what looks like a large bladder whose translucent membranes bulge out of the doors and windows. At the back of the house is something that resembles a large bellows with the nozzle shoved through a hole in the house. Moving closer, you see two pale little creatures pressing their noses against the membranes and waving to get your attention._ 

The creatures’ names are Bydmer Dribblecliff and Aramilia Crack, and they are dwelvers from the depths of the earth. The adventurers may already have encountered dwelvers in Stonegarden in _Raven’s Purge_ and learned that they are the underground ancestors of the dwarves. 

The dwelvers’ voices cannot be heard and they gesture to the people outside by rhythmically bringing their palms together – this is their way of telling the adventurers to increase the pressure in the house by pumping the bellows. If they do, they can hear the dwelvers’ voices and communicate through the membranes. 

If the adventurers try to cut their way inside, the dwelvers will be terrified and wave for them to stop, since the air would kill them (in a Quarter Day). The membranes in the bladder overlap in certain places so that food can be sent in through a primitive airlock. The only way to get the dwelvers out is to fetch their suits, which are found in the chapel (#15). See the box on the next page. 

## BYDMER DRIBBLECLIFF 

## AND ARAMALIA CRACK 

The dwelvers Bydmer and Aramalia resemble meter- high fetuses, half- formed and hairless, with extremely pale skin that shimmers with moisture and facial features that do not seem entirely constant, but float around over the bones of their faces.

**tribolia of the kogler**

## **THE DWELVERS’ STORY** 

Adventurers who manage to speak with the dwelvers will learn that they were trapped by the Kogler. They went up to Tribolia in peace to see what was going on when they suddenly heard the smithy’s safety door close. They wore protective suits because they are vulnerable to the gases in the smithy, but the suits were punctured by the Kogler’s mechas. 

The dwelvers almost died, but managed to inflate their emergency tent Blowdwell inside the cabin where they have been trapped ever since. The Kogler took their suits, without which they cannot leave Blowdwell. He gives them food, but experiments with what they can and cannot eat and questions them. He has probably repaired their suits and hidden them in case he ever needs to take them away. 

Adventurers who help the dwelvers will be offered something in return. For 

example, the dwelvers can help blow up the gate, since they are proficient with explosives – a technique they will not share with surface creatures. 

The dwelvers can also tell them more about the Metrochrone – that it is a narrative clock that brings forth paradigm shifts in the world. It must be restarted for the dwelvers to proceed with certain projects in the underground. For this reason they support the adventurers and advise them on how to repair the clock, but will not willingly go with them to the Watch of the Sisters. 

The dwelvers have no sense of honor towards surface creatures and will slip down through some crack in the mountain as soon as they think they have shown enough gratitude. Their suits are as elastic as their bodies, which allows them to squeeze through cracks no wider than a hand. 

## **BYDMER AND ARAMALIA** 

## **STRENGTH 2, AGILITY 5, WITS 4, EMPATHY 3** 

**SKILLS:** Endurance 3, Crafting 3, Stealth 3, Sleight of Hand 2, Move 3, Lore 2, Survival 3, Manipulation 2 

## 15. THE CHAPEL 

_On a rock ledge above the floor you see a small, run­ down marble chapel with a statue outside_ 

_depicting a human on horseback. The statue is missing its sword arm and is in pretty bad shape overall. The stairs that once led up to the chapel have been removed._ 

Climbing up to the chapel is fairly easy ( MOVE + 2), but one can also use a ladder found behind the ledge. This is a chapel of friendship built in the time of Sella the Liberator, and he is the rider depicted by the 

230 **chapter 17** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

statue. The statue was damaged during the war with the Lusitarians. 

On the rock wall inside the chapel is an exact copy of the Fresco of Sella, which the adventures may have already seen at the temple in Ashenstead and that depicts Sella the Liberator with his Astra (page 62). This is actually the original blueprint from which Horn’s Astra were created in Tribolia long ago. 

The horned dwarves created the artifacts as an experiment and to get the horse clans on their side after they were cursed by the goddesses Wail and Flow. They dared not wield the Astra themselves due to the severe side effect of accelerated aging, but thought it more appropriate to study the effect on the horse warriors who do not live very long anyway. 

**THE DWELVERS’ SUITS:** In a cabinet in the chapel hang two small suits made of strangely

## 16. THE WHISTLE HOUSE 

_A loud whistling sound shrieks through the entire cave. You trace the noise to a small building near ­ the rock wall. There are four metal pipes of dif ferent lengths sticking out of it._ 

The house acts as a four- note steam whistle. Each note is sounded individually every three hours, after which the cycle starts again. The process occurs automatically by means of water that flows down the rock wall, into the house and is vaporized by the lava below. As the pressure rises in its tank, the whistle blows and the mechanism switches pipes, and then the pressure starts building up again. The Kogler has divided the cave’s mechas into four teams, each responding to a separate note. The teams can be identified by the paint he has smeared on their breastplates: red, green, yellow, and white. When the whistle sounds, the working team goes to rest and wind each other up while another team leaves the whistle house and picks up where they left off.

AGNOSTICA<br>**----- End of picture text -----**<br>


**TYPE OF ADVENTURE SITE:** Settlement 

_You see a square, heavily weathered, forty­ meter­ high pyramid that looks like it has been there for thousands of years. Rocky ramps on all sides, which probably used to be stairs, lead up to the top where a rectangular, black monolith awaits. Several armies and key players have already arrived and set up camp around the place. People seem to be looking for something at the top of the ruin, digging among the stones and discussing with each other._ 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## BACKGROUND 

Agnostica is the moon elves’ old temple in Aslene. The name means “that which must not be known,” which is fitting since the moon elves have tried to stay hidden ever since they 

stole the Eighth Mother from the Seventh. But the temple’s name goes back further than that. 

Once it materializes, the temple seems suspended in mid- air 70 meters above ground, perpendicular to the ground with gardens and everything, hence “the Hanging Gardens of Agnostica,” which was considered one of great wonders of the age. There is no explanation as to how the temple can stay in the air like this – the whole thing is considered a perfect illusion conjured by long dead mentalist masters. 

In ancient times, Agnostica was actually a constant source of discussion about what distinguishes reality from perception, illusion, and interpretation. It was precisely for that purpose that the temple was built, which explains the name. 

Having fled to Ravenland and later to Aslene with Octomadria 1200 years ago, the moon elves settled down with their kin in Agnostica, 

232 

**chapter 18** 

but quickly realized that the worm people, the Seventh, would find them sooner or later. They could not get rid of Octomadria. 

That is why the moon elves made the temple disappear from the world for a period of time linked to the Metrochrone, the giant clock at the Watch of the Sisters. The clock stopped when the Kogler in Tribolia stole essential parts of it, and must be restarted for the temple to return to the world. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## GETTING HERE 

Agnostica does not materialize until four of the volcanoes known as the Sons of Horn (page 55) have awakened and the Metrochrone at the Watch of the Sisters has been restarted. Then the temple emerges in one of the hexes around the last dormant volcano in Aslene. Which volcano it is depends on the order in which Horn’s Astra are found and claimed, since each Astra is connected to a certain horse clan and volcano. 

At first the temple pyramid only appears as it will look in the distant future – as a crumbling ruin on the ground. This illusion is connected to a time altar behind a black monolith at the top of the ruin. For more information, see location #1. 

**==> picture [81 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## LEGEND 

_They say that the moon elves in the time before time built a temple with wondrous gardens that floated over Aslene. The temple could only be seen when the moon was full and never appeared in the same place twice, but was said to gleam like mother­ of­ pearl, so that all who saw it would sit spellbound in the night until it vanished. The memory alone is enough to bring tears to the driest of eyes, and yet nobody can describe what they have seen._ 

## LOCATIONS 

Described below are the most important places at Agnostica. 

## 1. THE PYRAMID 

_You stand before a crumbling stone pyramid where the remains of terraces and stairs are the only indications that it is a building and not just a pile of rubble. At the top of the pyramid is a black monolith._ 

The ruin is not yet real, but merely a projection of what it will look like in a thousand years or so. To summon the present- day pyramid the time altar inside the monolith must be activated. 

The monolith appears to be made of black stone and cannot be moved, since it is immaterial. The monolith is six meters high, four meters wide, and one meter thick. It opens if touched by one of Horn’s Astra with Level 3 (page 61) or all four of the moonstones found in Aslene (including those inside Vinhelm Kaal and Fiena Fromelei). 

The monolith opens to reveal a door that is three meters high and two meters wide. Behind it is a square, windowless, black room with a lone altar. If the Astra or the moonstones are placed on the altar, the monolith turns into a 70-meter- high pillar and the temple Agnostica emerges along with the Dimension Knot (#2). 

_The top of the tall pillar which a moment ago was a monolith holds what looks like a large knot of stone. Here and there are circular openings towards the center of the knot. What looks like a stone bridge protrudes from one side of the knot towards a temple building seemingly floating in the air, sev­ enty meters above ground and perpendicular to the ground. An equally vertical garden is hanging under the temple._ 

233 

**agnostica** 

## **THE KEY PLAYERS AT AGNOSTICA** 

Depending on how the game has developed, you decide which key players come to Agnostica and when they arrive. Some may have gotten there before the adventurers. The constellations consist of at least two opposing armies. 

The first key players to arrive at Agnostica only find the ruin of the temple pyramid. No one understands what to do or where they can find the Eighth Mother. The armies position themselves around the ruin, negotiate with each other, and decide to exchange ideas. You can use the key players present for violent confrontations, negotiations, and diplomacy. 

✥ **SELLIGAR HORNE** has no personal interest in the Eighth Mother and is only here because an ally wants to get hold of the creature or stop someone else from doing so. 

✥ **POANSA MOULTER** wants the Eighth Mother whom she believes to be Wyrm Reborn. Poansa searches the ruin’s exterior and examines the monolith. If the adventurers cannot figure out how to open the monolith (previous page), Poansa can do it instead. 

✥ **COLONEL GRUDENSTAAL** wants to kill the Eighth Mother whom he believes to be Wyrm Reborn. He too can figure out how to open the monolith. 

✥ **FIENA FROMELEI:** If Fiena has not yet gotten hold of a moonstone, she senses their presence in Agnostica and seeks them out. If Fiena already has a moonstone, she can now turn into a moon elf and get behind Vinhelm Kaal’s agenda. 

✥ **THE SEVENTH** know that the Eighth Mother is kept at Agnostica and have come to reclaim her. This is their only goal and their sole purpose in life. Once the floating temple appears, 

they will take over a bird’s skeleton and fly up there. 

- ✥ **VINHELM KAAL** and Fiena Fromelei will work together once she becomes a moon elf and can access his memories. They head for Agnostica to return all the moonstones to the temple, activate its time altar, and make it disappear forever along with the Eighth Mother. 

- ✥ **KONANA** and the Vasnians have no interest of their own in the Eighth Mother and are only here because an ally wants to get hold of the creature or stop someone else from doing so. 

- ✥ **VAEREFOR VAN REIBEN:** If Vaerefor possesses the Goblet Staff of Have, he thinks he will be able to turn the Eighth Mother into a red elf, in which case he would have a god on his side. Otherwise he could be here because an ally wants to get hold of the creature or stop someone else from doing so. When Agnostica has materialized, he can summon demonic vegetation beneath the temple which grows until the red elves can climb up. 

- ✥ **THE DREAMSTRESS** will likely come to Agnostica as an ally of Vaerefor or someone else, without any personal interest in the Eighth Mother. When she arrives, the Dreamstress sits down to dream of the creature in the temple and realizes that it is powerful enough to dream her back to Eoni. She will then make this her top priority and secretly seek new alliances. This could become a key element in thwarting the plans of Vaerefor or the Seventh, since the Eighth Mother under the Dreamstress’ guidance can dream them both away to Eoni and thus disappear from Aslene forever.

## 2. THE PILLAR OF WRATH 

_The pillar seems to be made of black stone. It is smooth and glossy and there is nothing to climb._ 

The pillar cannot be climbed by level- headed creatures. But an angry individual can grip the metal with hands and feet and easily climb 

up like a spider (no roll required). The pillar is designed as a safeguard against the Seventh, since they do not understand emotions and therefore cannot climb the pillar. 

Play this by asking the players what mood their characters are in. Until their answer is angry or irritated, they cannot get a grip on the pillar.

## 3. THE DIMENSION KNOT 

_You reach a platform from which a spiral staircase leads into the stone knot’s interior._ 

There is only one winding staircase from the platform through the knot and it leads out at Portal A, where the first bird cage is found (see below). The adventurers can then move on the upper side to one of the other portals and descend the stairs from there. Adventurers who move too far out must make a MOVE roll not to fall off the dimension knot, likely to their death. 

**ALTERED GRAVITY:** Each time the adventurers follow the stairs through the knot, from Portal B to C and from Portal D to E, the gravity changes, so that the portal they come out of feels like it is positioned horizontally. In other words, the adventurers will feel as though the staircase runs from the underside up to the ground. 

**THE BIRD CAGES:** There are two stylized bird cages at each of Portals A, C, and E; one on either side of the gateway. Inside each bird cage is a carved wooden bird as well as an egg – the side facing the cage floor is of black obsidian, the other is of silvery metal that has spread to the ceiling, since it is light metal which floats upwards. All eggs are engraved with the rune of Flow and can be picked up. 

Since Portals A, C, and E as well as their eggs each have their own “down,” the adventurers can pick up eggs that fall in six different directions. One egg of each direction is required to activate the orb in the chapel (#10). 

THE TEMPLE AGNOSTICA 

If the adventurers have done it right as they reach Portal E, the Bridge of Joy (location #6) and the temple with its gardens will be positioned horizontally. The ground below the temple will then seem like a horizontal wall. 

If the visitors take any other route to the temple, gravity will be normal and the floor of the temple will be vertical. This means that moving inside the temple requires climbing ( MOVE rolls where failure results in falling).

## 4. THE BRIDGE OF JOY 

_The stone bridge appears to be floating in mid­ air. The temple and its gardens await on the other side._ 

Whether the player characters perceive the temple as vertical or horizontal depends on which route they took through the Dimension Knot (#3). 

The Bridge of Joy is another safeguard against the Seventh who do not understand emotions, and can only be crossed by people in a joyful mood. Those who do not feel joy, or lose their joy midway, will fall straight through the bridge as though it were made of sand. Play this by asking the players what mood their characters are in. Unless their answer is joyful or happy, the bridge will not hold. 

If anyone falls from the bridge, the “downward” direction returns to normal and the unfortunate person falls to the ground 80 meters further down. The person can be pulled back up if they were secured with a rope. 

and immediately grow back after being picked from their branches. The fruits have different flavors and some even taste of deliciously cooked fish or meat. 

Fruits picked from a vertical garden feel strange in one’s mouth as the bite wants to fall sideways rather than down to the stomach, and the victim gets a stomach ache after one turn, since the intestines have trouble digesting the strange food. Treat it as an attack that deals damage to Strength, with seven Base Dice and Weapon Damage 1. 

Unlocked doors lead to the Hall of Serenity (#6), whose far end holds a bell tower. 

## 6. THE HALL OF SERENITY 

_The doors open into a large hall with long tables. The far end of the hall is elevated with what looks like a high seat and table of honor. From the ceiling hangs chandeliers and ten statues of warriors are standing along the walls._ 

## 5. THE HANGING GARDENS OF AGNOSTICA 

_After crossing the stone bridge you reach a beautiful floating garden full of fruit trees and flowers. In the garden is an L­ shaped temple pavilion with a bell tower. There are no living creatures in sight except for some birds and bees._ 

At the end of the Bridge of Joy, the adventurers reach an exquisite garden that is either horizontal or vertical depending on how they got there. Either way the garden behaves as if standing on the ground, with singing fountains, birds, and bees flying around the trees. In the garden stands the temple Agnostica. 

The garden is lush with flowers and fruits that can be eaten and gathered as provisions 

This hall is where the moon elves gathered for meals and assembly, but it is now empty. As soon as anyone walks one third of the way into the room, some of the statues (as many as the adventurers) come to life and start attacking all intruders. But if the adventurers stand still and do not defend themselves, the statues’ swords stop just before hitting their target, after which they return to their posts. 

Adventurers who defend themselves must instead fight them to the death. The hall can be bypassed by climbing up the bell tower and taking the stairs down to the dais. This can be particularly convenient if the temple is vertical. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** Stone warriors, as many as the adventurers.

## 9. THE DORMITORY 

## **STONE WARRIOR** 

_You enter a fifteen­ meter corridor with six doors on either side. The far end is a stone wall. Above each door is a name. High up the far stone wall is an inscription that reads: “She is in our names.”_ 

**STRENGTH 10, AGILITY 2** 

**SKILLS:** Scouting 1 

**ARMOR RATING:** 6 

**ATTACKS:** Slash, seven Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (slash wound). Stab, seven Base Dice and Weapon Damage 2 (stab wound). Parry, five Base Dice. 

Starting from the entrance, the twelve names are: 

|**LEFT SIDE**|**RIGHT SIDE**|
|---|---|
|Iacolne|Farber|
|Druer|Mazurenda|
|Sisela<br>Zentuner|Salunia<br>Snetom|

**STONE WEAPONS:** The stone warriors cannot be disarmed, but their weapons can be damaged. If the statues’ Strength is lower than the number of Base Dice for attack or parry, their current Strength rating is used instead. 

## 7. THE VISIONARIUM 

_Beyond the dais you reach a small passageway with windows on both sides and a door in the far wall._ 

Behind the unlocked door to each alcove is the moon elf whose name is carved above the door. They are all decomposed corpses, including Vinhelm and Salunia (Fiena). 

The windows of the Visionarium show moving scenes from other worlds, but they are only projections. Behind the glass is nothing but a stone wall. 

**THE SOLUTION TO THE RIDDLE:** By opening the following five doors in the correct order, the name Octomadria forms from the first two letters of each name: Ocroende – Totelema – Mazurenda – Druer – Iacolne. When this happens, another door emerges in the stone wall at the end of the corridor which leads to Octomadria’s chamber. 

## 8. THE CHAPEL 

_An empty, windowless chapel. There are rows of benches on either side of the aisle. In front it hovers what seems to be a meter­ wide marble orb. At the back of the room is a closed door of stone._ 

The door cannot be opened with physical force, but only by activating the orb. It has six small, round depressions, one on each side. Adventurers who examine the orb more closely will find the symbol of Flow at the bottom of each depression. If the six bird eggs from the Dimension Knot (#3) are placed in these depressions so that their direction of fall is toward the orb’s center, the orb is activated and the door to the Dormitory opens. 

- ✥ **CREATURES:** The moment the door to Octomadria’s chamber appears, the dead moon elves are restored to the prime of their lives and confront the intruders. This does not apply to Vinhelm and Salunia (Fiena), who are instead replaced by their present- day versions.

if the weapons they wield are Horn’s Astra. ## THE SHOWDOWN 

Agnostica is the intended location for the final showdown of the campaign _Legacy of Horn_ . Below is a summary of what certain key players might do during the finale. How it all plays out will of course be largely determined by the adventurers’ actions. 

## THE MOON ELVES 

Together with the moon elves, Vinhelm Kaal and Fiena Fromelei (Salunia) do their best to keep Octomadria in Agnostica until the temple and the snake woman fade from existence once more. If there is a risk of her falling into the hands of the Seventh, they immediately try to kill her. 

## THE SEVENTH 

If the Seventh encounter Octomadria’s earlier forms, they immediately try to kill them to summon her final form. Faced with her final form, they throw themselves at the creature to assimilate it and turn Octomadria into the goddess Meat with power over all living things. This appeals to Octomadria, but whether she actually agrees to mother the eighth generation of worm people depends on whether someone else can make her a better offer. If Octomadria refuses their request, the Seventh will simply lie down to die as their lives no longer have any meaning. 

## VAEREFOR VAN REIBEN 

Vaerefor van Reiben can offer to make Octomadria godmother of Reiben with the aim of conquering Ravenland, Aslene, and Alderland. If the Dreamstress is allied with Vaerefor and hears of his plans, she will try to sabotage his alliance with the Eighth Mother. Alternatively, the Dreamstress may already have realized that Octomadria has enough power to dream them both to her home world Eoni and offers to make the Eighth Mother goddess of all Eoni. 

## AGNOSTICA FADES AWAY 

## POANSA AND GRUDENSTAAL 

Poansa and Grudenstaal may try to kill Octomadria at first sight, which is only possible 

D6 days after Octomadria’s chamber has been opened, or whenever you see fit, the temple Agnostica will fade from the world forever, leaving just the ruin of the terraced pyramid behind. 

240 **chapter 18** 

## AFTERMATH 

his section describes what might happen in the wake of _Legacy of Horn_ . It is meant to offer guidance and Ttips that you can use based on the choices and alliances the players have made over the course of the game. 

**==> picture [80 x 15] intentionally omitted <==**

## THE SITUATION AFTER THE FINALE 

The campaign _Legacy of Horn_ can of course end in any number of ways depending on the players’ actions. A temporary and fragile peace settles over Aslene as the winners consolidate - their power and establish contacts in Raven land. Here are some suggestions on what the situation could look like. 

- ✥ A key player or alliance, with or without the adventurers, dominates Aslene. Their rival key players are neutralized, dead, subjugated, or gone from the world. 

- ✥ The moon elves have either disappeared along with Agnostica and Octomadria or chosen to stay because the threat of the Seventh no longer exists. 

- ✥ Octomadria has either joined the dominant faction or vanished from the world along with the Dreamstress or the moon elves. If she remains in the world, Octomadria can pose as Wyrm Reborn or as the Eighth Mother. 

- ✥ At least four of Horn’s Astra have been found and claimed, but it turns out that the artifacts can only be used in emergencies, since each use comes at the cost of accelerated aging. 

- ✥ The dominant faction in Aslene plans to attack Alderland and seeks military allies in Ravenland. The winning faction from _Raven’s Purge_ would be an appropriate partner. The attack on Alderland becomes the start of a future expansion set in Alderland. 

241 

**aftermath** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## POST- FINALE EVENTS 

Here are some events that can occur after the campaign finale. 

## GANDER’S PASS IS OPENED 

After the conclusion of _Legacy of Horn_ , Gander’s Pass will be opened to Alderland as the beginning of a future expansion set in the country to the southeast. Exactly when this occurs does not matter, so in the meantime you can easily keep playing in Aslene or elsewhere. 

But before the opening of Gander’s Pass a player character, or perhaps all of them, has a dream where a woman leads them by the hand to a door into a mountain. The path to the door is lined by huge pillars of stone, and a yellow- eyed vulture caws from its perch on an altar. The goddess Flow plucks the moon from the sky and places it on the altar, and then the door opens. Beyond it is a lush land with lots of people. 

In the dream, Flow says: “ _This land I give to you, if you can take it!_ ” 

## THE CAULDRON OF FLOW 

The Cauldron of Flow is a circular, kilometerwide lake which can be placed in any hex on the map. The lake has no function until it awakens, which it does as soon as the Metrochrone at the Watch of the Sisters is reactivated (page 219). 

From that point on, anything in the cauldron ages to destruction in a matter of moments. Living creatures die, decay, turn to skeletons, and finally to dust that blows away. Stones crumble into dust and blow away. Iron rusts, becomes dust, and blows away. 

When the cauldron awakens, its water is vaporized in a minute, which will cause extreme rainfall in the area during the week that follows. All that remains is an empty, hemispherical crater with a radius and depth of 500 meters. 

On the ground in its center is what from a distance looks like a building shaped like the symbol of Flow. Anything that comes near it is annihilated. An observant individual will recognize that the cauldron is a giant copy of the depressions in the Altar of Flow. 

## THE BOOK OF WINDS BECOMES KNOWN 

The Book of Winds can be found at the Temple of Winds (page 217), but its older parts cannot be understood without the Book of Water. The Books of Wind and Water will reappear in a future expansion, but for now it does not really matter who gets their hand on the Book of Winds, since it is currently as indestructible as it is unusable. 

If the book’s existence becomes publicly known, it will strengthen the Vasnians’ cause and claim to Aslene. The horned dwarves of Firestead in particular believe that a substantial part of the country should be returned to the Vasnians. 

242 **chapter 19** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## SELLIGAR HORNE OF HAVENMARK 

_It is said that the Houns’ chieftain, Selligar Horne of Havenmark, emerged from his mother’s womb a capable rider. He mounted the nearest horse, and with the umbilical cord fluttering in the wind, he immediately rode off to inspect his domains. Only afterwards did he return to her breast. The father was never again allowed near his wife, for the boy was jealous and believed that further offspring could only be inferior to himself. He was strong from birth and slew his first challengers in combat before he had grown his fourth tooth. Now he rides across the plains as a proud youth, like his ancestor Sella the Liberator before him, intent on gathering the clans so that all foreign malice can be driven from the Bloodmarch. The seers say that Selligar is destined to rule Ravenland and Alderland as well. He means to open the Galder Gorge to Alderland, and lifted one of the giant pillars with his bare arms just to prove himself worthy of such a task. But the time for doing so has not yet come._ 

## COLONEL GRUDENSTAAL 

_Oh yes, I’ve met Colonel Grudenstaal, the man they call the fist of the Ironbrows. A ruthless man to be sure, like when Holy Frensia was promised by the king – the old man feared the reprisal of the gods, you see – that she would never be exiled to Ravenland, and Grudenstaal kept the monarch’s word by personally kicking the seer over the edge of the precipice. But the man is more than just a hard heart. He listens to everybody and does not hesitate to change his mind if he likes your advice. He is certainly proud, so much so that he always goes his own way, and nobody knows what he might do. I suppose that’s why the powers that be in Alderland had him sent off to the Bloodmarch._ 

243 

**legends** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## POANSA MOULTER 

_You might think that Poansa is called “moulter” because of her flaking skin. Not so. It’s because she leaves empty shells of people in her wake. The shells are not her own, but the remains of those she has devoured along the way. One of them was Grenemer Treba, count of Strula until that venomous woman seduced him and found his secret Rust Altar. Once imprisoned he demanded, as a nobleman, to be executed by iron – for religious reasons that would become clear later – and appealed to Ad Vipera on the matter. He was surprised to find Poansa in one of the council seats. But the Moulter granted his wish and had him executed by iron as requested: they pounded iron nails through his body until the screams died down and the blood stopped flowing._ 

## FIENA FROMELEI 

_They say that one of the Penites’ powerful moon priestesses from Belifar has been sent to the Bloodmarch to finally eradicate the sin that the cult attributes to halflings and the goddess Eor. The priestess will seek forgiveness for some wrong committed by her kin at the time of the Shift, and will do so at the secret temple of the moon elves, although no one has ever seen it. Come to think of it, no one has even seen a moon elf, have they? So how she will accomplish this is anyone’s guess. Personally, I’ve barely even seen a halfling. Perhaps it’s all made up? Might as well forget the whole thing!_ 

244 **legends** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## KONANA 

_Konana is the salt of the mountains. The blood of the country flows through her veins with the power of the ancients. She is the coming of spring after a bitter winter, the ray of sunlight that cuts like a sharp blade through the fleeing dark­ ness of the storm. In her I put my trust, for she will lead the Vasnians back to our stolen plains, break the legs of the horses, and cave in the skulls of their riders._ 

## VAEREFOR VAN REIBEN 

_I once saw the lord of the red elves with my own eyes. He stood on top of a giant slug that crawled its way through the pass where cousin Welia and I were pick­ ing bird’s eggs on the mountainside. Vaerefor van Reiben they call him, and that face of his might as well be made of bark. They say he lives among the growths of the crimson forests – the ones that have gotten bigger lately, as you’ve probably heard. I believe he has something to do with it!_ 

## THE DREAMSTRESS 

_Your dog ran away, eh? Well then, you should see the Dreamstress in Oxengelder who knows where everything is and where everyone’s gone. There, in the lime­ stone region just below the mountains, she runs a school for dreamers. They say the Dreamstress is a giant, bigger than a house but milder than cream, teaching the so­ inclined how to galder dream, so that the dreams inform them about the world and can even alter things that displease them. But me, I prefer a good night’s sleep and to leave the world as it is._ 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## THE SEVENTH 

_I’ve been told that a handful of stern­ looking priests recently arrived in Raven­ land from beyond the seas, having made the same journey as the humans who first landed on our shores. The priests are on a mission from the snake god to make sure that the humans honor the god properly. Apparently no one can endure the sight of their faces, which is why they’re all wearing masks._ 

_The priests are said to be carrying strong shackles on their belts, with orders to once more enslave the humans if they have not proven worthy of the freedom they received as a test from the god. They leave the skeletons of the tried and rejected in their wake, but cannot die themselves. When these terrible judges heard of Fangler’s prophecy, that Wyrm had been reborn in Aslene, they headed west to worship the god in his new form and pronounce their final judgment on humanity. If you cross their path with an impure mind, beware._ 

## OCTOMADRIA – THE EIGHTH MOTHER 

_Did you hear the rumor that Wyrm will be reborn in Aslene? The Prophet Fangler in Alderland, that fellow with the all­ seeing third eye, supposedly had a clear­ cut vision from the gods. Mark my words, this will only bring calam­ ity upon us. They say the Congregation of the Serpent has already sent troops to Aslene to receive their god and that Bretien the Procreator is mobilizing a defense in Kantamar. Calamity, I say, and calamity there will be! It’s best when things return to normal and all that’s turned upside down is the soil under the farmer’s plow._ 

246 **legends** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## TAREGYLL 

_It is said that Taregyll beyond Shadowgate Pass is the last stop of the dead, where demon­ tainted wretches come from all over Aslene to find peace in the lava. Some even say that the burning fissures are pathways between life and death. Visitors who have returned across the pass claim that this is all nonsense meant to keep foreigners away from the riches that lie abandoned and ripe for the picking in the devastated western regions of Aslene._ 

_The red forests in those parts are scary alright, but mostly for demons and other unnatural filth, which makes it a good place for adventurers to prepare for expeditions into the unknown._ 

## ASHENSTEAD 

_When Horn wished to forge the holy Astra as weapons for Sella the Liberator to use against the Vasnian hordes, the volcano god raised Mount Faltenvil like an anvil from the plains and summoned his blacksmith Ungule from deep beneath the earth. On the face of Faltenvil the Astra were forged, but so great was the heat of the smoldering blacksmith that none other than Horn himself could assist him, and so bright was the forge’s light that no one could even look at the mountain. But once Ungule returned underground and the mountain cooled, the horse clans built the temple of Ashenstead on its summit so the priests could watch over the country and safeguard its unity. So arduous was Ungule’s work that his hands were blistered and bloodied, and his blue blood remained on the plains. Horn’s wife, Have, gathered the blood in a bucket and used it to water her garden so that it would not go to waste, and from it grew the blue pines of the Bloodmarch, whose sap becomes holy blue tar._ 

247 

**legends** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## SALTERSTAY 

_It is said that the nameless sky goddess is too proud to take any notice of the ground below. And yet she wants to see it beautiful to emphasize her own beauty. To put some color on the canvass of the world she created the rainbow Tincturus, whose colors are released with the rain to paint the flowers, trees, and rocks in splendid hues. Tincturus thrives in the sky, so the sky goddess had to lure the great painter down by hiding treasures in the earth which the rain­ bow is searching for like a truffle pig. One who acts quickly might get there first to claim the treasure, but would be competing with drakewyrms and must beware of their ravenous greed._ 

## OXENGELDER 

_Have you heard of Oxengelder – the paradise of dreams? It’s said that all the country’s peace and beauty took refuge in Oxengelder as the Demon Flood raged into Aslene. There the gentle giantess welcomes anyone of pure heart and open mind to study oneiromancy, the art of dreaming up a wonderful life! Would you rather dance than fight? Rather love than hate? Would you give life a second chance? Then let the dream come true! If you go to Oxengelder, be sure to wear flowers in your hair, for it pleases the Dreamstress._ 

248 **legends** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## BANN GUELDER 

_It’s shameful how the Sons of Nesos have softened and settled down like common peasants around the ancestral tombs in Bann Guelder. Their buried forefa­ thers must be writhing with shame at their delicate offspring. And Nesos himself has returned from the grave, they say, probably to drive the young folk from the village and into battle as befits the sons of Horn. They’ve grown fat, red­ buttocked from a day’s ride but with plenty of coin, wherever those came from – not from honest plunder, that’s for sure. My brother­ in­ law sold ‘em a couple of hogs and was paid in pure gold, would you believe it?_ 

## TRIBOLIA OF THE KOGLER 

_In the time of the clans, the grand smithy of Tribolia was the greatest mechan­ ical wonder in Firestead. It was possibly rivaled by the fire fountains of Mor­ mender at one point, but those days came to an end with the clan wars. Now Tribolia is said to be abandoned and tainted by demons, so no dwarf with any sense will go there. Since the clan wars, the dwarves have appointed a guard­ ian of the smithy – the so­ called Kogler. The current Kogler was a bit odd even before he was appointed, but the title really went to his head. He became obsessed with “claiming his domains” and sailed his airship into the smithy against the council’s orders. The gate slammed shut behind him and would not budge. Since no one wanted to come for him, the Kogler had to stay in his smithy and has probably died by now._ 

249 

**legends** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## WATCH OF THE SISTERS 

_It is said that Aslene was once a barren desert, until the sister goddesses Wail and Flow sent their servant Have to make the land lush and green through the goddesses’ warm winds and flowing freshwater._ 

_The magma god Horn stayed below ground at the time, but as roots started to pierce down into his underground domain, he grew curious and burst through the surface as a volcano. Horn was amazed at how the Bloodmarch had flourished. He delighted in the birdsong and the dance of the butterflies, and was struck with love for Have. But when he learned that the Vasnians had usurped the land without appreciating the goddesses’ creations, Horn was furious and brought forth the horse clans from his fire to banish the ingrates. He took Have as his wife and gave her fire to rule as her wedding gift. Soon their five volcanic sons sprung up from the ground, and Horn gave each of them a horse clan as their personal guard._ 

_But the goddesses Wail and Flow felt that Horn had seduced their servant and taken both her and the land out of their hands. In anger they raised an impenetrable forest around their temple. From then on, they wanted nothing to do with Aslene anymore._ 

## AGNOSTICA 

_They say that the moon elves in the time before time built a temple with won­ drous gardens that floated over Aslene. The temple could only be seen when the moon was full and never appeared in the same place twice, but was said to gleam like mother­ of­ pearl, so that all who saw it would sit spellbound in the night until it vanished. The memory alone is enough to bring tears to the driest of eyes, and yet nobody can describe what they have seen._ 

250 

**legends** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## ALDERLAND 

_It is rumored that civil war threatens Alderland beyond the Iron Lock, and that there are now two kings in the land._ 

_The Algarian dynasty, having been weakened ever since the death of king Algarod during the fourth Algarian wars, has returned to power, supported by the Sigalian guild of magicians and the Wyrm Church under the psycho­ pomp Vivírula. She has succeeded Vipera and assumed his role as head of Ad Vipera, the church’s fanatical secret police._ 

_King Alginor the Mild, a descendant of Algarod, is seen as a puppet of his allies. The Algarians rule the capital Brodelmarg and the surrounding southern region. It is rumored that the Sigalian guild intends to protect the capital by performing large scale human sacrifices on prisoners in order to raise a magical barrier._ 

_Against the Algarians stands the Rust Church, in support of the Kantamar dynasty. Bretien the Born has been crowned king. The Kantamarians and the Rust Church dominate the sea. They control the north part of Alderland from Raskalma in the mining district._ 

## DAVURA 

_The expanse of wetland south of Alderland is called Davura. When the Alderlanders received their land from the Raven God, the indigenous pop­ ulation known as the Boringers were expelled to Davura, where they built settlements in the swamps and made contact with the natives, reptilian kin of which little is known. Whether the Boringers are completely human is also unclear. Merchants who have traveled the area say that the Boringers dream of reconquering Alderland, led by the crocodile god A­Xanna. This god is said to be one of a handful of named colossi in the region – primeval creatures who inhabited the wetlands long before humans and Saurians showed up. Sodetar the Hermit claims that the primeval creatures regularly gather at large stone pillars in ruins rising from the swamps, waiting for the “Exudator” to be reborn._ 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## THE DRAWN LANDS 

_The Drawn Lands west of Aslene are said to get their name from the days of King Hilemod the Wise. As the king grew old, his four sons started a fierce war in competition for the juicy parts of his legacy. After years of battle, the ducal sons finally asked their father to settle the dispute, but did not respect his decision and declared war on each other once more. At that point Hilemod had his sons imprisoned, drawn and quartered. He buried one part of each son in each quarter of the kingdom to satisfy their greed, and then left the king­ dom to his only daughter Momesia, who turned out to be a just and competent ruler. It is said that the Drawn Lands have been ruled by a queen ever since._ 

_Long ago the Aslene horse clans roamed the Drawn Lands, pillaging the wealthy merchant cities and raiding their caravans, but for the last three hundred years the land has been impenetrable from the east, for the route was closed by magical means when the demons invaded Aslene in the year 875 after the Shift. Since that day, anyone traveling west towards the Drawn Lands across the Wailing has been met by ever­stronger winds that soon make it impossible to continue._ 

_In ancient times the land was called Hâ. The art of raising the dead is said to have originated there. Stories tell of great ruined cities and entire kingdoms of walking dead who still rule the Drawn Lands’ vast desert plains with the old cities Modrawynd and Hâvila, where the dead watch over treasures and secrets they will never willingly share with the living._ 

252 **legends** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## THE SPARSE 

_The vast archipelago east of Ravenland is called the Sparse, especially by people on the continent. The various inhabitants of the Sparse have always defended their independence from others, which means that little is known for certain about the sea dwellers. The bigger islands of Vorenda and Hamaland with their fringe of skerries traditionally vie for dominance of the outer seas with­ out really being interested in expanding their domains. Beyond them lie the independent principalities of the Black Islands, the Hammer Islands (called the Aes in the local language), the Clan Pearls, the Grumblers, and the Blood Skerries. Less accessible are Neetma of the Dark Sea as well as the Yonder Shells and the Tooth Reefs, beyond which the sea is said to drop into the abyss. In addition, rumors speak of living and drifting islands woven of seaweed by the semi­intelligent crabler kins._ 

_The inhabitants of the Sparse include humans as well as gray elves and various water­breathing scale kins. There are also numerous beasts, the larg­ est of which are said to swallow entire ships whole, only to spit them out years later with an undead crew. Equally feared are the Hammer Island pirates who are always hunting for slaves for their tin and copper mines. Even while avoiding these dangers, unwary sailors sometimes get caught in the living streams controlled by the Black Islands’ necromancers, never to be seen again._ 

_Most inhabitants of the Sparse worship the goddess Meera, said to rule over winds and weather at sea. She is rumored to dwell in a floating crystal palace somewhere in the Vile Stretches, where the water’s surface is said not to be flat, but twisted into hills and ravines among which only the most expe­ rienced sailors dare venture. Legend has it that Meera occasionally visits the underwater cities of certain scale kin tribes in person._ 

253 

**legends** 

**==> picture [427 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## IRONSTEAD 

_Dwarves speak reluctantly of Ironstead, an underground dwarf realm which can supposedly be reached from Belderand in Ravenland, as well as from the Bitter Reach and Aslene for those who know where to find the entrances, which are said to move around. Ironstead is believed to be located in the inter­ mediate rock layers, somewhere between the surface and the deep structures of the Dwelvers, but flying creatures have told of open, circular vales among ice­ laden mountains, vales whose domes immediately closed upon discovery. The dwarves of Ironstead are said to worship the goddess Tekne, wife of Huge and the mother of smithing, crafts, and invention. According to legend, the main temple of Tekne is located in faraway Polestead, where her priests grease the rotation axle of the world itself._ 

_Whiteglint, the southwestern region of Ironstead, is famous for its wrought crystal. Rumor has it that the dwarven masters of Whiteglint have sought to create artificial life by imitating elven nature, where flesh and minerals grow into life around a crystal. Rebellious creatures from failed experiments have laid waste to an entire region in Ironstead, known as Skrena, where rampant rocks have since grown like cancer in the mountain. Some believe that the dwarves of Whiteglint are planning to send armies of artificial warriors to drive all humans from Ravenland and its neighboring countries._ 

254 **legends** 

**==> picture [426 x 82] intentionally omitted <==**

## TREUWEN 

_Farther south, the swamps of Davura give way to the jungles of Treuwen, which become increasingly difficult to pass through where steep mountains rise high, cleft by deep ravines whose walls are home to hanging elven cities. The boundary is said to be fluid and imperceptible. Rumor has it that the plants in Treuwen’s forests are intelligent and their lives so intertwined that the iron oaks of Mahemma shake with fury whenever a flower is picked from Loroa’s meadows. Few humans have made more than a short stop in Treuwen. It is said that all who linger too long in the jungle will carry it with them forever._ 

_A few druids claim that they can move through the humid jungles in har­ mony with the living land. The same druids advise others to negotiate and sacrifice before harvesting the jungle’s potent healing herbs, unless they want to incur the collective wrath of nature._ 

## ANOMA 

_No one really knows what lies beyond the great seas east of Ravenland, but leg­ ends tell of the continent Anoma from whence the humans sailed west twelve hundred years ago, guided by the Raven god. None of the country’s written or pictorial documents remain, barely even any legends, but myth has it that the very bedrock of the land was devoured by a stone worm which forced the humans to flee._ 

_A couple of Rust Church inquisitors claim to have captured and interro­ gated a mysterious elf­like creature a few years ago. It babbled about an abyssal menace beyond the seas and secrets that must never come to light. The prisoner begged to be killed with his memories, and the interrogators granted his wish, albeit accidentally by overestimating the creature’s vitality. Those responsible were punished with dismemberment._ 

255 

**legends**
